# Decisions pending — the forward look

Two altitudes of "surface it before it's forced," each **consulted at a fixed trigger** so a deferral can't evaporate into [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) prose and be forgotten:

> **A trigger that is a claim about the code should be a test, not a note (M42, 2026-07-14).** Every entry below names a **trigger** — the condition that resurfaces it. Where that condition is a *fact about the code* (*"if packs are ever loaded from the filesystem"*, *"when a doctype bumps past v1"*, *"when a placement doctype carries a code-anchor"*), **prose is the wrong medium**: it fired once already and nobody noticed — the freeze-assert hole was deferred on exactly that trigger, **multi-pack shipped at M14 and made it true**, and the hole was found two milestones and an RC trial later. **A condition you can assert should be asserted**: write the test that holds *today* and **reddens on the commit that makes the trigger true**. Then the trigger **fires itself**, in the gate of the person who triggered it, instead of waiting for someone to re-read this file. *(Pack-shape: an optional `trigger-test: code-anchor` field on the `deferral-ledger` entry, `check: symbol-exists` — [methodology-docs.md](../design/methodology-docs.md) → the four doctypes. Triggers that are genuinely judgment — "when an adopter corpus surfaces a measured floor" — keep the prose field; that boundary is the A-3 bound, not a gap.)*

- **Before planning a milestone** — consulted at [milestone-planning](milestone-planning-workflow.md) → **Scope**. The **discuss-later log**: milestone-keyed *discussion topics / design forks* that earlier work deferred "to settle before milestone N." When we park a topic with a trigger milestone, it lands here, so the next planning run *starts* by pulling its due topics into the done-picture and gap list — instead of relying on memory that "we said we'd discuss this before then." The backlog to triage these from is [VISION.md](../VISION.md) → Open questions (the categorized index); this log adds the missing piece — each due topic's **trigger milestone**.
- **Before building an increment** — consulted at [milestone-planning](milestone-planning-workflow.md) → **Detect gaps**. Increment-keyed decisions a dev-workflow run will **force**, tagged **(D)** a genuine open *design* question or **(I)** an *implementation* pick (crate/algorithm that falls out at pickup) — surfaced so nothing ambushes a build mid-task.

This is a *decision/discussion* backlog, not a *task* backlog — tasks are still cut per-increment at pickup ([roadmap.md](roadmap.md)). Entries **graduate to [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) when settled; delete them here once logged.**

**How entries arrive — the write side (everyone, not just the planner).** Whoever *defers* a topic to a future milestone — in a [design](design-workflow.md) discussion, a [dev](dev-workflow.md) run, a planning [Settle](milestone-planning-workflow.md#the-loop-one-milestone), or a [completion](milestone-completion-workflow.md) triage — **records it here in the same motion as the deferral**, keyed to its trigger. The split is the point: [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) logs what was *decided*; this file logs what is *owed and when it comes due*. A deferral that lives only in a DECISIONS sentence has no trigger and will be forgotten — which is the exact failure this log exists to prevent.

> **An Nth-field-demand trigger is a counter, not a vibe (the project-alpha-3.0 drain, 2026-07-22).** The test-not-a-note rule above covers triggers that are *facts about the code*. Triggers that are *judgments fed by field evidence* ("when an adopter corpus surfaces a measured floor") have their own rot mode: **milestone-keyed deferrals get actioned; condition-keyed ones accumulate "trigger-adjacent evidence" notes whose count restarts in every reader's head.** Four deferrals (checkpoint/gate-record · gate-command evidence · acknowledged-findings ledger · managed-doc read-side) reached **3–4 independent field demands across four trials** while remaining formally untriggered — each trial's drain dutifully wrote the adjacent-evidence note, and nobody counted. **The rule: a condition-keyed deferral whose trigger is field demand carries a dated demand line appended in the same motion as each new datum, and the third independent demand forces it onto the next wave's Settle agenda** — where it is re-Settled against its recorded rationale, which may still win. The forcing is of the *decision*, never the build: "we counted three demands and still say no, because…" is a legitimate, recorded outcome; silent accumulation is not.

> **A charter fork that engages a record quotes the record's scope sentence verbatim — never a paraphrase (M43, 2026-07-17).** Charters are written at triage speed, and a paraphrase is where the drift enters: the M42 charter over-scoped the M30/B1 "surfaced, not prevented" record, M42's Settle corrected it — and the **M43 charter re-minted the identical over-scoping** (fork 7), plus mis-imported the A-3 bound into pack-metadata territory its recorded rationale never governed (forks 3/6). Three mis-citations across two consecutive charters, each caught only because a gap-detector went and read the record. So: when a fork here says *"engages record X"*, paste X's own scope-defining sentence next to the claim. The planning gap pass still verifies — but the charter should not need the correction.

## Before planning — milestone-keyed deferrals

Removed from pending.)*

### The rc.17 fix pass (M53) — CHARTERED 2026-09-21 · PLANNED 2026-09-21 · BUILT 2026-09-22 · ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-09-22** as `1.0.0-rc.17` · a fix pass, not a wave

**[Planned 2026-09-21 — and three of the four fix shapes below were corrected at the Settle, each on a driven datum; the charter's text is kept as chartered and the corrections live in one home:** [settle-record](../completions/artifacts/M53/settle-record.md) D1–D5 + §1–§14, decomposed at [roadmap](roadmap.md) → Milestone 53. `(3, A3-1)`: the shape stands, and `merged/` is walked too (the human's call). `(3, A3-2)`: *"refuses with the door's existing `foreign-bytes` identity"* — neither door has one, and the removal runs after the commit landed; settled as a conditioned removal + one advisory + **the residual rule** (D3, the human's decision). `(2, DEFECT A)`: the predicate gains two rebase conjuncts and the route is `git reset`, not `rm .git/MERGE_MSG` (false in a linked worktree). `(5, DEFECT 1)`: `write.unslugable-title`, not `work-unit.malformed-id` (inert on the derived id), and a third door, `add-from-spec`. **Five increments plus a close.** **BUILT 2026-09-22**, and the marker is deliberately not the ✅ SHIPPED its neighbours carry: the five increments and the close have landed and **none of the completion workflow's acts has** — no completion audit, no persisted verdict, no version bump + install, and no partial per-axis re-review of axes 2 · 3 · 5 — so the heading claims the build and nothing beyond it, and it takes no version numeral, because the 1.0.0 call this pass exists to make reachable is the human's. The pass's own forward record lives in the two `### Deferred …` sections below (the six rows it does not fix, and the three the build named), and its fold-back in [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) → project state.**]**

*(Chartered by the human on [the M52 per-axis review re-run](../completions/artifacts/M52/per-axis-review/README.md) — M51's instrument re-run on the installed `1.0.0-rc.16` from `a3eb026b`: **35 of M51's 39 rows CLOSED, 4 partially still open, no regression**, and **27 new findings** (4 tier-1 · 8 tier-2 · 15 tier-3), coverage 47/47. **The 1.0.0 call is NOT taken on `1.0.0-rc.16`** — tier 1 is the class that blocks it. **And the shape changes.** Every one of the four tier-1 rows sits inside a mechanism M52 itself minted (the `Displace` disposition · the nine-state posture family · the fixed-identity mint), exactly as M51's and M50's tier-1 rows sat inside theirs: a full wave mints new mechanism, and each new mechanism ships with one un-swept sibling cell the next full review finds. Six waves of that signature is the datum. So this is chartered as a **fix pass** — the human's decision, 2026-09-21, on the reading that *fixing usability led to new functionality with errors*, and that v1 must be a usable product, not a review that finds nothing.)*

**The exit rule — the human's own gate, written once here and graduated to `DECISIONS.md` the same day.** *[Completed 2026-09-22, on the first time it fired: a tier-1 row the partial re-review finds **outside** the fix pass's own new code is fixed as a **post-review fix under the same milestone** — fix, an independent review of the diff standing in for the audit, the record, a new stamp, the affected axes re-driven — never a new milestone and never a wave; the rule's original text named only the inside case.]* *The 1.0.0 call is taken when a partial re-review of the fix pass's affected axes finds no tier-1 row. Tier-2 and tier-3 findings never block the call and are triaged into the ledger for 1.x. A finding inside a fix pass's own new code triggers another fix pass and a partial re-review, never a full wave.* A review that drives a 47-leaf cell matrix will always find a surface lie somewhere; without a written criterion the loop is infinite by construction.

**The boundary — narrower than M52's.** *The four tier-1 rows, fixed with **no new mechanism**: a row in a registry that already exists, or a condition on a guard that already exists — no new disposition, no new family, no new registry, no new finding code unless the row's door already carries none, and zero schema-hash movement.* A fix that needs a new mechanism to be complete is a **halt**, not a build decision: it goes to the human with the mechanism named and its sibling cells enumerated, because a new mechanism is where the next tier-1 row comes from. Acceptance is **the three affected axes re-driven** (2 · 3 · 5) on the installed `1.0.0-rc.17` with M52's re-pointed instrument, not eight.

**Tier 1 — the four rows** (citations into [the review](../completions/artifacts/M52/per-axis-review/README.md) → §A Tier 1):
- **`(3, A3-1)` HIGH** — `jigc milestone finalize` removes `.jigc/milestones/<id>/` with `remove_dir_all` and destroys every byte jigc did not write there, at exit 0, named by nothing, while `milestone discard` and `uninstall` refuse or narrate over the identical plant. *The fix that is not new mechanism:* the milestone area joins the subject the wave already built — its jigc-written set as a registry row beside the task-area and sub-task rows, its complement displaced through the shipped primitive to `.jigc/displaced/<milestone-id>/`, named on the existing `displaced` key. The registry's own doc-comment (*every door answers for what it removes*) is the rule stated and violated.
- **`(3, A3-2)` HIGH** — when the displacement fails (a file at `.jigc/displaced`, or it read-only), both `Displace` doors remove the area anyway and say only that the *move* failed; the bytes are permanently gone at exit 0. *The fix:* the removal is **conditioned on the move having succeeded** — a failed displacement leaves the area in place and refuses with the door's existing `foreign-bytes` identity naming the path and the failing move; the axis `{move succeeds, fails} × {removal succeeds, fails}` pinned in all four cells at both doors.
- **`(2, DEFECT A)` tier-1 by consequence** — a clean `git cherry-pick --no-commit` leaves only `MERGE_MSG` (no `CHERRY_PICK_HEAD`, index fully merged), is a member of no `InProgress::ALL` row, and `jigc task finalize` concludes it at exit 0, committing the picked payload under jigc's subject and destroying the picked commit's authored message. *The fix:* one more `InProgress` member detected by `MERGE_MSG ∧ ¬(MERGE_HEAD ∨ CHERRY_PICK_HEAD ∨ REVERT_HEAD ∨ SQUASH_MSG)` — the authored-message-pending state — refusing at every acting door with the route git itself offers (`git commit` to conclude with that message, or `rm .git/MERGE_MSG` to drop it), and the family's three homes (`finalize.md:31`, `validation.md:664`, the enum's doc-comment) kept true by generation. Bound to carry: git 2.54.0's on-disk contract, as M52 declared it.
- **`(5, DEFECT 1)` tier-1 as repository harm** — `jigc milestone create ""` / `--title "!!!"` and `add-task`'s sibling mint and **commit** a record at the degenerate `<ty>:<ty>` identity, because `mint_id`/`mint_sub_id` apply an empty→type-name fallback with no guard in front of it — the guard `state.rs:2041`'s own doc-comment says exists. *The fix:* the guard the doc-comment names, at both minting doors, refusing before the commit with the shipped `work-unit.malformed-id` identity (the empty-id cell M50 closed at 25 resolve doors, applied at the two mint doors it missed).

**Tier 2 and tier 3 — 23 rows, triaged to the ledger for 1.x, none blocking.** The eight route/posture dead ends (`(1, A1-N1)` a `-`-leading operand dead-ends the receiving CLI at exit 2, two producers · `(1, A1-N2)` a `-` root-knob value makes the door's own `git mv` unparseable and the route prescribes re-running it · `(2, DEFECT C)` a posture breach at the commit seam prints no state clause · `(4, DEFECT 1)` `finalize.stage-failed`'s route not copy-runnable · `(5, DEFECT 2)` `config remove-step`/`replace-step` refuse a step in the include list · `(6, D-1)` orientation reports a live task's findings without the repository posture · `(6, D-2)` `fix-task` composable by name with its forbidden door · `(7, A7-F3)` `doc show` over a relocated managed doc routes at none of the repair paths) and the fifteen surface lies are **the first entries of the findings ledger** (below), each keyed to its review row, each with a 1.x trigger: *the first adopter finding on that surface, or the wave that next touches its producer*. **Not re-tiered, not silently dropped.**

**After the call — the port, and the findings ledger as its first act (direction, not a charter).** Once 1.0.0 is called, this repository migrates onto it (the post-1.0 direction set 2026-07-10, kept): the running docs first (`roadmap` · `deferral-ledger` · `decisions-log` · a `milestone-record` per new milestone), `completions/artifacts/` left as plain committed records, and `CLAUDE.md` shrunk to the invariants, how we work, and *run `jigc start`* — the project-state paragraph and its `foldback_truth` fence retire into the managed docs that already exist for them. **The port's first act is a methodology doctype for findings** — an append-only repeatable `findings-ledger` (an item per finding: tier · door · repro block · status · `pinned-by`) plus a `report-finding` workflow that appends one item and lands it as a record-only commit — so auditors, validators, subagents and adopters report through jigc, and the 23 rows above are its seed. A new methodology doctype is free at the freeze; triage stays the razor's, so the port never becomes a requirements source (M49's rule, kept). **Order, decided:** the four rows → the three-axis re-review → the 1.0.0 call → the port. Two of the four rows are in the doors a port exercises daily (`milestone finalize`, the displacement seam), which is why the fix comes first.

**Standing rules that bind the pass** — M52's, unchanged: a fix is complete over its class's axis; every rule-shaped fix names its axis and its acceptance iterates it; a source read is a lead, not a finding; **an agent's report is a lead, not a measurement**; zero schema-hash movement; fixers one at a time; the bump to `1.0.0-rc.17` after the audit's fixes, not before.

### The rc.16 wave (M52) — CHARTERED 2026-09-16 · PLANNED 2026-09-17 · BUILT 2026-09-20 · ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-09-21** as `1.0.0-rc.16`

*(**Carried out of the completion audit, 2026-09-21** — three defects the fixers found outside their finding's class, each with a trigger, per [VERDICT.md](../completions/artifacts/M52/VERDICT.md) → Declared bounds: **(a)** `reconciliation.rename`'s route names `jigc rename` on a fixed-identity doctype's uncommitted move, which `store.fixed-identity` refuses — a route dead end at that producer; *trigger:* the next wave that touches `reconciliation.rename`'s producer, or the next adopter finding on it. **(b)** `command-output-contract.md:197`'s rule *a reject that carries a finding takes the findings arm* is broader than the binary: `store.malformed-slug`, `pack.resource-missing` and the `workflow-refs.*` flatten path are deliberate exemptions the sentence does not carve out; *trigger:* the next contract act (any envelope arm move), which must either carve them out in the sentence or move them. **(c)** `jigc ingest`'s route for a below-version doc at a prior home says *move it, then re-run ingest* rather than naming `migrate-corpus`; *trigger:* the next `ingest` surface change. None is a loss cell.)*

*(**Planned 2026-09-17** — [settle-record.md](../completions/artifacts/M52/settle-record.md) D1–D13 + review amendments §1–§17, [roadmap](roadmap.md) → Milestone 52, eleven increments. **The six Settle forks below were re-posed against the driven baseline and every one resolved robust**; fork 4 dissolved into a repair (the docs already say `from`), fork 2's consent arm was unavailable (a posture consent flag is new capability), and forks 1 and 3 were re-posed because the baseline falsified their framing. **Deferrals minted at the Settle, each with a trigger:** (a) **git 2.54.0's marker contract** — the posture family's `REVERT_HEAD`/`applying`-vs-`onto`/squash facts are that git's on-disk contract, declared not fenced; *trigger:* the first CI or adopter report on another git major/minor where a marker cell diverges. (b) **The departed-doctype orphan half** (a stamped orphan whose doctype is gone from the composition, e.g. five doctypes under `placement-root: .` with the pack dropped, `validate` exit 0) — stays declared residual 2's second half; its trigger is the namespaced-stamp one already recorded in this file, which **cannot fire inside M52**, so the un-namespaced stamp key crosses the 1.0 pin, said so. (c) **The surface-tier latents** the baseline drove that Increment 10 does not carry — `staged_doc_ids`' directory-as-managed on `doc list --task` beyond the `is_file()` fix, the `task list` array's discriminator, `describe --commands`' argv; *trigger:* the first wave that takes those surfaces into the pinned contract, or the next adopter finding on any of them. The seven open leads' dispositions are D13 in the settle record. **BUILT 2026-09-20**, and the marker is deliberately not the ✅ SHIPPED its neighbours carry: the eleven increments have landed and none of the completion workflow's acts has — no completion audit, no persisted verdict, no version bump + install, no per-axis review re-run — so the heading claims the build and nothing beyond it, and it takes no version numeral because the 1.0.0 call is the human's.)*

*(Chartered by the human the day [the M51 per-axis review](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/README.md) landed, on his reading of it against the **RC-m50 precedent**: that trial's one blocker was an exit-0 destroying door, and the 1.0.0 call was not taken over it. The review found the same class behind **committing** doors, driven. **So the 1.0.0 call is NOT taken on `1.0.0-rc.15`.** Acceptance shape is **owed to planning** — `milestone-plan` runs next and decides it against a baseline that drives the binary — and is deliberately not decided here.)*

**The evidence base.** The review is M51's **D15 acceptance instrument**, not a trial: eight axes, three agents each (one Opus driver owning the `(door, cell)` table · one unseeded Codex source pass · one reconciler), every row **driven on the installed release `jigc 1.0.0-rc.15`** built from `35195f56` on 2026-09-16, fixtures built with [`dev/jigc-rig`](../dev/jigc-rig), nothing written into the working repository. Roll-up: **56 CONFIRMED · 5 REFUTED · 7 OPEN**, coverage **47 / 47 `VERB_KINDS` leaves** with `uncovered: none` and the `only-5` set down to one (`config list`). Of the CONFIRMED set, **§A's 39 name a defect or a contradiction** — **27 driver-found and 12 Codex-origin driven to a repro by the reconcilers** (axis 2 `codex-1` · axis 3 `C-1` · axis 4 `C1`–`C4` · axis 5 `DEFECT D` · axis 7 `C-1`/`C-2` · axis 8 `CX-1`–`CX-3`) — while §B's rows are the source passes' **positive** completeness claims, driven and held, and are not wave items. Every axis carries its own *what I did not drive* section; those are summarized in the README's **HONEST BOUNDS** and are not restated here.

**The boundary — M51's own, kept.** *All **39** of §A's rows, tiered; the **7** open leads dispositioned with triggers; **fixes + understandability only, no new capability**.* The wave changes what an existing surface **does** and what it **says**; it opens no new domain.

*(**Counting note, stated rather than smoothed.** The tiers below count the way §A counts — `C1`–`C4` are **four** rows, not one class — so tier 0 is **12**, tier 1 **17**, tier 2 **10**, and the three sum to 39. The charter as proposed to the human said 10 / 17 / 12: the 10 counted `C1`–`C4` as one class, and the 12 was a slip for 10. **Every one of the 39 sits in exactly one tier, and no row was moved between the proposed tiers and these.** Citations are into [`completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/`](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/README.md); each axis file's own §-numbers are on its §A heading.)*

**Tier 0 — exit-0 loss or repository harm through a committing or destroying door (12).** This is the tier that keeps the call untaken; it is built first, risk-first.
- **A1-D1 (HIGH)** — a bogus `<slug>` head on a **singleton** is accepted by five write doors (`doc set-slot` · `add-item` · `retitle-item` · `remove-item` · `set-field`) and `jigc task finalize` then silently drops all but one authored payload, at exit 0. ([axis-1.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-1.md) §3, §11)
- **A1-D2 (MEDIUM-HIGH)** — `jigc relocate <freeze-exempt type> --from .jigc` `git mv`s jigc's own install artifacts into the doctype's home at exit 0. ([axis-1.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-1.md) §3, §11)
- **axis-3 `C-1` class** (Codex-origin, driven wider than claimed) — a **non-`.md`** file under a task's working area is destroyed at exit 0 by **five** doors (Codex named three), `jigc task finalize` among them, which is in no destroying registry at all; no refusal, no narration. ([axis-3.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-3.md) §5, ledger §2)
- **axis-4 `C1`** (Codex-origin, driven) — the **promote** rollback is unconditional and emits no conflict finding; driven at `task finalize` and, new, at `milestone finalize`. ([axis-4.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-4.md) §5, §R1–R6)
- **axis-4 `C2`** (Codex-origin, driven) — `RecordPreImage` rollback restores the milestone record unconditionally; a racer's appended line is gone and nothing names it. ([axis-4.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-4.md) §5, §R1–R6)
- **axis-4 `C3`** (Codex-origin, driven) — `RecordFlipGuard`'s `Drop` is a **second** unconditional restore, driven at `milestone finalize` with the racer's edit likewise gone and no finding. ([axis-4.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-4.md) §5, §R1–R6)
- **axis-4 `C4`** (Codex-origin, driven) — `milestone provision` amends `.jigc/.gitignore` and then fails without acknowledging it, while the registry calls that ack *"the only notice"* of the change. ([axis-4.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-4.md) §5, §R1–R6)
- **axis-4 `DEFECT 2`** — M51's compare-and-swap rollback discipline reached **one** of the three worktree axes; **promote** loses a concurrent editor's bytes silently and **retire** never puts the retired original back, neither named by any finding. ([axis-4.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-4.md) §5, §R1–R6)
- **axis-2 `D3`** — an un-concluded **cherry-pick or revert** is not a member of the posture family, and `jigc task finalize --carry-staged` **concludes it** under jigc's own subject at exit 0: a *staging* consent silently spends the user's in-progress operation. ([axis-2.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-2.md) §4, §R.1–R.2)
- **axis-7 `D-1`** — `jigc ingest` durably **absorbs** a blocking out-of-band edit (`file-state.hash-matches`) and surfaces nothing — `"finding": null, "adopted": true`, later `validate` green — against `design/reconciliation.md`:186's *"No silent discard, ever. Every block surfaces; every absorb surfaces."* ([axis-7.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-7.md) §4, §8.1–8.2)
- **axis-7 `D-2`** — a **`Relocated`-only** schema bump is invisible to `migrate-corpus` (exit 0, every array empty), so `validate` is green and `doc list` says *no committed docs* while a below-version managed doc sits on disk: a **total** false green, on both home kinds, and `relocate`'s own refusal routes at the verb that changes nothing. ([axis-7.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-7.md) §4, §8.1–8.2)
- **A6-1** — a sub-task's composed surface is **byte-identical** inside and outside its worktree, and the milestone boundary then drops the staged code its own composed body invited, at exit 0. ([axis-6.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-6.md) §3, ledger B1–B2)

**Tier 1 — posture and route dead ends, code-less or undeclared refusals (17).** No byte dies here; a caller is told something that does not run, or told nothing at all.
- **axis-2 `D1`** — a commit-on-behalf door never reports `repo.operation-in-progress` under a **rebase** or a **bisect**, and the code it reports instead routes at a command git refuses. ([axis-2.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-2.md) §4, §R.1–R.2)
- **axis-2 `D2`** — `rebase-apply` is `git am`'s marker too, so jigc names *a rebase* and routes at `git rebase --abort`, which git refuses; HEAD stays attached, so all twelve acting doors reach the cell. ([axis-2.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-2.md) §4, §R.1–R.2)
- **axis-2 `D3b`** — git's own refusal during a cherry-pick is dressed as a **pre-commit hook rejection**, with no code and no route, where no hook exists. ([axis-2.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/axis-2.md) §4, §R.1–R.2)

**The 7 open leads — each with its stated reason and a trigger.** *(§D writes **six** entries where the roll-up totals **seven**: axis 6's roll-up counts four leads where its ledger writes three, folding the driver's four §4 leads into one entry. The README states that discrepancy itself and does not smooth it; the entries below are the leads as the axis files write them, and the count note travels with them.)*
- **axis-1 · `C10`'s fence half** — *all route constructors invoke the backticked-command-span fence in debug builds.* Reason it is open: not drivable here — the reconciliation binary is the **release** rc.15, where those `debug_assert!`s do not exist, and even on a debug build the fence fires only on a violating route, which no CLI argv can construct from outside the process. *Trigger:* the first wave that **adds or moves a `Route` constructor** — the fence's subject moves with it, and a debug-posture seam suite is the only instrument that can see it (M43's declared proof split is the precedent).
- **axis-1 · `O5`** — a tampered `.jigc/tasks/<id>/source-path` makes `--approve` silently retire nothing (`normalized()` resolves `../outside/victim.md` before the sink; `retire`'s already-absent arm swallows it). Reason it is open: **premise-bound** — the state needs a hand-edited gitignored workbench file and **no caller token can produce it** (`jigc migrate` refuses every `../` spelling at the door, driven). *Trigger:* the first wave that takes **workbench-state truth** as a subject — i.e. treats a hand-edited `.jigc/` file as an input jigc must adjudicate rather than trust. M49's declared bound on hand-authored shadows is the same shape and the same owner.
- **axis-2 · the fan-out `git merge --ff-only` re-probe** (`task.rs:5614-5619`). Reason it is open: **not driven at the seam by either pass**; the door above it is driven in every posture cell. *Trigger:* **M52's own tier-0 posture work.** If the Settle widens the posture family (fork 2 below), this seam is inside the sweep and must be driven; if the Settle scopes the family narrowly, the lead stays open and is re-posed at the next wave that touches the fan-out join.
- **axis-6 · `C5` is manifest-scoped** — a manifest-less project-local pack can ship an off-catalog workflow that `describe` narrates with `router_hidden: null` and no reason (driven). Reason it is open: **declared**, at `design/surface-contract.md`:127 and `design/introspection.md`:90 — a wave decision, not a hole. *Trigger:* the first wave that makes a **project-local pack a supported adopter surface** rather than a dev affordance — M49's *a project pack is a fork, not a subclass* bound is where that decision lives, and the un-markered M14 path deferral is its sibling.
- **axis-6 · the JSON arm of every composed door carries no task-state lines** (`resume:`, `what's-left:`, `task scope:`, `create-gates:`, `also open:`). Reason it is open: **declared at the seam** (`render::composed`'s `Format::Json` comment) — recorded because axis 6's C2/C4/C5 are, in consequence, **text-arm-only** guarantees. *Trigger:* the first **driver-facing demand** for task state on the machine surface, **or** any wave that moves a task-state line — the declaration is then re-posed rather than inherited, because the text arm and the JSON arm would have diverged by an edit nobody priced.
- **axis-6 · the driver's four §4 leads**, carried unchanged and re-read against the code: `migrate` mints silently (~~matching `write-commands.md`:185~~ while being a **third** work-starting door) **[Amended 2026-09-20 (M52 Increment 9 / T5):** the record it matched is corrected — [write-commands.md](../design/write-commands.md):187 no longer presents the two `jigc start` intent forms as *the* forms that start work, and now names `jigc migrate` as the third with this lead and its trigger. The **behaviour** is unchanged and this lead stays open exactly as written; what is gone is the reading that the silence was *stated* rather than merely carried.**] · ~~`task bind`'s two refusals carry no code and no route (bare `anyhow`, outside the route floor as written)~~ **[Discharged 2026-09-20 (M52 Increment 10 / T7):** its trigger fired at this wave's route sweep, and the settle disposed it as a member of D11's batch ([settle-record](../completions/artifacts/M52/settle-record.md) → D13 lead 6a). The live figure above is held to the table by `crates/cli/tests/count_fences.rs`.**]**; `migrate`'s silent mint is re-posed at the wave that next touches the `MINT_DOORS` enumeration (M49's registry), and the `describe` argv at the wave that takes `describe` into the pinned contract.

**Standing rules that bind the wave** — none of these is new; they are the ones this repo has been burned by, named so the Settle cannot quietly drop one.
- **A fix is complete over its class's axis, not over its reported repro** (M45's complete-fix contract). The evidence that this is still the live failure mode is inside the instrument itself: M51's completion audit widened **every** class it was handed, and this review widened two Codex claims the moment they were driven (axis 3's `C-1`: three doors claimed, **five** driven; axis 5's `DEFECT D`: **47/47**, larger than the claim).
- **Every rule-shaped fix names its axis, and its acceptance iterates that axis** — from a code-side registry, the class's defining case-set matched exhaustively, a derivation stated as one, or a manufactured shape space that **says** it is manufactured (the flow 46 → 52 mold). An arm that pins the reported repro is not acceptance for a rule-shaped fix.
- **The razor, with its necessity leg** — M46's three legs plus M49's fourth, as M51 carried them ([charter.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/charter.md) → *The razor*): **0. necessary** — a one-way door at the pin, or a hole in a declared surface (*this is the leg that refuses*) · **1. demonstrated, not asserted** — a rule stated in a locked artifact and violated at HEAD, or a defect driven on the binary; both require execution · **2. right for any adopter** — if the only beneficiary is our layout, naming or process, it is refused · **3. timing is named**. A razor that cannot refuse is the wrong razor; M49's, M50's and M51's each refused on the record, with citations, and this one is expected to.
- **A source read is a lead, not a finding** — the review's own reconciliation rule, which is M46's planning correction and M50's two planning halts in one sentence. **No fix ships on a source read alone, and no completeness claim ships on driving alone.** This binds subagent output as much as docs.
- **Zero schema-hash movement** — the wave's default, and M51's actual result (zero hashes, zero `schema-version`s, zero corpora). A tier-0 fix may genuinely need a bump — axis 7's `D-2` is a **relocation** class and a home change sits *inside* the `schema-hash` — and if one does, **the Settle decides it explicitly, in those words, with the adopter corpus migration priced**, never as a side effect of a fix.

**Owed at planning — the Settle forks already visible.** Stated here so planning re-poses them against a driven baseline rather than discovering them at decompose.
1. **axis 4 — do the unconditional rollbacks become compare-and-swap?** M51 gave `CONFIG_LAYER_SPECS` the CAS treatment; **promote**, **retire**, `RecordPreImage` and `RecordFlipGuard::Drop` are the third, fourth, fifth and sixth populations of the same class and did not get it. Fork: **one shared capture/CAS primitive every rollback path draws from**, versus per-site guards. **Owed an independent `robust-advocate`** — *"restore what we captured"* is exactly the cheap framing this class keeps re-buying. The answer also decides `DEFECT 1`: whether `finalize.rollback-conflict` reaches the machine surface **at all**, given that the reject envelope is single-key and a second document on stderr is what breaks stream discipline.
2. **axis 2 — does the posture family gain cherry-pick and revert members, or does `--carry-staged` refuse under any in-progress operation?** The two shapes are not the same size: family membership makes **every** commit-on-behalf door report `repo.operation-in-progress`; a `--carry-staged` refusal is narrower and leaves the other doors where they are. `D1`, `D2` and `D3b` ride on the answer, and so does open lead 3.
3. **axis 3 — what discriminator makes a non-`.md` task-area file a destroying-door subject?** The shipped `workbench_paths` excludes `tasks/`/`worktrees/` as *"answered by the doors that own them"*, and driven, those doors answer for `docs/*.md` and nothing else. Fork: widen the guard's subject to **every byte under the task's area** — M49's `fanout_worktree_paths` precedent, *a claim about shape where the door's question is about bytes* — versus a narrower rule that keeps build residue out. Two riders: `jigc task finalize` is in **no destroying registry at all**, so membership is part of the question; and the reachability argument (*the adapter tells agents not to write there*) was heard and **rejected** once at M49, so whichever way it goes it is decided in those words.
4. **axis 7 — does the corpus walk key on `from` or on `to`?** It is documented to key on `from`; driven, it keys on `to`, which is what makes a `Relocated`-only bump a total false green. Deciding **`from`** may move a `placement`/`location` home — see the zero-schema-hash rule, and price the migration before choosing.
5. **axis 1 — where does the `<slug>`-head identity check land?** M50 deliberately kept `store.malformed-slug` **out** of `Address::parse`, measured, because `doc_read_surface`'s law-1 fence reddens there. `A1-D1` is a **well-formed** token naming a non-existent singleton, so it is a different question with the same blast radius: parse boundary, write seam, or `finalize` refusing rather than dropping payloads.
6. **axis 5 — the third reject envelope.** `setup`/`uninstall` serialize a bare `Finding` where two arms are declared. Fork: move both doors onto a **declared** arm (a wire change on a shipped surface — free only while the pre-pin window is open) versus **declaring a third** arm. `doc schema` `contract-version` movement is the price of the second, and `DEFECT B`'s undeclared array is the same decision one door over.

**Acceptance: owed to planning, not decided here.** The instrument that found these was a per-axis review on the installed binary, and whether M52's acceptance is another one, a flow-53 arm set, a blind duress re-measure, or some pairing of them is `milestone-plan`'s call against its own baseline. **The wave ships an rc build at close (the rc-name rule). Then the 1.0.0 call — the human's.**

### M51 — the evidence-check wave (the unadjudicated-axis wave) — ✅ **SHIPPED 2026-09-16** as `1.0.0-rc.15`

*(Built + audited: eleven increments, each with its own [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) entry; [roadmap](roadmap.md) → Milestone 51; acceptance [worked-examples](../design/worked-examples.md) → flow 52. **Five audit findings — 1 HIGH · 1 MEDIUM · 3 LOW from the code review, with the e2e half's two confirmed defects and one LOW folded into the same fixes — all confirmed live before any fix and all fixed axis-complete**, re-verified **3547 passed / 0 failed** — [VERDICT](../completions/artifacts/M51/VERDICT.md); [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-09-16 M51 complete. The row took a dated *BUILT* marker until the bump landed, because the numeral was the bump's to name; it now names it — and `1.0.0-rc.15` is D15's per-axis-review acceptance binary, **not** the 1.0.0 call, which is the human's. **D15's acceptance is now run and persisted** — [the per-axis review](../completions/artifacts/M51/per-axis-review/README.md), eight axes driven on the installed rc.15 built from `35195f56`: **56 CONFIRMED · 5 REFUTED · 7 OPEN**, coverage **47 / 47 `VERB_KINDS` leaves**, `uncovered: none`. The wave is therefore **shipped as `1.0.0-rc.15` and its review complete**; its 39 defect rows are chartered as **the rc.16 wave (M52)** above, and the 1.0.0 call is **not** taken on rc.15.)*

*(Chartered on [the 1.0.0 evidence check](../completions/artifacts/evidence-check-1.0/VERDICT.md) — run 2026-09-10 on `1.0.0-rc.14` built from HEAD `bd348a83`, five Opus reviewers plus one unseeded Codex source review, the gate green at that HEAD (**3341 passed / 0 failed**). Scope, claim, razor, forks and exclusions: [completions/artifacts/M51/charter.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/charter.md). **Preparation only — the decomposition is the planning session's, against a baseline that drives the binary rather than the charter; 22 of the 43 ledger rows are `reproduced-live? yes` and the rest are leads.**)*

*(**Settled 2026-09-11 with the human** — [settle-record.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/settle-record.md), against [gap-findings.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/gap-findings.md)'s 69 ranked gaps, with **six independent `robust-advocate` runs** archived at [advocates/](../completions/artifacts/M51/advocates/). **Fifteen decisions; every fork resolved robust** — three advocates conceding scope against their own interest. [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-09-11.)*

*(Reviewed 2026-09-11 — Opus + Codex; all findings accepted and baked into [settle-record.md → Review amendments](../completions/artifacts/M51/settle-record.md#review-amendments-2026-09-11); four reuse claims corrected.)*

*(Baseline driven 2026-09-10 — [baseline-ledger.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/baseline-ledger.md); it escalated EC-1 to a sink defect, falsified fork 1's cheap arm, widened EC-3 to a class and N20 to every non-hook boundary error, and added 15 cells listed in the charter's Baseline amendments.)*

> **The verdict, in two independent halves: not 1.0-ready as rc.14 stands** — **two data-loss-at-exit-0 defects that no trial reached, both driven live, both outside every M50 registry** (a path-taking argument no registry classifies; a git posture no registry has a cell for) — **and the rc.14 trial's evidence holds and is *stronger* than its record**, whose single strongest stated caution against the 3/3 headline is false as written (EC-31).

**The boundary is decided, not open (the human, 2026-09-10).** The wave takes **every row of the evidence-check ledger (EC-1 … EC-43)** plus the fix-shaped rc.14 rows **F-5, F-9, F-11** and the carried defects **N15, N20, N23**; it leaves post-1.0 the four **capability additions** **F-3, F-8, F-10, F-13**. The criterion is the human's own, from [the RC-m50 handover](../completions/artifacts/RC-m50/handover.md): *"everything that becomes impossible or expensive to change once people start using the product. Now it's cheap."* **Capability additions are additive after 1.0; surface and contract fixes are not.** EC-36 is the pricing instrument that makes the line checkable — cost is per *mechanism* (text · additive key · envelope shape · schema-version bump + migration · schema-format change), never per *contract touched/not touched*.

**The claim, falsifiable:** *no caller-supplied token and no repository posture reaches a door that destroys, commits or moves without that door having adjudicated it — the two exemptions the M50 registries carried (`ArgToken::Plain`'s path family; HEAD posture at `COMMITTING_DOORS`) are closed as classes — and every surface 1.0.0 pins says what the binary does, so that the pin closes over declared keys, true counts and a stated evolution rule.* Falsified if either exemption turns out to be a list rather than a class (then the wave ships a named list with a fence over the list, and says so), or if any row the check calls wrong the binary turns out to have right.

**The tiers, by id** (each item's axis is copied from the ledger in the charter, not re-derived):

- **Tier 0 — blocks the 1.0.0 call:** **EC-1** (the path-taking argument family — `ArgToken::Plain`'s exemption set × {read, record, retire/delete} × {absolute, `../`, symlink, `.git/`}, **incl. re-validation at the destructive sink**) · **EC-2** (HEAD posture × the ten `COMMITTING_DOORS`).
- **Tier 1 — cheap now, expensive after the pin:** **EC-3 · EC-4 · EC-5 · EC-6 · EC-7 · EC-8 · EC-9 · EC-10**, plus **F-5** and **F-11** (a code and a route inside the flattened `{"error"}` string — the envelope shape is priced as fork 5), **N15** (the `--task` miss on the pinned read surface), **N20** (the boundary's non-hook refusal frame — **knob-independent per EC-37**, correcting its recorded scope), **N23** (false-green `validate` on doctype retirement — **re-driven and confirmed by EC-38**).

**The nine forks are SETTLED — [settle-record.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/settle-record.md) is the record, and the dispositions below are pointers, not restatements.** Every fork was argued by an independent `robust-advocate` against a proposer who did not author it (the six cases archived at [completions/artifacts/M51/advocates/](../completions/artifacts/M51/advocates/)), and **every one resolved robust** — three advocates conceding scope against their own interest.

[settle-record.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/settle-record.md) → *Owed at decompose*.

**Carried, not fixed — driven at Increment 1 / T3, outside this wave's subject.** `engine::file_state::ConflictBlock::task` composes `jigc unmanage <source>` as a `Route::mechanical` **eagerly**, on every migration finalize, whether or not a conflict exists — so a recorded source path carrying a byte outside the route fence's shell-inert set panics `jigc task finalize --approve` at **exit 101** in a debug build and emits an unrunnable route in a release one. Driven on an ordinary committed source named `docs/old (draft).md`: `a Route::mechanical argv must parse against the real CLI: token docs/old (draft).md is not shell-safe as emitted … argv ["jigc", "unmanage", "docs/old (draft).md"]`. It is **pre-existing and independent of T3** — reproduced at `abd81df`, before this increment's first commit — and its subject is a route's *quoting*, not a path's admissibility, so fixing it here would be scope this increment does not carry; the engine cannot call `crate::task::shell_token`, so the repair is a quoting seam rather than a one-liner. It is recorded rather than left in prose because a defect with no trigger is forgotten, and it is **stated in the test that routes around it** (`crates/cli/tests/retire_sink_validation.rs` → the pathspec arm drives `:/…`, and `cli::task::tests::every_pathspec_magic_spelling_is_refused_at_the_sink` sweeps the parenthesised forms at the predicate) so the narrowing is visible rather than silent. *Trigger:* the next wave that opens `ConflictBlock`'s routes or the route fence's `shell_safe` leg — **or** the first adopter report of a migration source whose path is not shell-inert, whichever comes first.

**Excluded, each on an existing trigger left in place:** **F-3** (a schema-*format* change plus `doc schema` `contract-version` 6→7) and **F-8** (a frozen `adr` `schema-version` 2→3 plus a shipped adopter migration) — both keeping their triggers in *The rc.14 trial's findings* below, and **both now excluded on the *necessity* leg rather than on *"additive after the pin"***, which the Settle corrected as **inverted** for a shape change to an existing frozen doctype ([settle-record.md](../completions/artifacts/M51/settle-record.md) → D13; **F-8** is flagged there as the natural first post-1.0 migration, an `AddedOptionalField` stamp-only bump, and **F-3** as the expensive one) · **F-10** (a new amend verb; **F-9, the cheapest link in the same chain, is in**) · **F-13** (the trial record classifies it *"a **design** question, not a fix"*) · [the doctype-completeness milestone](#the-doctype-completeness-milestone-post-10--the-jigc-self-migration-prerequisite--direction-set-2026-07-10) (post-1.0 by the human's decision of 2026-07-10) · [the harness-surface wave](#the-harness-surface-wave--chartered-2026-09-04-awaiting-a-milestone) (build infrastructure, reaching no adopter — M50 excluded it on the same ground) · [the capability wave's remaining ledger](#the-capability-wave-m46--the-deferred-capability-tier--chartered-2026-07-23-adjudicated-entry-by-entry-at-the-m48-settle-2026-08-13) (adjudicated entry by entry at the M48 Settle; an evidence check does not re-open it).

### M49 — the pre-1.0 completion wave — SETTLED 2026-08-28

*(Chartered on [the 1.0.0 trial](../completions/artifacts/RC-1.0-final/trial-record.md) — six sessions and an 11-arm walk on `1.0.0-rc.12`, **zero data loss, zero corruption, zero regressions, no blocking findings**. **Nothing in this wave blocks the 1.0.0 call**, which is the human's; the wave is the human's decision to hold v1 to a higher prose-and-routing bar than *"nothing blocks"*. Scope, razor, forks and exclusions: [completions/artifacts/M49/charter.md](../completions/artifacts/M49/charter.md); state of the world, ordered next steps and traps: [handover.md](../completions/artifacts/M49/handover.md). **Preparation only — the decomposition is the next session's, against a baseline that exercises the binary rather than the charter.**)*

> **SETTLED 2026-08-28 — every fork below is disposed; the record of decision is [settle-record.md](../completions/artifacts/M49/settle-record.md), against the driven [baseline-ledger.md](../completions/artifacts/M49/baseline-ledger.md).** The wave was **re-scoped by the human** from *surface completion* to the **pre-1.0 completion wave**: do the work now → ship 1.0.0 → then port this repo onto jigc. **The baseline corrected the charter in four places and falsified it in one**, so the entries below are kept for their reasoning and **superseded by the settle record wherever they disagree** — specifically: **P1 is FALSIFIED** (`execute.yaml` is the *increment* tier, `implement-tasks.yaml` the *milestone* tier — they govern different levels and do not contradict); **P2 is a recorded decision, not a gap** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-21, *the orchestration does not fold*); the **unverified port claim was driven** and is a routing dead end that self-recovers, so the real defect is the self-contradicting composed output; and the **"four shape items stay OUT"** entry is **superseded** — its third, load-bearing ground (*the razor could not refuse them*) was answered by **rebuilding the razor to be general** rather than by shrinking the scope. **N1 and N2 are both TAKEN** (N2 unified to a number). Of the two flagged non-prose items: `milestone provision`'s non-rollback is **REAL but LOW** (recovery holds as declared), and the carryover standing order is **REFUTED** — the gate does protect pre-mint staging, driven three ways.

- **(D) The claim is *apply each shipped fence over its axis*, not *fix N prose bugs* — and it is falsifiable.** Three independent instances: the clap-tip seam covers **2 of N error kinds** (`main.rs` says so itself — *"Every other clap error kind still prints clap's own render"*); the route floor binds `Finding`s only, so an anyhow refusal is outside it **by construction**; and the packs name `doc show` **34 times** and `doc schema` / `doc list` **zero**, because M48's fence pointed at `doc show`. **The discoverability lens has landed seven consecutive times across three dedicated surface waves** — a fourth wave of one-off fixes predicts an eighth. *Trigger:* the M49 Settle.

- **(Q) ✅ TAKEN at M49 (Increment 8) — N1, the nested-section hop returning four finding codes across six doors.** One defect, four codes; the `--unset` cell is verbatim the sentence a prior commit's own message named as the defect it removed. A **stable-key** change — a driver keys on `(code, target)` — so it is cheap pre-1.0 and breaking after. Carried open by M47 as *"the wave's own class through an un-swept axis"*, refused **for scope**, not merit. *Trigger:* the M49 Settle — decide it or lose it.

- **(Q) ✅ TAKEN at M49 (Increment 8), additively — N2, `schema-version` a number on one pinned contract and a string on the other.** `doc show` gained a top-level integer beside its stringly `fields` map, so no existing key changed type and no contract-version moved. `jigc doc schema` emits `2`, `jigc doc show` emits `"2"` — verified live 2026-08-28. [doc-read-surface.md](../design/doc-read-surface.md) records it as *"recorded, not repaired"*: *"a type change is not an additive key, so the pre-1.0 window does not cover it… and a fix wave does not get to make that call by accident"* — while naming the cost: *"comparing the two is exactly the upgrade-path check a driver automates… and today that comparison needs a cast."* **The pre-1.0 window is the last chance**; after the pin, the command-output contract has no version integer and a change means minting a v2. *Trigger:* the M49 Settle — decide it or lose it.

- **(D) F15's structural half is decided OUT, and the one-way-door ledger closes rather than going quiet (2026-08-28).** A managed `roadmap-entry → milestone-record` edge is a **feature**, not a fix, and M48 refused it on three independent grounds: a one-way door on two manifest-frozen doctypes (the *second* candidate bump on `milestone-record` in one cycle) · a per-item `ref` shape **zero shipped doctypes exercise** · and [team-ready-state.md](../design/team-ready-state.md) settling it the other way outright. **Its residual pain ships recorded** as a capability gap — `add-from-spec` seeds from a spec's *criteria* while planning produces prose in a *slot*, so intents are hand-retyped. **Its prose half is IN**: `methodology-docs.md:40` still justifies the roadmap's prose decomposition with *"repeatable-inside-repeatable is not expressible in the schema model"*, **falsified at M22** — the conclusion survives on other grounds, the stated reason rotted.

- **(D) The self-hosting port was analysed and changes nothing structural in this wave (2026-08-28).** The Claude Code workflow survives the port: the planning→execution handoff closes **without** a managed edge, because the prose→intent step is legitimately the LLM's under the determinism boundary (this repo already does it with its `milestone-reader` agent), and `planning-finalize` has named the handoff since M48. **Four shape items stay OUT** — `roadmap.decomposition` slot→nested-repeatable (the only one needing a **new engine transform kind**: *"⛔ build the kind first"*), a per-task `workflow` leaf on `milestone-record` (the code prices it *"a one-way door, out of charter"*), and `increment`/`status` item fields. Out for three reasons, the third load-bearing: they are schema changes the charter excludes · **this repo is not self-hosted, so zero managed roadmap instances exist** and no cheap-now window is being spent · and they violate **no stated rule**, so the razor could not refuse them and the claim would stop being falsifiable. *The counterargument is recorded in the charter rather than omitted.* **The pack does not lie about the gap** — `planning-finalize.yaml` states it verbatim.

- **(D) The port question yielded three Tier 1 items of the wave's exact shape.** **P1** — **two shipped packs describe milestone execution incompatibly**: the methodology pack says *"strictly serially… they share one working tree"* while the dev pack provisions an **isolated worktree per sub-task** and fans out, and `milestone-completion-workflow.md` records **two production incidents (M38, M42)** from violating the serial rule. **P2** — `robust-advocate` is named in **zero pack files** (verified) while two locked artifacts make it mandatory before any defer recommendation. **P3** — `validate.yaml` never states the **independence** requirement that is the point of that phase. *Trigger:* the M49 Settle.

- **(Q) ✅ DRIVEN at the M49 baseline — the port claim is a routing dead end that self-recovers, not a capability gap.** The emitted `Spawn:` line named the wrong workflow; following the refusal's own route composed correctly at exit 0. Fixed at M49 (Increment 10) by reading the recorded per-task workflow. The map asserts `milestone add-task --workflow <other>` then `milestone execute` emits `Spawn: … jigc workflow sub-task --task <id>`, which the W-equality re-entry guard then refuses — *"two features individually correct and mutually unusable."* **Not driven.** A routing dead end belongs in this wave; a capability gap does not. *Trigger:* the M49 Settle — drive it first.

- **(D) Two non-prose items are flagged so they are not lost a second time.** `milestone provision`'s mid-phase-2 non-rollback, which [next-wave-scope.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-1.0-gate/next-wave-scope.md) marked *"IN, if cheap"* and **no M46 increment took**; and an undischarged standing order — *"the carryover gate protects staging that exists at the mint, not staging that existed before the session — verify it before the Settle… do not drop it"* — which no M46 artifact records verifying. *Trigger:* the M49 Settle, as scope questions rather than as prose fixes.

### Chartered and cut, awaiting a wave — the Fix phase rides the fan-out

*(Settled 2026-09-02 against an independent [robust-advocate](../completions/artifacts/M49/) brief that drove the whole shape end to end on `1.0.0-rc.13` across nine throwaway repos. The fork was **(a) fan out the Fix phase** vs **(b) enforce seriality with a fence**; **(a) taken**. Written in increment form so it drops into a decomposition unchanged.)*

**Why (a), in one line each.** (b) can only live in `.claude/agents/*.md` or the JS harness — **outside anything `cargo test` can red** — because jigc structurally cannot build it without revising *Concurrency is one bounded primitive only* **and** contradicting a pinned surface (`crates/cli/src/render.rs:312` composes *"several open tasks are legal… you can run them in parallel while their work stays disjoint"*, with a standing test pinning that affordance). So (b) is **tier 3 forever, and is remedy #4 of a shape that has failed three times** — M38 reinforced the prose, M42 added the ⚠, M49 put it in an agent file, and each remedy is the object the doc itself indicts (*"a rule that lives in prose the actor must remember to read… rots at exactly the moment it is needed"*). (a) is **tier 1**, needs **zero Rust**, and is the transcription [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-21 Direction already committed to (*"the eventual encode is transcription, not redesign"*).

**Not a one-way door, and the advocate declined to claim it was** — no schema bump, no frozen-doctype change, no new engine primitive; reversal is a step rewrite plus a golden regeneration.

**Not sold on speed, on the advocate's own measurement**: cold gate **8m39s**, warm **4m34s–5m18s**, ~1.5 G `target/` per worktree; four serial warm gates ≈ four parallel cold ones. **The win is correctness and unattended safety.** And the human's supporting argument was corrected in the same brief: increments run **serially by design** (*each task builds on the last*), so self-hosting will not make *them* fan out — but **audit fixes are the one set in this methodology that is independent by construction**, which is why the primitive fits here and nowhere else.

**There is no "do nothing" arm.** `packs/methodology/steps/fix-gate.yaml:7` still says *"strictly serial over the one shared working tree"*, and the doc's interim mitigation (*gate in an isolated worktree, stage only your own paths*) **reaches no composed step at all** — `worktree` appears **once** in the whole methodology pack, in `migration-finalize.yaml`, about something else. Even (b) would require a pack edit.

---

#### PREREQUISITE increment — the milestone boundary gates as hard as the task doors — ✅ SHIPPED 2026-09-04 (`1799a2d`)

*(Built ahead of its wave, because the trial that follows runs on the binary it fixes. **The class was two author-required leaves, not the one reported** — `type` and `summary`, the latter landing `feat:` with an empty subject; the acceptance asserts set-equality with the schema's author-required set, so a third leaf reddens rather than shrinking the axis. `squash: true` is carved out with its reason — the aggregate message is CLI-synthesized and reads no commit doc — and `design/finalize.md`'s claim that the gate is *"knob-independent"* was corrected rather than preserved: which docs the boundary commits is exactly what the knob decides.)*

**Deliverable.** `jigc milestone finalize` gates a sub-task's transient `commit:<sub-id>` doc the way `jigc task finalize` does. [validation.md](../design/validation.md) states the door list is complete and that *"for every finding the trailer counts, **that door gates as hard as the two task doors**"* — **false at HEAD**, driven: a sub-task commit doc with `type` unset blocks at **exit 3** on the task door and lands at **exit 0** through the milestone door, because the transient doc is materialized by the join, never promoted, and so never enters the merged doc set the M45 conformance gate validates.
**Grouped scope.** The gate's subject widened to the materialized sub-task commit docs · a red test at the milestone door for an author-required field · the axis stated (every `squash: false` fan-out, not the reported cell) · `validation.md`'s claim made true rather than softened.
**Proves.** A pre-existing defect on any `squash: false` fan-out, and a **hard precondition** of the increment below — routing every audit fix through the milestone boundary would otherwise ship worse commit messages than the serial path it replaces.

#### Increment — the completion workflow's Fix phase composes fan-out/join over confirmed findings — ✅ SHIPPED 2026-09-07 (M50 Increment 11)

*(The argument below is the record of the fork as it stood; what landed differs on two points, both settled at D11. Partitioning is by each fix's **projected write-set**, not one-sub-task-per-file — this repo's own group roots refute that — and `fix-task` ships **without** `allows-create`, since a fix that would revise a settled decision is contested and belongs at the human gate. `fix-gate.yaml` no longer says "strictly serial over the one shared working tree"; §3 states the composition the step composes.)*

**Deliverable.** A confirmed audit finding becomes a milestone sub-task, provisioned into its own git worktree at the audited base pin, fixed concurrently, and joined at the barrier — so **two fixers physically cannot race one index**, and the concurrency rule stops being a thing an orchestrator must remember. **Zero engine or CLI change; no new `fan-out:` marker in the methodology pack** — the fan-out already ships in the dev pack's `milestone-execution` walk, and `jigc milestone execute <id>` composes it whole.

**Grouped scope.**
- **`steps/triage.yaml`** (edit) — a confirmed finding becomes `jigc milestone add-task <fix-milestone> "<code>: <finding>" --workflow fix-task`. Same-file findings are **partitioned at triage** into one sub-task or the next round; `join` is a free pre-check that commits nothing.
- **`steps/fix-gate.yaml`** (edit) — the *"strictly serial over the one shared working tree"* clause is replaced by `Run: jigc milestone execute <fix-milestone-id>`. The `checkpoint:` front-matter and the three-round cap are untouched. (The step cannot itself carry `fan-out:` — `compose.rs` enforces *a step is one kind* — which is what keeps the cut minimal.)
- **`workflows/fix-task.yaml`** (new) — mirrors the dev pack's `sub-task`: `creates-task: true`, `selectable: false`, `suppressed: {reason, expires: never}`, `allows-create: [{type: adr, as: decision}]`.
- **`steps/fix-finding.yaml`** (new) — **transcribed** from `fix-gate.yaml`'s existing body: the red that fails *because of the defect*, the minimal green, and the **derive-the-axis-not-the-site-list** brief. The serial sentence is the only clause that does not survive the move, because the worktree makes it unnecessary.
- The pack-load fences these must satisfy (`suppressed` reason/expiry · `when:`/`description:`/`usage:` presence · the named-fact map · `read.staged-read-back`) and a compose-golden regeneration.

**Proves.** The fence is the primitive rather than the prose: the rule that failed at M38, M42 and M49 becomes unstatable-because-unnecessary. Driven acceptance — run the completion loop over ≥2 confirmed findings and land **one conventional commit per finding** in task-id order (`finalize.fan-out.squash: false`), then prove a same-file pair blocks at `join` with `combine.code-collision`, its contending sub-task ids, its route, and **nothing committed**.

**Declared bounds, carried in.** Same-file findings need partitioning at triage — serial execution handles them better, because fixer 2's red test would run against fixer 1's *committed* fix; this is the genuine simplicity the cheap arm keeps. A dirty main checkout is safe **unless** it holds edits to a file a fixer also touched, which fails as a raw `git merge --ff-only` error with **no jigc code and no route** — pre-existing on the shipped primitive, not introduced here, and worth its own entry.

*Trigger:* the next chartered wave — this is cut, not deferred; it awaits a milestone to belong to, and the prerequisite increment lands first.

### The harness-surface wave — CHARTERED 2026-09-04, awaiting a milestone

*(Origin: an external study read the build harness as a system and reported four defects ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-09-04). Three verified, one PARTIAL — its mechanism and cause exact, its diff wrong: the command names **11** of the 14 gates and **3** are missing, not 4; `census` has been present since the same week it was minted. All four are **fixed**. What follows is what the sweep for their *siblings* found, driven not read.)*

> **The claim, and it is falsifiable: every fence this repo built stops at the repo's own tooling.** `crates/cli/tests/planning_gate_home.rs` taking this repo's own `.claude/` harness as its subject on 2026-09-04 is the **first time any fence has** — every prior `.claude/` reference in the suites is the adapter's installed artifacts inside a *throwaway* repo, or a comment — and it found an 11-of-14 stale copy on its first run. The three laws ([surface-contract.md](../design/surface-contract.md)) and the axis-iterating substrate ([pinning.md](pinning.md)) bind the product; the harness that *builds* the product is held to neither, and it is the surface every delegated agent acts on. **The razor is M46's, three legs, and it must be able to refuse:** the rule is **stated** in a locked artifact · **violated** at HEAD · **demonstrable** by driving. If it refuses nothing, the claim is wrong.

**Boundary, stated so the wave is not smuggled into M50.** M50's admission criterion is about **the product** at v1 — *"everything that becomes impossible or expensive to change once people start using the product"* ([handover](../completions/artifacts/RC-m50/handover.md)). Almost nothing below meets it: a stale `.claude/` role file reaches no adopter. **Exactly one item is product and is routed to M50 instead** (T1-a). The rest is build infrastructure and competes for no v1 scope. **Two items are time-boxed to *before* the release** and are called out as such.

**Tier 1 — a confident wrong answer at exit 0, or work destroyed.** *(Ranked by silence, per the sweep.)*

- **(a) → ROUTED TO M50, not this wave. `jigc task validate ""` panics at exit 101.** Driven on `1.0.0-rc.13`: `crates/engine/src/finding.rs:741` — *"a `Route::mechanical` argv must parse against the real CLI: token `` is not shell-safe as emitted"*, argv `["jigc","task","discard",""]`. A shipped verb panics on empty input, and the route fence asserts on a route built from an id the door never validated. This is the **un-swept sibling of M49's own audit finding** (*"the clap seam derived as 17 kinds… one of them panicked at exit 101"*). **The class was sized, not assumed:** of seven id-taking doors driven with `""`, exactly **one panics** — and `jigc task discard ""` **acks at exit 0**, a second smell on the same axis. The axis is *doors that build a `Route::mechanical` from an unvalidated caller token*, and it is code-side enumerable.
- **(b) The gate's probe prebuild silently stops preventing the race it exists to prevent.** [dev-workflow.md](dev-workflow.md):13-18 and [machine-setup.md](machine-setup.md):25-31 both write the prebuild **without `--target-dir crates/cli/probes/doc-code/target`**, which `dev/gate`:213-219 passes and whose own comment states the reason. An agent hand-running the prose form with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` set — which `dev/gate --private-target` exports and `JIGC_GATE_TARGET` makes a habit — builds into the private dir while ~30 suites still race on `probes/doc-code/target`. Exit 0 throughout. *(The three gating role files were routed at `dev/gate` on 2026-09-04, which removes the hand-run form there; the two docs and CI are untouched.)* *(Datum 2026-09-20, M52 Increment 9's validator: `doc_code_gate::task_validate_probe_present_resolves_clean_control` went red on two of three full `dev/gate` runs with `probe not found at …/probes/doc-code/target/debug/doc-code` while a parallel group target was relinking that shared binary; `g_doc` alone and the third full run were green. The race this item exists to prevent is live inside the gate itself, and a red gate indistinguishable from a regression is the trigger firing; explicitly not in M53's scope.)*
- **(c) CI runs no probe prebuild at all.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs fmt · clippy · build · test and one manifest-fence step — no probe step, so CI is the one gate runner that never prebuilds. Driven by reading every `run:` in the file.
- **(d) `dev/gate`'s red-gate diagnostic folds in a *different workspace's* run.** `gate_totals` correctly excludes test **names** after `4171e10`, but not a **nested** cargo run's genuine summary lines: `crates/cli/tests/doc_code_probe_suite.rs`:33-39 shells out to the detached probe workspace and embeds its stdout verbatim in the panic message, which lands in the log. Driven via `--report`: the totals absorb the nested run and `gate_failing_names` re-enters its `failures:` block, naming `resolve::tests::vue_script_extract` — **a test not addressable from the root gate at all**. Verdict-safe (it only fires on a red gate), but that suite exists *precisely* to go red on a probe regression, so the input is guaranteed at the moment the report is read.
- **(e) Two forks in `milestone-build.js` that a patch cannot settle — they need a decision.** The rate-limit breaker's doc-comment states its discriminator as *"a genuine blocker fails ONE agent repeatedly (same label, retried in place)"*, but **no label is reused across rounds** (`fix:incN:rM`, `validate:incN:rM`), so a genuinely broken fix task trips the **rate-limit** breaker and a code fault is reported as an account limit. Re-keying labels changes the agent-call cache key and therefore the **resume contract**. Separately, `deliverable_holds` is **required by `VALIDATION_SCHEMA` and never read**: `deliverable_holds:false` with zero blocking findings and a proven gate logs *"validated CLEAN"* and proceeds to the audit.

**Tier 2 — a fence exists, and its scan stops one directory short.** *(This is finding #3's class, and the reason the harness-surface claim is not merely tidiness.)*

- **(f) The "twelve group targets" fence is blind twice over.** `crates/cli/tests/test_target_registration.rs`:355 builds its needle as `format!("{} group targets", …)` — a **decimal** — and checks exactly **two** files (`Cargo.toml`, its own source), while its doc-comment claims *"the number is asserted where it is written"*. Five other statements spell it **"twelve"**: `CLAUDE.md`, [dev-workflow.md](dev-workflow.md), [machine-setup.md](machine-setup.md), `.claude/agents/build-executor.md`, `.claude/agents/increment-validator.md`. Accurate today; unfenced and unreachable by the fence, in both form and location.
- **(g) `COMMITTING_DOORS` is 10 in code and 9 in the doc of record — and the acceptance suite contradicts itself four lines apart.** Driven: 10 members in `crates/cli/src/invocation_log.rs`; `design/worked-examples.md`:3005 says *"9 doors, 9 distinct identities"*; `crates/cli/tests/flow47_acceptance.rs` says *"10 doors since M49 Inc 2 T3"* at :18 and *"all nine pairwise distinct"* at :21. The tests iterate the code table, so they pass — **only the prose lies**, and nothing reads it. **CLOSED 2026-09-15 (M51 Increment 7, T3)** — `crates/cli/tests/count_fences.rs` reads every home that states the count and asserts it against `COMMITTING_DOORS`; the two live homes were corrected to ten and the historical ones dated-bracketed. The fifth home, `MIGRATING.md:39`, is carried as that suite's `OwedAt` row and lands in Increment 9's single guide batch.
- **(h) `ERROR_CODE_REGISTRY`'s doc mirror is unfenced.** [surface-contract.md](../design/surface-contract.md):115 declares itself an exact mirror — *"member-for-member — **eleven**"* — and the registry test hardcodes 11 and **names that file in its assert message**, while no test reads it. Accurate today; (g)'s twin on the next door added. **CLOSED 2026-09-15 (M51 Increment 7, T3)** — the same suite now reads the table, member-for-member and in order, plus the size the sentence states; both arms were proven to bite by applied mutants.
- **(i) The `rm`-on-a-variable-path scanner fences `dev/` only.** `crates/cli/tests/dev_rig_parity.rs`:157 walks `dev/` recursively. Driven repo-wide: **36** `rm -rf "$VAR"` sites outside it, and the class **splits** — **8 in the live reusable trial tooling** CLAUDE.md indexes by name (`trial-harness/`, `trial-corpus-template/`, `trial-driver/`, `workflow-eval/`), and **28 in archived `completions/artifacts/*/evidence/` drive scripts**, which are frozen records and belong outside the rule for the same reason `DECISIONS.md` does. Only the first 8 are in scope. Per CLAUDE.md's own rule the guard scans the command **text**, so a delegated agent hitting one parks on a prompt nobody is watching, with no transcript record.

**Tier 3 — stated and false, in documents an agent acts on.** *(Finding #1 and #2's class. All four driven.)*

- **(j) [doctype-authoring.md](doctype-authoring.md):30 — a forward-looking caveat for a release that shipped seven waves ago.** *"**Reading this before rc.6 ships:** … until it lands, the **⚠** clause in each affected row ***is* the live behaviour**, and an unclassifiable bump **strands the corpus silently** instead of refusing."* rc.6 is M42 (2026-07-14); the installed binary is `1.0.0-rc.13`. Line :24 of the same file states the opposite as live (*"refuses loudly"*). It sits directly above the transform-kind matrix a doctype author reads **at the moment of bumping a schema**, and it is marked *forward*-looking rather than historical — so a reader who does not know rc.6 shipped takes it as now.
- **(k) [pinning.md](pinning.md):15 — a present-tense claim about the code that the code's own doc-comment refutes.** *"today `crates/cli/src/lib.rs` exposes **neither** the pack registry **nor** the clap tree to `tests/*.rs`, so making that true is a **prerequisite**"*. Driven: `lib.rs`:19 is `pub mod cli;`, :38 is `pub mod pack;`, and the module doc cites **this section by name** as the reason it exists. CLAUDE.md routes planners here as *"the axis-iterating substrate of record"*, so a planner scopes a prerequisite increment for a seam M45 already shipped.
- **(l) [roadmap.md](roadmap.md):839 — a superseded charter whose retraction sits *below* it, now describing a real verb falsely.** :839 charters *"`jigc doc rename`"* with one set of semantics; :841 retracts it (*"the verb is the top-level `jigc rename` (**not** `jigc doc rename`)"*). Since M48 `jigc doc rename` **exists**, with different semantics — so a top-down reader meets a plausible, false description of a shipped verb before its retraction.
- **(m) The gate's own home contradicts itself in one screen.** [dev-workflow.md](dev-workflow.md):13 heads **"all four pass"** over **five** bullets, then :22 asserts *"It is the five commands above, **in order**"* — false on the order too (`dev/gate` runs probe · fmt · clippy · build · test; the doc lists fmt · clippy · probe · test · build). Four further copies each carry a different order. This is the **declared home** of the rule.

**What the sweep found clean, with its evidence — a clean sweep stated is a result.** `completions/trial-driver/driver/channels.py` is the **model** for the Class-C shape: it killed three false positives by name and deliberately keeps one over-match *measured* rather than smoothed. `crates/cli/tests/manifest_freeze_fence.rs` sweeps its trailer scrape over `escape::SHAPES` — the opposite of this class. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` scrapes no output (its gap is (c), not a match bug). Class A over `dev/`: `grep -rniE "historical|previously|old rule|supersed|no longer|deprecat|obsolete"` → **0 hits**. Class A over both packs: 42 hits, all domain vocabulary (`adr.supersedes`, the `deprecated` changelog enum) or past-tense retirements carrying their milestone. Verified-accurate-and-fenced enumerations: the doctype set and shipped `schema-version`s (fenced by `doctype_map_versions.rs`), `MINT_DOORS`, `SetKind::ALL`, `GATE_COVERAGE`, `VERB_KINDS`. **`STORE_EXIT_FLIPS` is the pattern to copy** — [validation.md](../design/validation.md):380 **refuses** to restate the count, recording that the hand-count went stale three times.

**Declared bounds, carried in.** (1) This wave changes **no product behaviour** except T1-a, which leaves it for M50 — so it cannot be justified by v1 readiness and is not offered as such; its case is that the harness is what builds v1.1. (2) The sweep was **three classes over one repo**, not a general audit: (f)–(i) are the members of *"a fence whose scan stops short"* that those three classes reached, and there is no reason to believe the class is closed — the honest form of (i) is that the scanner's own axis is the *repo*, and nobody has enumerated it. (3) Two items are **time-boxed to before the 1.0.0 call, not to this wave**: `completions/trial-harness/verify-pair.sh`:72 matches `"jigc 1.0.0-rc."*` and sends a released `jigc 1.0.0` to its `*)` failure arm — it fires loudly on the very next release; and the same file's :119 `awk '/^  [a-z]/'` drops **digit-leading slugs**, which jigc mints, feeding `""` into the probe at :144 — the reader that found T1-a.

*Trigger:* **the milestone after M50** — this is chartered, not deferred, and it deliberately does not compete with M50's product scope. T1-a is routed to M50; the two `verify-pair.sh` items are due before the release whoever takes them.

*(The review caught the first draft writing new triggers while leaving four **already-fired** ones

  banned — see [settle-record.md](../completions/artifacts/M49/settle-record.md) → D4. **Its fourth
  sub-shape is separately discharged as already working:** a *leading bare-prose slot before a nested

  nested add-item → rewrite the leading slot → set nested slot, all exit 0, bytes correct).

  D5 — ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-30 (M49 Increment 6).** The trigger (*a composition of 3+ packs*) fired the
  moment PB-1 shipped. Settled and built as settled: a listed project pack composes **below** the

  it. The standing entry below carries the discharge, the surviving closed-enum-union deferral, and
  the pointer to the three declared bounds now recorded in

- **The mis-keyed leaf that silently erases a whole section — DISCHARGED as a PRECONDITION of D5 —

  Not the `deny_unknown_fields` attribute this entry named — serde forbids it beside the flattened

### Deferred at the M49 Settle — each with a written trigger

*(Written as triggers because this ledger's own multi-slot ∧ nested entry fired unnoticed once already — and that is the exact failure this ledger exists to prevent. Grounds for each: [settle-record.md](../completions/artifacts/M49/settle-record.md).)*

- **(D) The `roadmap` increment tier (S1) — structured `milestones → increments` instead of a prose `decomposition` slot.** Refused at leg 1 of the M49 razor: **the port does not need it** — the migration writes decomposition prose into a slot, exactly as `planning-finalize.yaml` already states, and the residual pain (intents retyped by hand) is recorded rather than hidden. *Trigger:* a **second** independent driver that is not this repo's own planning convenience — an adopter's decomposition needing per-increment identity, status or addressability, **or** `add-from-spec` being asked to seed from a roadmap entry. **Note the binding precondition, corrected at M50 Increment 6:** the cost is not that the block would be stuck at its birth shape — the classifier descends into a nested block since M50 Increment 6, so a leaf edit one level down classifies with its own kind (`crates/cli/tests/migrate_locus_axis.rs`; [corpus-migration.md](../design/corpus-migration.md) → The third locus). The cost is that **the bump introducing the tier could not run at all**: adding a nested repeatable block to an existing item block is the shape `AddedNestedRepeatable` would name, and that shape is refused, so `roadmap` could never reach the version that has the tier.
