- **(D) `AddedNestedRepeatable` + a nested arm for the existing transforms.** Falls with the tier; building it now would be speculative. *Trigger:* a schema change that **adds** a nested repeatable to an existing item block — which `migrate-corpus` refuses with a route that terminates in `crates/engine/src/transform.rs`, a file no adopter can edit. *(The trigger's second clause as written — a leaf edit one level down — was discharged at M50 Increment 6 and is struck from it; see the discharge below.)* *(Engaged 2026-08-31 — **M49 Increment 4 T2** improved the refusal without building the kind: an added, dropped or reshaped nested leaf now **emits** `Unclassified` instead of vanishing whenever it rode alongside a classified change, which had restamped the doc at a version whose nested shape it does not carry. The deferral and its trigger are unchanged.) **DISCHARGED IN PART at the M50 Settle, 2026-09-04 (D6) — the nested arm, not the kind.** The trigger described HEAD in the present tense and had stood fired since it was written — the failure this ledger exists to prevent. M50 built the third locus; the spike found the cell is `insert_item_slot` generalized over a locus path (~25–35 lines), not a new primitive, and the prior refusal's ground (`M49/settle-record.md:66`) was the port-as-requirements rationale M49 itself retracted at `:35`. **Which half landed, recorded at M50 Increment 6 / T3, because the Settle's word was *in full* and the build is narrower.** The trigger's **second** clause — a leaf edit one level down — **is discharged**: `diff_item_fields` recurses, each such leaf classifies with its own kind at its own locus, and the `kind × locus` disposition is a production table the driver asks before folding, driven by `crates/cli/tests/migrate_locus_axis.rs`; the three byte-writing arms at that locus — `AddedItemField`'s `default:`-carrying half, `AddedItemSlot` and `ValueRemapped` — **fold** since M50 Increment 7, so the table leaves no cell half-built. The trigger's **first** clause — a nested repeatable **block** added to an existing item block — is **not** built: `AddedNestedRepeatable` does not exist, the shape sits outside the table by deliberate design, and this entry **stays open** on that half, its trigger unchanged for it.*

- **(D) A delegation primitive in the workflow dialect** (a one-shot, read-only, result-returning spawn beside `fan-out`/`join`). M49 takes the **prose-only** arm — steps name the actor and the instrument, jigc dispatches nothing. A primitive is an **invariant revision** ([CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) → *Concurrency is one bounded primitive only*), and nothing in the port needs jigc to dispatch — the harness already does. *Trigger:* a composed workflow that cannot state its contract in prose because the actor must be **chosen or counted at runtime** (a bounded retry, a quorum, a fan-out whose width is data) — i.e. the point at which prose stops being able to say what must happen.

- **(D) The `deferral-ledger` 2→3 · `vision` 1→2 · `idea` 1→2 bumps — refused at leg 2, and it is the corpus that changes.** All three were refused at **leg 2** of the M49 razor ([settle-record.md](../completions/artifacts/M49/settle-record.md) → the Tier 3 disposition table, and *Refusals on the record, each at a named leg*): the beneficiary of each is **our** document shape — the `(Q)` kind this very file uses, `VISION.md`'s 12 real `##` sections against 3 slots, and `idea`'s multi-paragraph `trigger` — and the rule is D6's, *we migrate the project to fit the product, not the reverse*. So the manifest standing still at `deferral-ledger: 2` / `vision: 1` / `idea: 1` is **the decision holding, not an omission**: no `schema-version` bump is owed by anyone. What *is* owed sits on the **corpus** side — the port rewrites those three documents into the shipped shapes — and **the window that closes at 1.0.0 does not bind it**: reshaping our own files costs adopters nothing whenever it lands, which is why this refusal, unlike the frozen-bump fork further down this file, does not expire at the pin. *Trigger:* the **self-hosting port** — the *self-hosting port was analysed* entry above, which the wave's settled order places after 1.0.0 — **if** the migration cannot write one of the three documents into the shipped shape without loss (a `(Q)` entry with nowhere to go, a `vision` section the schema cannot hold, an `idea` trigger flattened into one paragraph). That doctype then re-opens as a **schema** question and is decided at the price the pin by then charges — an adopter migration on a released binary — rather than waived for being inconvenient. *(Written 2026-08-31 at the M49 completion audit: [baseline-ledger.md](../completions/artifacts/M49/baseline-ledger.md)'s three `UNPINNED` rows called the bumps *owed* and named increment 12 / T3 as the task landing them, while T3's own scope was the eleven increment entries and it landed no such entry — the punt-with-no-trigger shape this section exists to prevent. `crates/cli/tests/leg_two_refusal_homed.rs` now reads this entry, the Settle's row and the manifest together, so the three cannot disagree again.)*

### Deferred at the M51 completion audit — each with a written trigger

- **(D) A namespaced stamp key, so the orphan sweep's subject can widen back to the whole repository.** The audit's HIGH was that `schema-conformance.orphaned-instance` fired on **any** committed `.md` whose YAML front matter carried a `schema-version:` key — a plain team document using that key for its own purposes flipped `jigc validate` to exit 1 on a stock corpus, with a message calling the bytes *"a jigc schema-version stamp"* (driven: `printf -- '---\ntitle: API notes\nschema-version: 3\n---\n' > api-notes.md`, commit, `jigc validate` → exit 1). **The root cause is the stamp, not the sweep**: the key is unnamespaced, so nothing in the bytes says jigc wrote them, and the *location* is the only honest discriminator available today. The shipped fix narrows the subject to `cli::orphan::Territory` — the resolved `docs-root` tree ∪ a non-repo-root `placement-root` ∪ every resolved doctype's home directory — keeping the finding blocking and its `STORE_EXIT_FLIPS` membership, and leaves **two stated residuals** ([validation.md](../design/validation.md) → the M51 Increment 8 registrations): a foreign stamped file *inside* the docs home still fires, and an orphan at a **root-level** placement home (`VISION.md`) goes unflagged at exit 0. **The real fix is a namespaced key** — jigc already namespaces in the one artifact it owns outright (`SKILL.md`'s `jigc-version:` / `jigc-body-blake3:`), so the precedent and the spelling are on the shelf. Once the stamp is unambiguous the subject widens back to the whole repository **with zero false positives**, both residuals close, and the finding's wording can claim the provenance it currently must not; the widening is **non-breaking** in the safe direction (a stamped doc gains a namespaced key; the un-namespaced key stays readable for corpora written before the bump). *Why it is not built now:* M51's hard bound is **zero schema-hash movement, zero `schema-version` bumps, zero corpus migrations**, and a stamp-key change is all three at once. *Trigger:* **the next `schema-version` bump of any doctype** — that bump already ships a corpus migration, which is the one vehicle the key change needs, so the two ride together or the key change is paying for a migration twice.

- **(I) ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-09-16 (this commit) — the gate's own speed.** All three parts landed on the trigger, before the first `milestone-build` run after M51: `[profile.dev] split-debuginfo = "packed"` (`[profile.test]` inherits it, so the test binaries that produce essentially all of the litter are covered by the one setting); a `cargo clean` of both target trees — **529,616** entries in `target/debug/deps` and **3,206** in the probe's, 53.8 GiB and 735 MiB, to **0** each; **[`dev/clean-litter`](../dev/clean-litter)** as the repeatable reset, named as step 5 of the [milestone-completion workflow](milestone-completion-workflow.md) so the cold rebuild it costs is paid at the close; and — because the profile change cannot see a tree that already carries litter or a profile someone flips back — a **watch**: `dev/gate` counts `<target>/debug/deps` at the top of every run, prints `gate: deps <N> entries`, and warns above 100 000 naming the cleanup, never failing the gate on it (fenced by three arms in `crates/cli/tests/dev_gate_report.rs`). *(The original entry, for the measurement it records:)* Measured by the orchestrator on 2026-09-14, mid-M51: `target/debug/deps` held **1.8 M** split-debuginfo `.rcgu.o` files, and deleting them mid-run took a full `dev/gate` from **~20 minutes to ~9.5 minutes**. The gate is run once per task by every build agent, so the cost is paid tens of times per wave and it silently re-accumulates — which is the shape that makes it a deferral rather than a note: nothing in the repo notices the directory growing back. *What is owed:* set `split-debuginfo = "packed"` on the workspace dev profile so the object litter is not produced in the first place, `cargo clean` once to drop what exists, and add a cleanup step to the build loop so the reset is a step somebody runs rather than a thing somebody remembers. *Why it is not done at the M51 close:* a workspace-profile change forces a **full recompile**, and the close increment's own deliverable is a gate run this record quotes — changing the profile underneath it would make the quoted total unattributable. *Trigger:* **before the next milestone build**, i.e. the first `milestone-build` run after M51, where the saving is spent rather than measured.

### Deferred at the M52 Settle — each with a written trigger

*(On the two sections above's mold, and for their reason: a deferral left in prose has no trigger and will be forgotten ([CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) → *Decisions log*). Rows **(a)**, **(b)** and **(c)** are minted at the M52 Settle — the `### The rc.16 wave (M52)` charter parenthetical at the top of this file states them at the Settle's own altitude and is **cross-referenced here, not restated** ([settle-record.md](../completions/artifacts/M52/settle-record.md) → §16 and D7). The **two residuals inside (c) are not from the Settle**: they were named during the build and landed by no increment, and the clause that lands them here is [roadmap.md](roadmap.md) → Milestone 52 Increment 10's declared bound — *"the surface-tier latents not listed here defer with triggers in `decisions-pending.md`."*)*

- **(D) (a) git 2.54.0's marker contract is declared, not fenced.** The posture family's on-disk facts — `REVERT_HEAD`, `rebase-apply/applying` vs `rebase-apply/onto` as the `am`-vs-`rebase` discriminator, and the squash cells — are **git's** contract, read at the one version the build and its acceptance ran on. Increment 3's guard, Increment 2's fixture builder and flow 53's arm 2 *state* them rather than derive them, because no git API publishes them; so a git release that moves a marker moves a cell underneath a green suite, silently. *Trigger:* the first CI or adopter report on another git major/minor where a marker cell diverges.

- **(D) (b) The departed-doctype orphan half — its trigger cannot fire inside M52, so the un-namespaced stamp key crosses the 1.0 pin.** D7 split `C-2` on its seam: the **declared-home** half was re-taken as `schema-conformance.home-vacated` (Increment 7), and the **departed** half — a stamped orphan whose doctype has left the composition, so no resolved home remains for `cli::orphan::Territory` to read — **stays declared**, as the second half of M51's residual 2. The residual itself, its size and the drive behind it are stated once, in the design home ([validation.md](../design/validation.md) → the M51 Increment 8 registrations, rewritten at D7 to say which half it covers and which it does not); what is owed here is the **trigger**. *Trigger:* the **namespaced stamp key** entry above — *the next `schema-version` bump of any doctype* — and no other, because the honest discriminator is the stamp rather than the location, and widening the subject before the stamp says *jigc* re-opens M51's HIGH. **That trigger could not fire inside M52**: this wave's boundary is zero `schema-hash` movement, zero `schema-version` bumps, zero corpus migrations (asserted, not asserted about, at Increment 11's negative fence), so the un-namespaced stamp key **ships into 1.0** — recorded at the Settle as a consequence, not discovered after the pin.

- **(D) (c) The surface-tier latents Increment 10 does not carry — the three from the Settle, and the two this build named.** The **three** are the charter parenthetical's, cross-referenced and not restated: `staged_doc_ids`' **directory-as-managed beyond the `is_file()` fix** — Increment 4 / T1 landed L-3, so the *shape* half is closed, and what stays open is *provenance*: an entry that is a file and whose name parses as a staged doc id reads `managed` on the 1.0-pinned `doc list --task` no matter who wrote it, which is axis-1 `O5`'s own class (a hand-shaped workbench entry the tool trusts) · the **`task list` array's discriminator** — `command-output-contract.md:181`'s refusal holds, reshaping the one surface a driver reads on every loop costs every driver, and the Settle refused it again · **`describe --commands`' missing argv** — declared non-contractual ([introspection.md](../design/introspection.md) → *Non-contractual by design*, re-declared at `command-output-contract.md:177`). *Trigger:* the first wave that takes those surfaces into the pinned contract, or the next adopter finding on any of them. **Two more defer with them**, each carried here with the datum that put it here rather than left to evaporate. **`start::blanket_base_pin_refusal`** — the sub-task base-pin refusal is one byte-string across the provisioned-but-wrong-checkout state and the never-provisioned one ([baseline-surfaces.md](../completions/artifacts/M52/baseline-surfaces.md) §4.6), the same shape one door earlier than the `resume:` line Increment 10 / T4 discriminated; **Increment 10's basis *and* its T4 notes both say it *"defers with the surface-tier latents"*** and neither that task nor any other took it. **The `parked-foreign` narration half** — `config set`'s *"a foreign file parked out of a destination"* line is a **success**-path narration of `relocate_stranded`'s displacement, so it has no rollback to ride and no clause of Increment 5's Grouped scope reaches it; Increment 5's declared bound **(vi)** routed it to *"the record tier (Increment 10)"*, and none of Increment 10's ten tasks carries it. (Its sibling — the **failed-move** half of the same Increment 1 / T1 residual — *is* discharged, at Increment 5 / T8, where a failed move became a rollback trigger and the fact reaches the findings arm under `config.rollback-conflict`; only the success-path half is owed.) *Trigger:* for both, the surface-tier trigger above — or, whichever comes first, the next wave that opens that refusal's constructor or `config set`'s success-path narration.

*(**The closure check, stated and re-runnable** — it fences the **form** only: that every row under this heading carries a `*Trigger:*` clause, and that the two residuals no increment landed are named **by symbol** rather than gestured at. No mechanical checker can say whether a trigger is *live* or whether a row still belongs here; that is the re-read a later wave's charter does by reading. Run from the repo root; it prints one line per row and exits non-zero on any row that carries no trigger or any residual that has gone missing.)*

```sh
awk '
  /^### Deferred at the M52 Settle/ { s = 1; head = 1; next }
  /^### / { s = 0 }
  s && /^- / {
    rows++
    key = match($0, /\([a-z]\)/) ? substr($0, RSTART, RLENGTH) : "(?)"
    trg = ($0 ~ /\*Trigger:\*/)
    printf "%-4s %s\n", key, trg ? "trigger" : "!! no *Trigger:* clause"
    if (!trg) bad++
    if ($0 ~ /start::blanket_base_pin_refusal/) r1 = 1
    if ($0 ~ /parked-foreign/) r2 = 1
  }
  END {
    if (!head) { print "!! no `### Deferred at the M52 Settle` heading"; bad++ }
    if (!rows) { print "!! the section carries no rows"; bad++ }
    printf "residual start::blanket_base_pin_refusal %s\n", r1 ? "named" : "!! ABSENT"
    printf "residual parked-foreign %s\n", r2 ? "named" : "!! ABSENT"
    if (!r1) bad++
    if (!r2) bad++
    exit (bad > 0)
  }
' implementation/decisions-pending.md
```

### Deferred at the M53 Settle — the 1.x ledger rows, each with a written trigger

*(On the three sections above's mold, and for their reason: a deferral left in prose has no trigger and will be forgotten ([CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) → *Decisions log*). These are the rows the M53 **baseline** surfaced beside the four tier-1 rows the pass fixes — stated once at the Settle's own altitude ([settle-record](../completions/artifacts/M53/settle-record.md) → *Rows the baseline surfaced that M53 does not fix*) and **landed here with their data**, because the Settle's list is one paragraph of names and a name is not a trigger. The Settle tiered every one of them **tier-2/3** — no byte dies and no repository is harmed at any of them — and the **exit rule** is what makes that tiering load-bearing rather than decorative: *tier-2 and tier-3 findings never block the call and are triaged into the ledger for 1.x.* So none of these blocks the 1.0.0 call, and every one takes **the charter's trigger**, quoted where it binds: *the first adopter finding on that surface, or the wave that next touches its producer.* They are the seed of the findings ledger the port's first act mints, along with the rc.16 wave's own tier-2/3 set above.)*

- **(D) (a) `jigc doc add-item --title ""` answers under a third code for the same condition.** One fault — *a title that yields no id* — carries three identities across the write surface: `create.empty-title` at `jigc doc create` and `jigc doc author`, `write.unslugable-title` at the engine write path, and `schema-conformance.field-value-conformant` at the empty cell of `doc add-item` — a conformance identity about a **field value**, raised where the fault is an **unslugable title**. **Driven** at this task, on a `spec:<slug>#criteria` item: `--title "!!!"` → `write.unslugable-title`, *write rejected: title "!!!" has no slug-able content for an item id*; `--title ""` → `schema-conformance.field-value-conformant`, *add-item rejected: the heading is empty (id-from field `title`)* — same door, same argument, two keys, both exit 1. Every cell is safe: all three refuse and nothing is written. The wobble is the driver's: a caller keying on `(code, target)` cannot recognise one condition across three spellings, which is the stable-key discipline M42 shipped, applied one door short. *What is owed:* one identity for the condition, or a stated reason why the empty cell is a different condition from the unslugable one. *Trigger:* the charter's — the first adopter finding on that surface, or the wave that next touches its producer.

- **(D) (b) `create.empty-title` names a door the operator did not run — and the class is the whole finding, not only its route.** The Settle recorded this as *the route says `--title` at `doc author`*; **driven** at this task it is wider, and the widening is recorded rather than smoothed. `engine::state::empty_title_finding` is parameterised on the **doctype alone**, so both of its sentences are literals: `jigc doc author adr` over a payload whose `title:` is `"!!!"` answers *`jigc doc create adr` needs a title that yields an id, but the given title is empty or slugs to nothing*, routed *re-run with a non-empty `--title` (its slug becomes the doc id)* — a **verb the operator did not run** and a **flag that door does not take**, the title having come from the payload's `title:` key. It is law 1's class (a surface saying what the binary does not do), tier-3 because the refusal itself is right and nothing is written: what is false is which door it tells the operator to fix. *What is owed:* the message **and** the route parameterised on the door, as the producer's doc-comment already scopes them to `create`. *Trigger:* the charter's — the first adopter finding on that surface, or the wave that next touches its producer.

- **(D) (c) *"no recorded workflow"* is code-less at `jigc task bind` and at the create-gate doors — and the Settle's *"the `doc` write verbs"* is wider than the binary.** Increment 3 closes the **residual** half of this cell: an area carrying no base pin is a task at no door, so it never reaches the workflow read. What stays open is the genuinely workflow-less area — a pinned area whose `workflow` file is absent — and **driven** at this task, on a minted `single-task` area with that one file removed: `jigc task bind decision adr:x <id>` answers *task at "/private/var/…/repo/.jigc/tasks/probe-bind" has no recorded workflow — discard it and re-start with `jigc start`*, exit 1, **code-less** (a bare `anyhow` through `cli::task`'s `workflow_def`) **and host-absolute**, the area rendered with `{:?}`; `jigc doc create adr --task <id>` answers *the active task has no recorded workflow — …*, exit 1, code-less, flattened to `{"error": …}` on `--format json`, with no path. The producer there is `cli::doc`'s `workflow_gate`, reached by `run_create`, `run_author` and the copy-in role binding — **the create-gate doors**, not every `doc` write verb: `jigc doc set-slot --task <id>` over the same area lands at **exit 0**, so the Settle's broader phrasing is struck with that datum. Both are the route-floor and law-1 classes this repo has swept repeatedly; neither loses a byte. *What is owed:* an identity at both producers, and `task bind`'s path through `render::repo_relative`. *Trigger:* the charter's — the first adopter finding on that surface, or the wave that next touches its producer.

- **(D) (d) `free_displacement_path`'s suffix walk is an unbounded loop.** The parking home's no-clobber walk tries `<name>.2`, `.3`, … in a `loop` with no ceiling (`crates/cli/src/task.rs`), so a pathological `.jigc/displaced/` tree hangs the displacement rather than refusing it. **READ at the baseline, not driven** ([baseline-a3-2-failed-displacement.md](../completions/artifacts/M53/baseline-a3-2-failed-displacement.md) → the no-clobber row), and Increment 2 left it untouched by declared bound — the increment's own subject is the **removal**, and a ceiling is a condition on a different guard. It loses nothing: the loop's whole job is to avoid overwriting. *What is owed:* a ceiling, and the refusal it hands back when it is reached. *Trigger:* the charter's — the first adopter finding on that surface, or the wave that next touches its producer.

- **(D) (e) `mint_task` writes the base pin non-atomically where `mint_milestone` persists it.** `mint_task` writes `base.json` with a plain `std::fs::write`; `mint_milestone` writes the same pin through the atomic `crate::state::persist`. Increment 3's residual rule keys on the pin's **existence** (`symlink_metadata(<area>/base.json).is_file()`) and is therefore unaffected — which is exactly why the asymmetry survives this pass rather than being closed by it — but a predicate keyed on the pin's **parse** has a torn-read window at the task door and none at the milestone door. **READ, not driven**, and no racer is driven either: [storage.md](../design/storage.md) → *Concurrent writers* names the hook as the designed racer, and no hook runs inside `mint_task`. *What is owed:* the two mint doors writing the same file the same way. *Trigger:* the charter's — the first adopter finding on that surface, or the wave that next touches its producer, which here is the first predicate that **parses** the base pin rather than asking whether it exists.

- **(D) (f) The M52 review's R-I row asserts its loss with evidence that cannot establish it.** In this harness `grep` is a shell function wrapping `ugrep --ignore-files`, which honours `.gitignore`, and `.jigc/` is gitignored whole — so `grep -rl <marker> .` answers *not found* under `.jigc/` whether the bytes are there or not. `completions/artifacts/M52/per-axis-review/axis-3.md` §3 R-I carries three `grep -rl '<marker>' .` lines as its **working-tree-absence** evidence, so the leg that says *the bytes are gone from disk* does not establish it — the row's other leg (`git grep -l '<marker>' HEAD`) is sound, and answers a different question: not committed, which was never in doubt. The verdict stands: two M53 auditors hit the same instrument independently and re-proved every loss with `command grep` plus a before-control, `git log -S` and `git fsck` ([baseline-ledger](../completions/artifacts/M53/baseline-ledger.md) → *An instrument finding, recorded once so nobody repeats it*), and the re-review's drivers are told to drive it that way. What is owed is the **recorded evidence**, not the finding — a repro block nobody can re-run is how a true verdict becomes unfalsifiable. *What is owed:* R-I's repro block re-driven and replaced in place, with the instrument note beside it. *Trigger:* the charter's — the first adopter finding on that surface, or the wave that next touches its producer, which here is the next act that cites axis-3 §3 R-I as evidence; the partial re-review carries the instrument note, a later reader does not.

### Deferred during the M53 build — each with a written trigger

- **(D) Whether `InProgress::SquashMerge` should name `git commit` as its concluding command.** M53 Increment 4 minted `InProgress::UncommittedCherryPick`, whose route **names `git commit`** as the way to conclude the pick (git prefills the message from `MERGE_MSG`). That falsified the stated reason the squash merge names **no** concluding command — *"a squash merge is concluded by the user's own `git commit`"* — which, from the moment a sibling member spells that command, explains nothing about why one row spells it and the other withholds it. The clause is **struck** where it was written ([finalize.md](../design/finalize.md) → 1. Preflight; `cli::repo::InProgress::conclude`'s doc-comment), and the **row is kept as shipped**: `git merge --squash`'s route is a shipped user-facing line, and rewording one is a behaviour change D4 did not decide — the increment's own acceptance pins the five conclude-bearing members' bytes for exactly that reason. *What is owed:* decide whether the squash merge names `git commit` with a qualifier of its own (git prefills from `SQUASH_MSG`, so the same claim is available) or keeps its silence for a reason that actually distinguishes it. *Trigger:* **the next task that changes any member's route text** — the qualifiers now live per member, so that task is already re-reading this exact table and is the cheapest place to settle it; failing that, the next posture-family wave.

- **(D) `reseed_sub_task_areas` skips on `exists()`, not on the pin — Increment 3's declared bound.** Increment 3's rule is *a task is an area that carries its base pin*, and the record-driven re-seed is the one site that still asks a different question: it skips any sub-task whose area **directory exists** ([`engine::milestone::reseed_sub_task_areas`](../crates/engine/src/milestone.rs)), so a **pin-less residual** at a sub-task id is never rebuilt. Under an open milestone that sub-task is a local dead end — after Increment 3 no roster lists it, no by-id door resolves it, and no jigc verb clears it — until the named path is removed by hand, after which the next operating milestone op re-seeds it and the state heals. Nothing is destroyed and nothing is claimed falsely; the cost is that the recovery is a human act the surfaces name rather than a verb. *What is owed:* decide whether the re-seed adopts the residual rule and rebuilds **over** a pin-less directory — which mints into a directory holding bytes jigc did not write, the class `unwind_area` exists to close — or keeps `exists()` and says so where the dead end is reached. *Trigger:* **the next task that changes the re-seed's skip predicate or `MINT_DOORS`' `Exempt` rows** — that task is already holding both halves of this question; failing that, the first field report of a sub-task that will not resume on a fresh clone.

- **(D) `staged_task_prose` enumerates `.jigc/tasks/` itself, so a residual still reads as a task there — Increment 3's declared bound.** The staged-prose guard behind the three `*.staged-prose` refusals reads the tasks root with its own `read_dir` and a `path.is_dir()` test ([`cli::task::staged_task_prose`](../crates/cli/src/task.rs)) rather than asking the shared base-pin predicate, so a residual directory holding a `docs/<ty>:<slug>.md` is still named as a task carrying staged prose at `jigc uninstall`, `jigc task discard` and `jigc milestone discard`. It fails in the safe direction by construction — the guard's whole job is to refuse rather than destroy, and it refuses over bytes it cannot prove disposable — and it is the one surface left where a residual reads as a task after Increment 3. *What is owed:* decide whether the guard's subject becomes the base-pin predicate, which would reclassify a residual's staged-looking bytes from *staged prose* to **foreign**, routing them to the door's `foreign-bytes` identity and its `--force` consent instead of the `*.staged-prose` one. *Trigger:* **the next task that changes `staged_task_prose`'s subject or any `*.staged-prose` refusal text**; failing that, the next wave that takes the residual rule to the consent guards.

- **(D) `finalize.foreign-bytes` is stderr-only at `jigc milestone finalize` until 2.0 — Increment 2's declared bound.** The pass's one new code rides the `findings` array at `jigc task finalize`, whose landed arm is pinned at `Object(&["committed", "findings", "schema_version"])`, and is **stderr-only** at `jigc milestone finalize`, whose landed arm is pinned at `Object(&["committed"])`: the pre-1.0 additive-key window closed at M48, so growing a `findings` key on that arm is a **shape** change, not an addition. The identity is still recorded in the invocation log, and the advisory's safety does not rest on being heard — an area left standing is inert at every door. The asymmetry is **stated once**, in the contract's `finalize.*` sub-table ([command-output-contract.md](../design/command-output-contract.md) → the family is not only a *blocked*-finalize family), and cross-referenced here rather than restated. *What is owed:* the key, so a driver reads the advisory off the same array at both doors. *Trigger:* **the next contract act that moves a landed arm's declared key set** — i.e. 2.0, which is the one vehicle a pinned arm's shape change can ride; the evolution policy M51 Increment 5 wrote is where that act is governed.

*(**The closure check for both M53 sections, stated and re-runnable** — it fences the **form** only, on the M52 check's mold and for its reason: that every row under **either** M53 heading carries a `*Trigger:*` clause, that the Settle section carries all six of the baseline's rows, and that the three residuals no increment landed are named **by symbol** rather than gestured at. No mechanical checker can say whether a trigger is *live* or whether a row still belongs here; that is the re-read a later charter does by reading. Run from the repo root; it prints one line per row, one per named row or residual, and exits non-zero on any row that carries no trigger or any name that has gone missing.)*

```sh
awk '
  BEGIN {
    n = 9
    tok[1] = "add-item";               why[1] = "1.x"
    tok[2] = "create.empty-title";     why[2] = "1.x"
    tok[3] = "no recorded workflow";   why[3] = "1.x"
    tok[4] = "free_displacement_path"; why[4] = "1.x"
    tok[5] = "mint_task";              why[5] = "1.x"
    tok[6] = "grep -rl";               why[6] = "1.x"
    tok[7] = "reseed_sub_task_areas";  why[7] = "build"
    tok[8] = "staged_task_prose";      why[8] = "build"
    tok[9] = "finalize.foreign-bytes"; why[9] = "build"
  }
  /^### Deferred at the M53 Settle/    { s = "ledger"; seen_ledger = 1; next }
  /^### Deferred during the M53 build/ { s = "build";  seen_build  = 1; next }
  /^### /                              { s = ""; next }
  s != "" && /^- / {
    rows[s]++
    trg = ($0 ~ /\*Trigger:\*/)
    printf "%-6s row %d  %s\n", s, rows[s], trg ? "trigger" : "!! no *Trigger:* clause"
    if (!trg) bad++
    for (i = 1; i <= n; i++) if (index($0, tok[i])) hit[i] = 1
  }
  END {
    if (!seen_ledger) { print "!! no `### Deferred at the M53 Settle` heading"; bad++ }
    if (!seen_build)  { print "!! no `### Deferred during the M53 build` heading"; bad++ }
    if (rows["ledger"] < 6) { print "!! the Settle section carries fewer than six rows"; bad++ }
    for (i = 1; i <= n; i++) {
      printf "%-6s %-24s %s\n", why[i], tok[i], hit[i] ? "named" : "!! ABSENT"
      if (!hit[i]) bad++
    }
    exit (bad > 0)
  }
' implementation/decisions-pending.md
```

### The rc.14 trial's findings — owed dispositions

*(2026-09-10: F-5, F-9, F-11 are absorbed into M51 — see the M51 section; F-3, F-8, F-10, F-13 stay on their triggers.)*

*(**Re-read and closed 2026-09-16 — M51 Increment 11, T4.** Every row below now opens with its **finding id** and carries either an absorption bracket naming the **M51 increment and task** it landed in or a **live** `*Trigger:*`; a trigger this wave fired is recorded as fired rather than left standing, and a trigger this wave *did not* fire says so with what it re-read. **The four rc.14 rows this section does not carry** are disposed in one home and not restated here ([findings-verification.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-rc14/findings-verification.md)): **F-1** and **F-12** are REFUTED, **F-6** is carried below as **N27**, and **F-7** is carried below as **N15** — **fixed** by this wave at M51 Increment 5 / T6, and its row says so.)*

*(Opened 2026-09-10 at the close of [the trial that follows M50](../completions/artifacts/RC-rc14/trial-record.md).
Every row is driven with a repro block in
[findings-verification.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-rc14/findings-verification.md); this entry
exists because **a finding left in a trial record has no trigger and will be forgotten** —
[CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md)'s own rule, and the reason N31 sat discharged-but-listed for two days.
The **headline is not here**: the duress cell landed **3/3 against the 1/3 it had to beat**, and
its reading is the human's, beside the 1.0.0 call.)*

*(**The closure check, stated and re-runnable** — it fences the **form** only: that every row is keyed by a finding id and carries a disposition. No mechanical checker can say whether a trigger is *live*, which is why the re-read above is written out per row and verified by reading. Run from the repo root; it prints one line per id and exits non-zero on any row that carries neither.)*

```sh
awk '
  /^### The rc\.14 trial/ { s = 1; next }
  /^### / { s = 0 }
  s && /^- / {
    abs = ($0 ~ /absorbed into M51/); inc = ($0 ~ /M51 Increment [0-9]+/); trg = ($0 ~ /\*Trigger:\*/)
    if (abs) d = inc ? (trg ? "absorbed+trigger" : "absorbed") : "!! absorption bracket names no increment"
    else d = trg ? "trigger" : "!! no disposition"
    if (d ~ /^!!/) bad++
    t = $0; n = 0
    while (match(t, /F-[0-9]+/)) {
      id = substr(t, RSTART, RLENGTH)
      if (!(id in seen)) { seen[id] = 1; printf "%-7s %s\n", id, d }
      t = substr(t, RSTART + RLENGTH); n++
    }
    if (n == 0) printf "%-7s %s\n", "(no id)", d
  }
  END { exit (bad > 0) }
' implementation/decisions-pending.md
```

- *(**F-2 · F-9 · F-10.** **F-9 landed 2026-09-16 — M51 Increment 11**, T1 (`engine::state::RenameRecord`: the pre-rename `# H1` becomes durable task state at `.jigc/tasks/<id>/renames.json`, keyed on the heading moving and never on the re-slug) + T2 (`commit-recording.stale-title`, one shared predicate with two producers — `jigc doc rename`'s ack carries it at the invocation that makes the summary stale, and a post-pass in `TaskArea::validate` re-raises it until the summary is repaired), pinned by `doc_rename_in_task::the_stale_commit_summary_is_noticed_where_it_happens_and_re_raised_until_repaired`. **The trigger below fired on its FIRST clause only** — this wave opened `doc rename`'s ack — and its **second clause did not fire**: no second trial arm has been observed leaving the adapter to repair a commit. **That second clause is the residue's live trigger**, and **F-10** (no verb amends a landed finalize; excluded at the 2026-09-11 Settle as a new amend verb) stays owed on it. **F-2** (the carryover gate's prose implies a foreignness notion that does not exist, and `task validate` is silent about content staged *during* the task while `--dry-run` and the ack both name it) stays owed on it too and takes a **second live clause**: the next wave that opens the carryover gate's step prose or `preview_gates`' membership — the seam M51 Increment 5 made the single source of the `--dry-run` forecast (EC-20), and the one that would decide whether this is previewed at all.)* *(absorbed into M51 — the Settle's scope batch, as a **fired trigger**, 2026-09-11: F-9 is in — this wave opens both `doc rename`'s ack and `finalize`'s pre-commit manifest. F-10 stays parked on its own trigger beside it.)* **(D) The one adapter bypass, and it is a chain of three — the trial's only finding about what jigc *does* rather than what it says.** Raised by the human on reading the record, after an earlier draft understated it as *"commit surgery on the workspace"*; driven, the commit B1 rewrote **contained a managed doc**, so that doc's committed state was produced by raw `git commit`. **Nothing was corrupted** — the doc's bytes are byte-identical across jigc's commit and the manual one, and the file-state baseline is **content-keyed rather than sha-keyed**, so `jigc validate` reading clean afterwards was right rather than lucky. The chain: **F-2** finalize commits the index, so two paths the worker restaged mid-task were swept in (the `--dry-run` manifest and the ack both name them; `task validate` does not) → **F-9** `jigc doc rename` re-slugs the doc and says nothing about the staged commit doc whose summary still names the **old** title, `task validate` says nothing, and `--dry-run` prints the stale subject and the new path on **adjacent lines** without connecting them → **F-10** none of the 17 top-level verbs amends a landed commit, and a successful `finalize` closes the task, so raw git is the only recovery. **F-9 is the cheapest link to cut and is not a determinism-boundary violation:** the re-slug is a structural op jigc owns, it knows both titles and knows the task stages a commit doc, and *noticing* is not *rewriting* authored prose. *Trigger:* the next wave that opens `doc rename`'s ack or `finalize`'s pre-commit manifest — **or** a second trial arm observed leaving the adapter to repair a commit, whichever comes first.
- *(**F-5.** **Landed 2026-09-16 — M51 Increment 6**, T1 (the task family's unknown-id refusal becomes a typed `Finding` carrying the engine's shipped `finalize.no-task`) + T2 (the milestone family joins it on its own shipped `milestone.unknown`, one condition and one answer across the family), so **every** `WORK_UNIT_ID_DOORS` row now answers the unknown-id cell with the declared findings envelope and a resolving `(code, target)` key where 22 answered with none; pinned by `work_unit_unknown_envelope::every_work_unit_id_door_answers_the_findings_envelope_for_an_unknown_id`, which closes on a **set equality** against the registry, so a row driven twice while another is missed is red. **Trigger recorded FIRED** — the wave opened `work-unit.malformed-id`'s producers, which is the clause below verbatim.)* *(absorbed into M51 — D7, 2026-09-11: the declared contract is made true at the 22 doors through the shared home, on the engine's shipped `finalize.no-task` and M50 Increment 9's typed `Result<_, Finding>`; `command-output-contract.md:202`'s false sentence moves with it.)* **(D) The unknown-id column: 22 doors refuse correctly, with a route, and no code.** Walk arm 17 enumerates 25 id-taking doors × {`""`, `no-such-task`} and splits clean on rc.14: the **empty** column is **53/53 green** (`work-unit.malformed-id` + route — M50's centrepiece landing exactly where it was aimed), and **every one of the 22 failures is the `[nonexistent]` sibling**, which answers `exit 1 NOCODE route`. **Driven on both binaries and identical**, so not a regression, and the ledger declares the cell unchanged. But a driver keying on the stable `(code, target)` pair gets nothing when it names a task that does not exist, at 22 doors — and this is the **complete-fix lens on M50's own work for the fifth consecutive wave**, found by the same arm that found RC-m50's blocking finding, the same way. *Trigger:* the next wave that opens `work-unit.malformed-id`'s producers — the guard and its sibling are one `if` apart.
- *(**F-3.** **Excluded 2026-09-11 at the M51 Settle on the *necessity* leg** — D13; the M51 section above is the record and is not restated here. **Re-read 2026-09-16 (M51 Increment 11, T4): the trigger's SECOND clause fired and did not discriminate.** M51 Increment 8 opened `schema-conformance` — it minted `schema-conformance.orphaned-instance` and `schema-conformance.unversioned-doctype` — and this row was still excluded, because F-3's cost sits in the schema **format** (a conditional ref requirement plus a `doc schema` `contract-version` 6→7) and not in that family's code set. So *"or a wave opening `schema-conformance`"* is **struck with its falsifying datum**: it fires on any new code in the family and says nothing about the cost. The row stands on its **first** clause, which is the necessity-bearing one and is **live** — the first doctype that needs a conditional field requirement.)* **(D) `status: superseded` with no `supersedes` target validates clean at every door.** Driven: `task validate` shows only the routine staged-copy advisory, `jigc validate` says *no findings — the committed store validates clean* at exit 0, finalize exits 0, and the store still reads clean after the commit. `superseded` is a valid enum member, so schema-conformance says nothing. **Not a false green about anything jigc claims** — the determinism boundary gives it structure and leaves prose to the LLM — but the structural half *is* expressible and undeclared: **a conditional ref requirement** (`status == superseded` ⇒ `supersedes` present) that no doctype states. B2 put the consequence better than the finding does: *"a green validate reads as 'correct,' not just 'well-formed.'"* *Trigger:* the first doctype that needs a conditional field requirement, or a wave opening `schema-conformance`.
- *(**F-4.** **Re-read 2026-09-16 (M51 Increment 11, T4): NOT fired, and the near-miss is named.** This wave opened `jigc doc rename`'s **ack** (Increment 11 / T2) and gave top-level `jigc rename` a `--slug` name-ceiling refusal (Increment 9 / T3) — but it opened **neither verb's help** and **none of the orientation view's routes**. The four help texts it did mint are `jigc validate`'s, `jigc doc show`'s, `migrate-corpus`'s and `jigc task finalize`'s (Increment 9). The trigger below therefore stands **live** and unamended.)* **(D) `jigc doc rename` vs `jigc rename` costs every worker 2–3 help reads — the trial's most-corroborated finding.** Raised independently by **both** interactive arms in their debriefs, and corroborated by the invocation logs rather than resting on them: **four of five arms** read 2–3 `--help` outputs immediately before renaming. **B3-h2 is the sharpest cell — it ran the wrong verb first** (top-level `jigc rename` on a staged doc, **exit 2**) and only then reached `doc rename`. The refusal routed it correctly, which is the surface contract working; what no surface did was prevent the wrong call. Neither verb's existence is surfaced where the other is used. *Trigger:* the next wave that opens either verb's help or the orientation view's routes.
- *(**F-8.** **Excluded 2026-09-11 at the M51 Settle on the *necessity* leg** — D13, which also flags it as the natural **first post-1.0 migration**, an `AddedOptionalField` stamp-only `adr` 2→3 bump; the M51 section above is the record. **Re-read 2026-09-16 (M51 Increment 11, T4): NOT fired** — no increment of this wave needed a cross-doctype ref, and the wave moved **zero** `schema-hash`es by its own declared bound. The trigger below stands **live** and unamended.)* **(D) No structural ref from an `adr` to a `research` doc.** `doc schema adr` offers `supersedes` (ref → `adr`) and `cites-code` (code-anchor → source) and nothing that points at another *doctype*. B2 authored the research doc and the ADR it informed, and could only link them **in prose** — *"a soft, unenforced link instead of a structural one that jigc could validate or that a rename/migrate would keep pointed correctly."* Note the second clause: an unstructured link is exactly what `jigc rename` cannot repoint. Capability gap. *Trigger:* whichever wave first needs a cross-doctype ref; the design-altitude pair (`vision —grounded-in→ research`) is the shipped precedent.
- *(**F-11 · F-13.** **F-11 landed 2026-09-16 — M51 Increment 6**, T3: `parse_author_payload` returns `Result<AuthorPlan, Finding>` and **all four** of its refusal sites answer with a severity, a code (`write.wrong-shape` for a payload the grammar does not admit, `write.malformed-value` for a value whose `<<…>>` form contradicts its declared leaf), an `at:` and a route — on text and in the declared findings envelope — pinned by `author_payload_floor::{every_payload_refusal_answers_with_a_severity_a_code_an_at_and_a_route, …_emits_the_findings_envelope_with_a_resolving_key}`, whose site set is `PayloadReject::ALL`, closed both ways. **F-11's half of the trigger below is recorded FIRED.** **F-13**'s half — whichever wave first takes the sequential-execution question — is **re-read 2026-09-16 and NOT fired**; it stands **live**, and it is a design question, not a fix.)* *(absorbed into M51 — D7, 2026-09-11: F-11 takes a code and a route inside the flattened string now, and its **envelope** disposition is one row of D5's pinned-envelope registry. F-13 stays post-1.0 on its own trigger — a design question, not a fix.)* **(D) Two un-swept siblings of M50's own sweeps, and one capability gap the milestone arm hit.** **F-11** — the `doc author` **payload parse** error answers *malformed `doc author` payload: unknown field `status`, expected `title` or `sections`* with **no severity, no code, no `at:`, no route and no footer**, while the same door answers a declared-address miss with `blocking · write.unknown-field` + `at:` + a `jigc doc schema adr` route. M50 Increment 9 closed the write-miss floor **at the four target resolvers**; the payload parse sits upstream of every one of them. It cost a real worker a round trip in R4. **F-13** — a milestone offers **one execution shape**: B4-h planned a three-increment *dependent* spine, read four `--help` outputs, correctly judged that the fan-out's isolation *“would silently drop each increment's dependency on the last”*, and moved to abandon the milestone it had just built. That is also **why the `1799a2d` boundary tightening was reached by nothing** — the uncovered arm and the gap are one event. *Trigger:* F-11 — the next wave opening `doc author`'s payload boundary or `write.unknown-*`'s producers; F-13 — whichever wave first takes the sequential-execution question, and it is a **design** question, not a fix.
- **(D) The apparatus had no write channel, and the fix is landed.** The bypass above was invisible to `run.py observe`: the reader had a FILESYSTEM channel for **reads** and nothing for **writes**, so a `git reset` + manual `git commit` over a managed doc scored clean. It surfaced only because the worker volunteered it in its debrief and a human then read the record — **the second time this apparatus has learned the same lesson on the same axis**, after `AFindPipedIntoCatIsARead` was added because a duress *read* was found from a debrief rather than by the reader. Reads were fenced; writes were not; that is the incomplete-fix shape, in the instrument. **Fixed 2026-09-10 in the same motion as the finding**: `observe.commit_writes()` (transcript-side, heuristic, evidence-for-review like reads) plus `observe.history_surgery()` (**corpus-side reflog, exact** — it does not depend on the worker's account of itself), both walked over the subagent set too, surfaced in the row, and fenced by `test_observe.py::ACommitMadeOutsideJigcIsSeen` **against B1's archived transcript** rather than a mock. Verified: it catches both acts on the real case, stays silent on `jigc task finalize`, stays silent on `git add`/`restore`/`status`/`log`, and reports nothing on the four clean arms or the archive. *Trigger:* none — closed; recorded because the **lesson** is not: a channel fenced on one direction of an axis is fenced on none of it.

### The trial that follows M50 — protocol inputs

*(**Handover written 2026-09-09**: [completions/artifacts/RC-rc14/handover.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-rc14/handover.md) — state of the world, the binary's sha256 and the proof it is HEAD's tree, the three owed apparatus items, the traps, and the declared bounds. The behaviour-change list below is its one home and the handover points at it rather than copying it. The trial is **not** this session's to run: the session that planned, built and audited the wave is a contaminant, and the repo's pattern is a separate session against a pre-registered protocol.)*

*(Opened 2026-09-04 at the close of [the pre-v1 trial](../completions/artifacts/RC-m50/trial-record.md). The next trial is the one that re-measures the duress cell after M50 acts on it, and it runs on the binary M50 ships. Written as triggers because an apparatus item left in a handover's prose has no trigger.)*

- **(D) The duress cell is the headline again, by the human's decision, and the reading it must beat is 1/3.** RC-m50 landed 1 VERB / 2 FILESYSTEM across two transports on plant E, read as a pull-tier finding on state and routed into M50 (F-5 + fork 2). The re-measure keeps the same plant, the same N=3-across-two-transports shape, and compares against **1/3**, not against the fence's read-back series (which has stood at 100 % effective for five trials and buys nothing more). *Trigger:* drafting the next trial's protocol — the instrument list is decided there.
- **(D) The reader walks subagent transcripts.** `observe` reads the largest `.jsonl` under `.session-transcript/` — the main session. B2's two subagents read nothing managed, but a worker that delegates its orientation would move the FILESYSTEM channel into files the reader never opens. *Trigger:* drafting the next trial's protocol — before any figure is read; the fix is small and belongs in `driver/observe.py` with a test over a synthetic `subagents/` tree.
- **(D) `observe` over a refused out-dir says whose evidence it is reading, or refuses (I-2).** `run-session.sh` refuses a pre-existing out-dir correctly; the reader then scores the old trial's evidence at that path without a word. *Trigger:* the next trial's apparatus rehearsal — the fix is a `PROVENANCE.txt` image/sha check against the round's gate record.
- **(D) The corpus template's `IngestQueue` (PT-D, third trial running).** `push()` is called on no live path; four workers found it and two put it in a deferral ledger. Wire `tick()` into a live path, add the `check-corpus.sh` bar that fails when a symbol a plant depends on is unreachable. *Trigger:* before the next trial's corpora are instantiated — a plant whose subject is dead code is a plant whose falsifier is *"the worker fixes the code instead"*.
- **(D) M50's declared behaviour changes are briefed before the first session — the list itself, derived at the close (2026-09-08) from the increment records rather than from memory, and *completed 2026-09-09* when the trial that consults it found it five sources short (see the sub-list at the end).** Every item below is met by an arm, most of them within minutes, and an unbriefed observer scores one as a regression (RC-1.0-final §0's near-miss, twice over). Each names the increment that landed it; the reasoning is in that increment's [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) entry and is not restated here.
  - **Met on every arm, within minutes.**
    - **A degenerate work-unit id is refused at all 25 doors that take one** (Inc 1) — one blocking `work-unit.malformed-id`, `engine::slug::is_slug` as the predicate, a route stating the grammar. **Two exit-0 → non-zero flips are intended and declared**: `jigc task validate ""` answered *the task validates clean* and `jigc doc list --task ""` answered *no docs staged* at exit 0 through rc.13; both now block. **Five divergent milestone-door texts collapse to one.** A *well-formed but unknown* id keeps its unchanged roster answer, so only the malformed cell moves.
    - **`jigc start` stops calling a repo with a live task clean** (Inc 5) — `engine::result::OrientationView` gains a third variant, `active-task`, collapsing [bootstrap.md](../design/bootstrap.md)'s states 3 and 4, so `"state": "clean"` over a repo holding a task is gone. **`engine::result::SCHEMA_VERSION` goes 2 → 3** — the wave's only result-contract bump, and the one adopter-shaped consumer a trial can contain is a driver pinned to 2.
    - **Both minting `start` forms name the work already open** (Inc 5) — `jigc start "<intent>"` and `jigc start --workflow <X> "<intent>"` append an **`also open:`** block, one row per open task with its id, its minting workflow and the resume/validate/finalize/discard directives. This is F-5's push half; the duress cell's re-measure meets it directly.
    - **Orientation now does real work** (Inc 5) — the active-task view's `findings` key runs the task-scope sweep, which shells out to git, spawns the `doc-code` probe and materializes `.jigc/index/edges.json`, at the door the adapter binds to `SessionStart`. A session's very first `jigc start` therefore writes.
    - **Every text-rendered finding carries `severity · code — message`** (Inc 10). Four surfaces — `describe`, `doc`, `start`, `task` — printed the bare message before, so text a worker or a scraper matched on has changed shape.
  - **Met on the arm that reaches it.**
    - **A caller-typed address whose `<slug>` head is not a slug is refused** at the nine `DoctypeArg::Address` doors — `store.malformed-slug`, routed at `jigc doc list` (Inc 2). `jigc doc show 'research:../../outside'` no longer serves bytes from outside the repository through the pinned JSON at exit 0. `jigc rename --slug` refuses with **`write.malformed-slug`**, and `jigc rename` onto an untrackable destination with **`write.untrackable-destination`** in place of a code-less bail that printed the host's filesystem.
    - **An unmappable write address answers with a code and a route** (Inc 9) — the item, field and slot resolvers returned a bare, code-less `{"error": "no field addressed by …"}` envelope for every miss shape except the undeclared section; each one now adjudicates, so a mis-addressed `jigc doc set-field` / `set-slot` / `add-item` / `remove-item` / `retitle-item` is a keyed finding a driver can act on.
    - **`jigc task discard` refuses over staged prose, and `--force` is the single consent** (Inc 3) — a door that always succeeded now blocks. Plant E's arm meets it.
    - **The Claude Code profile's `deny` floor gains `Bash(jigc uninstall:*)` and `Bash(jigc milestone discard:*)`** (Inc 3). A worker's call to either is blocked **by the harness**, so the transcript shows a permission denial and no jigc output at all — an observer counting denials must not score these two as friction, and a walk arm that needs either must run it outside the agent.
    - **Both root knobs refuse jigc's own workbench and an undescribable root** — `config.workbench-root` (`docs-root .jigc` / `placement-root .jigc`) and `config.unusable-root` (file-shaped, and the two siblings) — and **`jigc uninstall` gains a third subject**, blocking `uninstall.untracked-workbench-file` over a `.jigc/` path outside the transient prefixes that no index carries (Inc 4).
    - **`jigc doc schema --format json` goes `contract-version` 5 → 6**, a `ref` field carrying its **`to:`** target, mirrored as `ref -> <to>` in the plain listing (Inc 8); a hand-authored pack whose `ref` lacks a resolvable `to:` now fails **pack-load**, where it loaded clean at exit 0 before.
    - **`migrate-corpus` classifies a nested-repeatable leaf edit at its own locus** instead of answering `migrate-corpus.unclassified-change` with a route into this workspace (Inc 6/7), the three byte-writing arms fold there, and a finding raised inside a nested item composes the full hop chain (Inc 7). The `changelog` corpus is where an arm meets this.
    - **`jigc milestone join` states its verdict as the headline when blocked** instead of opening with a completed-join line (Inc 10); **the landing ack names every commit the boundary made** (Inc 11); and the Fix phase is a **fan-out** over a new composed workflow **`fix-task`** — a catalog member `jigc describe` did not list before (Inc 11).
    - **A printed finding path is repo-relative**, and every absolute one that stays says why (Inc 12); a **non-directory leftover** is answered honestly at every destroying door; **`jigc setup` says what the next door will refuse** while still refusing nothing; a missing code anchor **names the grammar** instead of asserting an absence; a pack is **named one way** on every surface that names one; and `jigc task finalize <id> --dry-run` gains a **`subject`** key forecasting the composed commit subject.
    - **`jigc milestone finalize` gates each sub-task's transient `commit` doc as hard as `jigc task finalize` does** (`1799a2d`, landed before the wave) — **a fan-out that landed clean on rc.13 may now block at exit 3**. It is the intended tightening, and it is the item most likely to be met late in a milestone arm, where it costs the most to misread.
  - **Completed 2026-09-09 — five sources the derivation above could not have covered, because it ran before them.** The header pinned itself to *the twelve increment records* at the close (2026-09-08) while the wave shipped **thirteen** increments and the completion audit ran on **2026-09-09**. Found by the session executing the trial, at the moment the list was consulted — which is the third consecutive time this ledger has been one or more short at exactly that moment, and the reason the count is now stated with its derivation date.
    - **Increment 13, the close.** `QUICKSTART.md`'s discard paragraph and `MIGRATING.md`'s back-out ladder both stated `jigc task discard` as unconditionally safe; both now name the refusal by the identity the door prints (`task-discard.staged-prose`) and `--force` as its single consent — plus the fact a reader meets first: **minting a task stages its commit doc, so an ordinary discard hits the refusal from the moment the task exists**. Both guides are `include_str!`'d into `.claude/skills/jigc/SKILL.md`, so **shipped bytes moved and `jigc-body-blake3` moved**: re-running `jigc setup` over an rc.13-installed, *locally edited* SKILL.md now takes M48's `adapter-guide.user-modified` refuse-to-clobber path.
    - **Audit F1 (HIGH).** `jigc milestone add-from-spec` read a doc from **outside the repository** at exit 0, seeded a sub-task from it and committed naming the foreign source. **It now refuses** — a door that succeeded now blocks, met on any milestone or spec arm. Its declared bound rides with it: `ArgToken::Plain` is unchecked from the outside.
    - **Audit F2 (MEDIUM).** The finding identity was swept over **31 production sites**: `config.*`, `pack.resource-missing`, `workflow-refs.*` and all 11 `rename::RefusalKind` refusals now name themselves in the **invocation log**, where ~29 doors dropped their code before. **This is the trial's own measurement channel** — a comparison of rc.13-vs-rc.14 *log contents* is not like-for-like, while the argv-keyed channels are unaffected.
    - **Audit F3 (LOW).** Law 1 widened from the destroying/provisioning doors to the shared predicate **plus 4 read-path sites** (`store.not-found` *and* `store.unparseable`, both `engine::store` arms plus `engine::milestone`'s copies), so text **and** JSON bytes on **read** doors moved. **This is also N31's discharge** — see the strike below.
    - **Audit F4 (LOW).** `jigc milestone discard` destroyed a sub-task's staged prose at exit 0; it now blocks with `milestone.staged-prose`. Exactly one cell of the driven 2×2 moved. **Brief it together with the deny floor** (`Bash(jigc milestone discard:*)`), or an arm mis-scores twice over: a harness permission denial read as friction, and the new refusal never observed because only an out-of-agent run reaches it.

  *Trigger:* drafting the next trial's protocol — before the first blind session is briefed.

### The trial that follows M46 — protocol inputs

*(Opened 2026-08-19 at M46 Increment 3. The next trial is the last instrument before the **1.0.0 call** (the human's), and it runs with the cue card replaced — the redesign and the rule it yields live in [cue-card-postmortem.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-1.0-gate/cue-card-postmortem.md), not here. This entry is where a **deliberate behaviour change** lands so the protocol reads it as designed rather than meeting it blind and scoring it a regression.)*

- **(D) A blind session can be driven headless without moving the channel — measured, and it makes an instrument available that the trial has never had (2026-08-25).** Increment 0 of [completions/trial-driver/](../completions/trial-driver/) ran the B3 prompt three ways on `jigc-gate:rc11`: the archive supplies the interactive baseline (stonefly **6** VERB, rosewater **7**), and a headless `claude -p` arm on the same image and prompt scored **6**, completing the whole arc — four pieces, six finalize commits, 39/39 tests, **0** denials. **The channel does not invert.** The paired result is the one the protocol should act on: the wave planned this on `knowledge/17`'s claim that `-p` ignores project allow rules, which would have made `--strict-permissions` *misrepresent* the adopter's asymmetry. **That is false on this build** — all 13 jigc invocations executed under `--permission-mode default` and all 7 permission denials were file writes, so `jigc setup`'s `Bash(jigc:*)` allowlist is honoured. **`--strict-permissions` headless therefore reproduces the adopter's real condition**, which protocol §9 has carried as a declared directional confound since 2026-08-16 and which no trial has been able to run unattended. Record + evidence: [increment-0.md](../completions/trial-driver/increment-0.md). *Trigger:* drafting the next trial's protocol — the instrument list is decided there, not here.

- **(Q) — ✅ SETTLED 2026-08-28 in [the RC-1.0-final protocol](../completions/artifacts/RC-1.0-final/protocol.md) §3.4, settlement 1: VERB keeps counting *attempts*, and `VERB-effective` ships beside it, never folded in — with `attempts > 0 / effective == 0` pre-registered as a *finding* (the worker chose the channel and the channel refused it), and every prior trial's figure relabelled rather than reinterpreted.** The question as it stood: **does §3.3's VERB count *attempts* or *successful reads*? The registered wording says attempts, and the archive contains both (2026-08-25).** §3.3 registers VERB as *"`jigc doc show … --task …` **appears in the invocation log**"*, and the 1.0.0-gate record counts it that way — but **rosewater's 7 contains two calls that exited 1**, so only **5** returned document bytes (b3-bypass carries the same shape). A failed read is not a read-back, and the wave's claim is about read-back. The driver deliberately **does not resolve this**: it reproduces the registered count *and* carries `verb_succeeded` / `failed_reads` beside it, on the same reasoning as the row below — a reader that quietly redefines a registered measurement is the failure the apparatus exists to prevent. **The protocol owns the choice**, and whichever way it goes, the prior trials' figures are attempts and should be relabelled rather than silently reinterpreted. *Trigger:* drafting the next trial's protocol — before any figure is compared across trials.

- **(Q) — ✅ SETTLED 2026-08-28 in [the RC-1.0-final protocol](../completions/artifacts/RC-1.0-final/protocol.md) §3.4, settlement 2: the counter was aligned to §3.3, not §3.3 narrowed to the counter** — `task validate` joins, a bare `doc list` leaves, `driver/channels.py` is the authoritative implementation and the shell counter is demoted to an explicitly indicative quick look. Verified when it landed: the aligned counter reproduces the reader's §3.3 numbers exactly on all four archived sessions (**2 · 9 · 5 · 6**, against the old **0 · 4 · 0 · 1**), and `run.py observe --archive` still reproduces the 1.0.0-gate table. **The fix also caught a second, invisible divergence**: `is_shipped_adjacent` modelled the shell grep as an *argv prefix* when the grep is a **substring** match, so the model was stricter than the thing it claimed to model — the archive happened to agree, which is why nobody saw it. The question as it stood: **`run-session.sh`'s counter is labelled `(VERB-ADJACENT, §3.3)` and implements two thirds of it (2026-08-25).** *(The `:174` line reference in the original wording is stale — the counter now sits at `:263`; `:174` is the `--shell` branch.)* It misses `jigc task validate <id>`, which §3.3 lists verbatim, and counts a bare `jigc doc list`, where §3.3 requires `--task`. Net undercount of **2–5 in all four** 1.0.0-gate blind sessions. **No published figure is wrong**: the record broke `task validate` into its own column and said beneath the table that §3.3 counts it too — the humans compensated by hand, every time. The driver reports the gap (`Observation.adjacent_counter_gap`) rather than resolving it. What is owed is one decision — align the counter to §3.3, or narrow §3.3 to what the counter means — so the compensation stops being required. *Trigger:* drafting the next trial's protocol.

- **(D) `jigc validate` exits non-zero once un-adopted files sit at managed homes — a declared behaviour change, not a defect (M46 Increment 3, 2026-08-19).** Through 1.0.0-rc.11 a stock brownfield repo — a real Keep-a-Changelog `CHANGELOG.md`, a hand-written `VISION.md`, an MADR `docs/decisions/*.md`, plus `jigc setup` and nothing else — validated at **exit 0** with one adoption advisory per file. It now **exits non-zero**: `schema-conformance.unadopted-instance` is `render::STORE_EXIT_FLIPS`' fifth member ([validation.md](../design/validation.md) → Exit semantics, where M42's exit-0 is withdrawn with what it bought). **The finding is untouched** — advisory, `GATES_NOWHERE`, routed at `jigc ingest` — so no task gate, no `finalize`, and no pre-commit hook starts blocking; what changed is the *sweep's* verdict on its own run. The paired half is the reason it had to: `migrate-corpus` now excludes a never-adopted foreign file before its fold ([corpus-migration.md](../design/corpus-migration.md) → The foreign arm), which removed the only other surface that made a squatter audible, so leaving the sweep green would have turned one door's false route into both doors' false green. **Why the protocol needs it in writing:** a blind session's first act on a brownfield corpus is `jigc validate`, so this is met on **every** adoption arm within minutes, and an unbriefed observer scores a non-zero exit on a repo that has done nothing wrong as a regression — the trial then spends its budget re-deriving a decision this repo already took. Two consequences worth designing for rather than discovering: an adoption arm cannot use `jigc validate`'s exit code as its "clean start" signal (read the findings, not the code), and a session that adopts its foreign docs should see the exit go to 0 as the last of them lands — a **positive-control-shaped** check the protocol gets for free. *Trigger:* drafting the next trial's protocol — before the first blind session is briefed.

- **(D) `jigc task validate` no longer reports a conformant task clean — the changelog advisory joined the previewed set (M46 Increment 6, 2026-08-19; recorded here 2026-08-28, when drafting the protocol found it missing).** Through 1.0.0-rc.11 a `single-task` that wrote no changelog entry validated with `no findings — the task validates clean`. It now draws `changelog-recording.gate-granted-unused` at **`jigc task validate`**: the advisory's predicate moved from an item count to a **staged write-touch** against the un-authored baseline, and it joined `preview_gates`, so it fires at the door where its in-task route is still live. Exit stays **0** at the default severity; a project that promotes the key to `blocking` gets `jigc task validate` **exit 3** on the same state `finalize` refuses. Measured as the clean golden moving (`crates/cli/tests/task_lifecycle.rs` → `NO_DELTA_CLEAN_VALIDATE_GOLDEN`, which *is* the advisory block) and declared as bound (4) of the increment: *“The advisory now rides every gate-granting task's `task validate` until the changelog is touched — measured above as the clean golden moving. It is intended.”* **Why the protocol needs it in writing:** this is the **second** declared change a blind session meets, minutes after the first — every arm that mints a task through a gate-granting workflow draws it on that task's first `task validate` — and an unbriefed observer scores an advisory on a task that has done nothing wrong as a regression. **The entry above is therefore not the whole brief: the protocol carries two declared behaviour changes, not one**, and this bullet exists because a ledger whose job is completeness was found one short at the moment it was consulted. *Trigger:* drafting the next trial's protocol — before the first blind session is briefed.

### The rc.9 wave (M45) — the complete-fix contract — ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-24

*(Chartered 2026-07-22, **Settle complete 2026-07-23** — every fork re-posed against a real-binary baseline (five capability-auditors + four gap-detectors + an independent robust-advocate + a three-part cost spike, all at `d55c88f`) that **corrected seven charter premises** and found **six defects outside the charter**, three of which commit clean and validate clean forever; four chartered forks were mis-posed and one trigger was unfireable as written. The **overload valve was re-cut at the *fork* boundary**, not the fix/infrastructure boundary — four ordered tests (**T1** integrity · **T2** axis · **T3** capacity · **T4** no-hollow-claim) — so **M45 = the integrity wave** (the six new defects + the charter's tier 1 + the pinning infrastructure unchanged + forks 1/2/3/4/9-rider/11), the fired-trigger capability forks route to **M46** (below), and the ~20-item tier-2 law batch is the release valve. Full record + rationale: [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-23 M45 planning: the Settle. Post-Settle scope + acceptance: [roadmap.md](roadmap.md) → Milestone 45; the testing-infrastructure foundation, corrected in the same motion: [pinning.md](pinning.md). M45 precedes the **1.0.0 call** (the call stays the human's). Graduated — remove on next pass; the deferrals it mints are below.)*

**The three in-wave forks the graduation deleted — restored, and kept here until M45 ships, because nothing else here holds their statements** (settled 2026-07-23 at the pre-decompose review; full rationale [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-23, *Three forks the Settle declared in-wave and settled nowhere*):

- **Fork 2 — form-vision role-binding. Resolved: bind-on-copy-on-write, plus the recovery route repaired.** A write verb that copy-on-writes a doc matching an **unbound `allows-create` role binds it** (the type→role map is unambiguous today — no workflow declares two `allows-create` entries of the same type), **and** `create.serial-collision` over a same-identity staged copy **acks `existed` and binds** instead of refusing, since today's route points away from the only repairing action. The class is **6 workflow×role pairs with two symptom shapes** — silent-empty `@`-slices and slug-less, unusable `<<author:>>` addresses — and the root fix closes both. One sub-decision is left to the builder deliberately: bind-on-first-copy-in vs matching the create-gate's existing last-write-wins. **Load-bearing for sequencing** — the golden generation waits on it ([pinning.md](pinning.md) → Sequencing).
- **Fork 4 — the milestone-execution on-ramp. Resolved: suppress**, with `suppressed: {reason, expires}`. The workflow composes a degenerate walk today (zero `Spawn:` lines, an unresolved `<MILESTONE_ID>`) while sitting unsuppressed in the router catalog. The design-sized on-ramp question itself stays out, per the rc.7 sequencing note (below, the M43 entry).
- **Fork 11 — the clean-room residue. Resolved: no re-probe before 1.0** — the live fan-out of the project-alpha-3.0 trial discharges the chartered probe. *Honest bound, recorded rather than absorbed:* this leaves the **pre-trial environment matrix** with no binding occasion inside M45, so it ships **unexercised**.

### The rc.10 wave (M47 — the surface-fundament wave) — ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-09

*(Chartered 2026-07-25, **Settle complete 2026-07-26** — fourteen forks resolved over a baseline that **corrected eight charter premises** and found **22 defects outside the charter** (five integrity-tier, five on the acceptance trial's own probe routes), then an independent pre-decompose review that **refuted two Settle claims against the code** and forced four re-settles. Three one-way doors each argued by an independent robust-advocate before the human chose, all resolved robust: the validate preview contract · the slug rule at generation 3 · the schema-hash presentation projection. Full record: [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → the 2026-07-26 Settle + review entries. Persisted baseline + gate-record: [completions/artifacts/M47/](../completions/artifacts/M47/). Post-Settle scope + acceptance: [roadmap.md](roadmap.md) → Milestone 47 — eleven increments. Graduated — remove on next pass; the deferrals it mints are below.)*

- **(D) The manifest-hash-change fence — ~~DEFERRED to M46 at M47 planning (2026-07-26)~~ DISCHARGED 2026-08-15 (M48 Increment 11).** **Built as mechanism (b)**, the CI check — with its window corrected at the increment-11 plan halt from `HEAD~1` to the **pushed range**, because Actions fires one run per push at the tip and the only real instance in this repo's history (`6e81d53`, the 16 re-pins) landed 34 commits deep, where the settled form is provably clean. It fires over both manifests, one violation per entity whose hash moved while its co-located version stood still, with the per-entity escape `Manifest-Repin: <entity>` for a legitimate re-pin. The other two candidates **stay refused for the reasons recorded below**: (a) the append-only ledger is a new stored format; (c) the in-process successor rule is unimplementable in-process. **Two declared bounds, taken deliberately:** it protects **this repo only, not adopters**, and it is a **build fence, not a surface**. Fence: `crates/cli/tests/manifest_freeze_fence.rs` + the named `manifest-freeze fence` step in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`; statements of record: [corpus-migration.md](../design/corpus-migration.md) → The freeze — declared *and* enforced, and [storage.md](../design/storage.md) → Identity. *(Original entry text kept below for the reasoning it records; its "Trigger: the M46 Settle" is spent.)* Rider 6 of the schema-hash decision — *"any manifest hash change without a `schema-version` bump is a build error"* — was taken by the human and then **dropped at the pre-decompose review**: it has **no implementable mechanism**. A pack-load assert can only verify `schema_hash(schema) == entry.schema_hash`; it cannot observe that a manifest value *changed* without a recorded prior, and the decision explicitly forecloses snapshots. Worse, the narrowing it rides on requires **16 bare re-pins at unchanged `schema-version`s** — the first thing the fence would forbid. It was also the wave's only piece of genuinely new machinery with no field defect behind it. **The problem it was meant to solve is real and recorded:** while the freeze is comment-guarded, *"re-pin the hash"* is one keystroke with two meanings — a legitimate prose fix and a silent freeze breach that ships an un-migrated structural change past every gate at exit 0 (spike-proven live on rc.9). M47's narrowing is the **precondition** for fencing it: with only contract keys in the projection, every hash move is a real contract change. Candidate mechanisms for the M46 Settle: an append-only `(type, version) → hash` ledger asserted at pack-load (strongest, protects adopters, but a new stored format) · a CI git-diff check against `HEAD`'s manifest (cheap, protects this repo only) · the successor rule M47 states without fencing — *each hashed entry's hash may move only when its **co-located** version field moves* (`slug-rule.version` for the rule, `schema-version` per doctype). *Trigger:* the M46 Settle.

- **(D) The M46 demand-counter re-count — ~~OWED AT THE M46 SETTLE, keyed to M47's landing (2026-07-26)~~ DISCHARGED at the M46 Settle, 2026-08-18.** `:14`'s rule forces a thrice-demanded deferral onto *the next wave's* Settle agenda, and M47 executes before M46 — so capability-wave entries **2, 3, 4 and 8** formally route to M47's Settle, which each entry's own forward key contradicted, every one of them keyed at that Settle until M46 Increment 9 re-keyed them. Resolved by **deferring the re-count, not the entries**: M47's scope **drains evidence three of them are counted on** — entry 3's count includes the drift hook crying wolf on four consecutive commits (M47 increment 7 fixes it) and 21 `title-names-symbol` firings (increment 10 fixes the message); entry 8's trigger fired on N1's recovery needing the recorded baseline set (increment 2 removes the motive). Re-counting *before* M47 lands would hand M46 a count this wave has already paid down. The re-count was taken over the post-M47 state, and its outcome is recorded **per entry** in the M46 disposition table under *the capability wave* below — 2 and 4 re-keyed, 3 and 8 retired — so `:14`'s blessed form is discharged entry by entry instead of surviving as one standing count. *Trigger:* none — the entry is closed, not re-keyed.


**Provenance:** the project-alpha-4.0 unseeded-trial adjudication ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-25 the final unseeded trial; verified findings + repro blocks [completions/artifacts/RC-alpha4/findings-verification.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-alpha4/findings-verification.md)), sequenced by the human 2026-07-25: **fixes and understandability only — no new functionality** (new verbs/flags/surfaces stay at M46). The razor: change now what is contract-pinned or would be a breaking change post-1.0; everything else that lands is wording the surface contract already owes. **The boundary, checkable:** the wave changes *what existing surfaces say*, never *what surfaces exist*.
