- **Tier 1 — the contract-touching fixes** (pre-1.0-cheap, post-1.0-breaking): **H1** the validate preview contract (carries the wave's main Settle fork — *validate runs the finalize gates* vs *scope the four "previews what finalize gates on" surfaces*; engages M45 Decision 6's validate-0/finalize-3 split on its rationale) · **P5-4** the wrong-item-id finding-code/route flip (`write.wrong-shape`→`write.not-present` + containing-section route — a stable-key change, now or never; moves the `write_not_present_route.rs` pin) · **the `task diff` envelope hole** (the one task verb ignoring `--format json`) · **H2** the hook-rejected-commit survivable frame swept over **every committing door** (migrate-corpus: typed error + registry code + a re-run that notices staged-but-uncommitted; axis check against milestone join).
- **Tier 2 — the understandability batch** (the trial's verified wording/clarity set, each item keyed to its findings-verification row): AGENT.md exit-code sentence (H3; the adapter golden currently pins the lie — regen rides) · setup hook path threaded real (P1-7; golden regen rides) · `title-names-symbol` message honesty (P1-6) · drift-hook severity discrimination (P1-8; one grep clause — the only behavior-adjacent member, foldable out at Settle) · the append-vs-update prose pair aligned to real semantics across the five pack steps + `author --help` (P3-2) · the commit-doc line into the dev finalize step (P2-1) · owner-artifact ladder: repo-root stated + one absolute example, route names the root (P1-1) · copy-in acks on the five edit verbs (P4-6) · stale-copy note self-phrasing (P3-3) · miss-routes teach: `store.no-such-section` names the real sections (P2-6 residue) · `doc list` headers + `create-gates:` defined + "`{#id}` anchors are the item ids" stated (P4-5/7) · preview advertising reconciled with its refusals (P1-5; small fork: qualify the two lines vs degrade preview to the direct compose for non-minting members) · the advisory-is-scope-dependent clarification (P1-4) · the slug rule's one-liner on a user-facing surface (P5-3, the documentation half only) · "documented code" defined (P4-2) · the `doc show` leaf-purity guarantee documented (P3-9).
- **Tier 3 — the test conversion** (pinning.md §3 discipline): every findings-verification repro block lands — confirmed blocks as the fixes' red tests, **refuted blocks as `pinned_facts/`** (address forms resolve · bare-path code-anchor accepted · finalize-JSON purity's non-empty-left-out arm into `machine_output.rs`) · the two lie-pinning goldens regenerate with their fixes, never before.
- **~~The latent-surface sweep — a chartered INCREMENT, not an acceptance probe~~ DISCHARGED 2026-08-13** (`20800f7`). **The derivation was run against the verb registry and the `verb_suite_coverage` map, and it turned up one arm** — which is a *result*, exactly as this entry instructed, not a shortfall. Dispositions, each checked **by test content**: `relocate`'s happy-path move and frozen-doctype refusal are fenced (`flow40_acceptance` arm 3 · `anyhow_route_spans`), and it is correctly **absent from `ERROR_CODE_REGISTRY`** because it `git mv`s and stages but never commits; `milestone join` is driven at five call sites across three suites and commits nothing; and **the leftover re-`provision` stopped being a sweep candidate the moment the trial confirmed it as a data-loss defect** (RC-pre-1.0 → F3) — a standing test over its current behaviour would pin data loss as expected output, so it routes to that fix's red test. The one genuine hole was **`relocate`'s foreign-squatter displacement**, fenced only by an in-module unit test: it `git rm --cached`s a user's committed-but-unmanaged file and renames it into the **gitignored** `.jigc/` workbench — F3's shape one door over, except the bytes survive, which makes *the ack naming it* the whole safety property and is precisely what a unit test cannot reach. Pinned at `relocate::a_foreign_squatter_is_displaced_into_the_workbench_and_the_ack_names_it`, proven live by an applied mutant (drop `workbench` from the renderer → red; revert → green). *(Original charter text kept below for the reasoning it records.)*
- **The latent-surface sweep — the original charter** (moved out of the trial 2026-08-09 on review). Its deliverable is **standing tests**, which is build work: it needs a builder, a red step and a gate run, and in an acceptance slot it has no owner and decays into the findings list it was chartered not to be. **Scope derived from the verb registry** (`jigc --help`, its 17 top-level verbs and their leaves) **minus what the trial walks minus what a named standing suite fences** — never from the leftovers of a changed-file table, which is how the first attempt was built and why it missed `jigc config <verb>`, `jigc workflow --preview` and `jigc task discard` while wrongly including surfaces already covered. Two corrections it inherits: `unmanage` **has** been walked (RC-alpha3 session 8; RC-adoption/impl-rc5), and `relocate`/`upgrade`'s five changed lines each are entirely inside `mod tests` — zero production change, so neither is a regression candidate. `jigc upgrade` genuinely appears in no RC artifact. **Scheduled: before the 1.0.0 call** (2026-08-09 — the human's call, on the stated goal that the v1 decision rests on real coverage rather than on what a trial happened to reach). **Its scope was derived rather than left open, and it is small.** Against the verb registry (`start` · `workflow` · `doc` · `task` · `config` · `milestone` · `setup` · `uninstall` · `upgrade` · `ingest` · `migrate` · `migrate-corpus` · `unmanage` · `rename` · `relocate` · `describe` · `validate`), minus what the trial walks, minus what a **named** suite fences: `upgrade` (`upgrade.rs` · `upgrade_v1_v2.rs` · `upgrade_finding_keys.rs`), `unmanage` (`unmanage.rs`), `describe` (`describe.rs`), `workflow` (`workflow_preview.rs` · `workflow_reentry.rs` · …), `config` (five suites), `rename` (`flow37_rename.rs` + `commit_rejected_axis.rs`) are all **fenced**. So the sweep's real work-list is the **thin** residue: `relocate` (no dedicated suite — only incidental mentions in three others), the **cross-verb** cells no single-verb suite owns, and a re-`provision` over a non-registered leftover. **Each finding lands as a standing test, which is the whole deliverable** — the sweep is run once and the suite holds the ground afterwards. If the derivation turns up nothing beyond those, that is a *result*: record it and close, rather than manufacturing arms to justify the increment.

- **Acceptance — the pre-1.0.0 trial** (revised 2026-08-09 after an independent adversarial review of the first widening; [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-08-09 the acceptance entries). **This bullet is the single home for the trial's shape** — [roadmap.md](roadmap.md) and [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) point here and must not restate it (the first widening left two stale *"three-probe"* restatements behind, which is the cross-reference-never-restate rule breaking exactly as predicted). Two instruments, deliberately different in kind:

  **Three blind sessions** — clean-room rules, verbatim prompts, per-session feedback, **invocation log ON** (`jigc config set invocation-log true` in the worker prompt, as every prior trial did — M47's nine per-door error codes have the log as their *only* observation surface). **G1 cold start** (setup → first task → first ADR → finalize on a zero-doc repo) carrying the two plants — a rejecting pre-commit hook under `core.hooksPath` and pre-staged files before the mint. **G2 the design altitude from zero** (do-research → form-vision → roadmap → first milestone). **G3 the corpus accretes from nothing** (first changelog entry · spec → implement-from-spec · first arch-doc · one hand-authored foreign doc mid-stream for detect-and-route). G1–G3 also **read the docs they are meant to read** — `QUICKSTART.md` (+41 lines this wave, carrying the recovery chapter G1's hook plant looks for) and `MIGRATING.md`; a changed doc no probe opens is tested by nothing.

  **One operator-scripted verification walk (V1)** — *not* a blind session, and the reason is evidence, not preference: its value is entirely operator-placed plants and scripted destructive sequences, and the prior protocol's own P5 probed fan-out *"only if a milestone arises naturally"*, i.e. a blind agent cannot be relied on to reach any of this. The walk, each arm justified by a behaviour M47 changed that no blind probe reaches:
    **The middle column was then applied to the reviewer's own list, and most of it dissolved** — verified against the suites, 2026-08-09, before any arm was baked in. **Test-fenced, therefore NOT walk arms:** `rename` under a rejecting hook, and Increment 2's four record-only doors (`create` · `add-task` · `add-from-spec` · `discard`), and `migrate-corpus` under rejection — all of them driven by `commit_rejected_axis.rs`, whose door set is derived from `ERROR_CODE_REGISTRY` (*one list, two consumers*), so a door added without a code is a red test; and `ingest`'s near-miss route, driven by `ingest_flow12.rs`. The review was right that **no probe** reaches these and wrong that they therefore need probe arms — which is precisely the error the two-way partition caused, reproduced one level up. *(The `rename` correction stands on its own: calling it "untouched" was false.)* What survives as genuinely **neither**:
    - **`jigc migrate <path> --as <doctype>`** end to end through the migrate workflow — the transform, not G3's detect-and-route.
    - **`migrate-corpus`'s N2 signature specifically** — the axis fences the *rejection*, not the **recovery** it enables. Needs both plants: a committed `schema-version:` stamp hand-downgraded **and** a rejecting hook, repaired, then the re-run. **Both, or the arm is vacuous** — no schema-version moved in M47, so an ordinary corpus answers `already current`. Expect *"an earlier run's migration was written but never landed"*.
    - **A re-`provision` over a non-registered leftover** — checked and **unfenced by any suite**, consistent with its being a declared bound (knowingly unfixed, therefore untested). The rest of the teardown matrix is fenced: `uninstall` over a live abort by `uninstall_worktree_guard.rs` (all three rejection causes), `discard` ±`--force` by `milestone_discard.rs`. Plant staged **and** unstaged **and** untracked work plus an authored doc in one sub-task area, so a destroyer shows as **visible loss rather than inference** — the reason the `uninstall` hole was legible at all.
    - **Fresh clone → `milestone finalize` FIRST** (expect the zero-contribution refusal) → follow its route → `provision` → execute the emitted `Spawn:` line → land. One arm covers Increment 3(b)(i), the reseed, and route-followability. *(Continuing the work directly would skip the refusal that motivates the reseed.)*
    - **An rc.9-authored corpus continued on rc.10** — build/install `1.0.0-rc.9` to a temp prefix, author ~5 managed docs of mixed doctypes + a milestone record, commit; then switch to rc.10 and continue (`validate` · `doc show` · a task → finalize · a `rename` · `migrate-corpus`). **This is the most common real 1.0.0 upgrade path and the state Increment 1's one-way doors are about**, and it is covered by nothing today — not even the fixture builder, which constructs every state by driving the *current* binary. Costed honestly: the prior trial corpora died with the machine, so the rc.9 build is real setup.
    - The three-cause abort matrix is a **cheap re-check only** — the M47 e2e already drove all the committing doors **[Corrected 2026-09-15 (M51 Increment 7, T3):** *all nine committing doors* — the axis M47 carried; it carries **ten** since M49 Increment 2 T3.**]** and all three rejection causes through this binary.

  **The coverage rule, stated so it stays checkable:** *the acceptance must reach what the wave changed.* Applied against the diff, every changed surface lands in exactly one of **three** columns — **trial-reached** · **test-fenced** (naming the suite) · **neither**. The first widening used a two-way split, which had no way to say *"fenced by a test, not by the trial"* and so inflated the probe count; ~27% of M47's changed lines fell in neither column unexamined. Worked example: `task diff --format json` is a **frozen-contract addition** named on **no** composed surface (`grep` over both packs and a fresh `AGENT.md` → zero hits), so no blind probe will drive it — it is **test-fenced** by `format_json_success_axis.rs`, and that is the honest disposition rather than a probe arm.

  **The conversion obligation, with an owning act.** Every CONFIRMED **and** REFUTED verdict arrives with a repro block, and a refuted block's obligation is to become a standing test (pinning.md §3) — the refuting fact is exactly what drifts when nothing tests it. **The fence is the human's own gate: the 1.0.0 call is not taken until every trial repro block carries `pinned-by:` or `UNPINNED: <why>`.** Named because the trial is *a report, not a second close*, and no wave follows it to inherit the conversion — as prose alone it would be the failure mode this repo has already had twice.

  **Trial cadence after 1.0 — and an honest note on the trigger.** A new feature earns a probe scoped to it rather than a full re-drive; the ground is held by standing tests (pinning.md's three drift mechanics). A **full sweep is owed** on a one-way door, a frozen-contract/schema change, or a wave changing **≥3 user-facing verbs or ≥3 composed workflows**. **Stated plainly: as written this fires on every wave M41–M47** (each moved a contract-version, a schema-version, or a slug-rule generation), so the saving is for **post-1.0 point releases and single-fix patches**, not for waves of the size we have been running. The count replaces *"many surfaces"*, which was undecidable. **Two bounds so this is not read as tests replacing trials:** a trial is a **finder**, a test a **fence** (the proptest relationship one level up) — tests cannot pin what nobody has thought of yet, and the last five trials' dominant lens (*the capability exists, no composed surface names it*) is not expressible as an assertion until after it is found; and a scoped probe **cannot see interaction** by construction, which is what the triggered sweep is for.

  The wave ships **1.0.0-rc.10** at close (the rc-name rule); both instruments log on it; **then the 1.0.0 call — the human's.**

- **The conversion obligation binds all six probes** (pinning.md §3, stated here because it is what makes the cadence below safe): every CONFIRMED **and** REFUTED verdict arrives with a repro block, and a **refuted** block's obligation is to become a standing test — the refuting fact is exactly what drifts when nothing tests it. A probe whose findings do not convert has bought a one-time observation, not coverage.
- **Trial cadence after 1.0 — targeted by default, full sweep on a trigger.** Once the ground is held by standing tests, a new feature earns a **probe scoped to it**, not a full re-drive. But a **full sweep is owed** on any of: a **one-way door** · a change to a **frozen contract or schema** · a wave touching **many surfaces at once**. The trigger is stated rather than left to judgment, because *"we still do full sweeps, just less often"* decays into never. **Rationale, and the honest bound:** a trial is a **finder**, a test is a **fence** — the relationship the repo already states for proptests, one level up. Tests cannot pin what nobody has thought of yet, and the last five trials' dominant lens (*the capability exists, no composed surface names it*) is not expressible as an assertion before it is discovered. A scoped probe also **cannot see interaction** by construction: M47's sharpest finding existed *because* one increment changed the state model another increment's prose described. That class is what the triggered full sweep is for.

The wave ships **1.0.0-rc.10** at close (the rc-name rule); the trial logs on it; **then the 1.0.0 call — the human's.**

### The rc.11 wave (M48) — ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-15

*(Chartered 2026-08-13, **Settle complete the same day** — every chartered fork re-posed against a baseline that **exercised the real binary** (five capability-auditors + four gap-detectors + an independent robust-advocate at `8979f16`), which **corrected 19 charter premises** and found **8 defects outside the charter**, two of them HIGH data loss. **Four charter premises were refuted outright** — F14's routing cause (the defect is bundling; the catalog states the intent verbatim) · F7's fix arm (settled *against* at M36, and the freeze cannot signal it) · F5's quoted rationale (it governs the `docs-root` `git mv`s, not the manifest write) · `doc create --slug` (it already exists; the finding is true only of `doc author`) — and **two chartered items were re-tiered** (F7 out of tier 1; F12 into the now-or-never subset, which the charter had marked only F2 and F7). **Settled:** F3 = the guard's subject becomes *every path the call is about to remove*, at three doors + the authored-prose arm · F2 = reject + an in-task doc-level title change · F1 = the `cli ∪ schema` union derivation (30/31 steps), the fence iterating the derivation, + the read-surface doors · F10 = **convergence** on the shipped store-scope code, so M21 is honoured not overturned · F8 = the two config verbs + jigc owning the unknown-subcommand error surface · F14 = the gate stated as a discriminating axis · F7 = fix the guidance · `describe` = the filter, not the positional form · the **pre-1.0 additive window closes** with a fenced text/JSON parity sweep over every machine-output surface · the **manifest-hash CI fence** is built · flow 48 **mints the registries it lacks**. **Seven refusals, recorded because a wave that cannot refuse cannot halt:** the search verb · the file-state read verb · the checkpoint record · F15's structural half · F7's canonical-form change · F14's unbundling · `describe <name>`. One dissent recorded as a dissent: the **first adapter-owned instruction artifact** (VISION's never-built *"small set of skill/command files"*) was taken over the proposer's recommendation to park it. Full record + rationale: [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-08-13 M48 planning: the Settle; persisted baseline + settle record: [completions/artifacts/M48/](../completions/artifacts/M48/settle-record.md). Post-Settle scope + acceptance: [roadmap.md](roadmap.md) → Milestone 48. Graduated — remove on next pass.)*

**Provenance:** the **pre-1.0.0 trial** on the installed `1.0.0-rc.10` — the acceptance instrument for M47, three blind sessions plus the operator-scripted walk over six fresh corpora, 294 logged invocations ([completions/artifacts/RC-pre-1.0/trial-record.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-pre-1.0/trial-record.md); 16 CONFIRMED · 4 REFUTED, every verdict with a repro block, in [findings-verification.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-pre-1.0/findings-verification.md); [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-08-13). Zero data-loss or corruption defects on any *blind* path and zero regressions — but the walk found one **unrecoverable data loss**, and the discoverability lens landed for the **sixth consecutive trial**, this time on the very capability built to answer it.

**Sequenced by the human 2026-08-13: pre-1.0, and deliberately generous.** The reasoning is recorded because it sets the razor: jigc is a *context compiler*, so a discoverability defect is not a one-time annoyance but **a tax on every future session, forever** — three sessions in this trial burned tokens `cat`-ing files to do what a shipped verb already does. Fixing it once dominates paying it per use. Pre-1.0 is also the last moment the now-or-never subset is free rather than breaking.

**The razor — generous, but still able to refuse.** *In scope if it makes an existing surface more **reliable**, more **discoverable**, or **cheaper to use correctly** — including **new** surface where that is what discoverability requires. Out of scope if it opens a **new domain capability**.* Stated in this form deliberately: M47's razor is what let its planners **refuse** things and halt twice on false premises, and a wave scoped as *"everything that improves quality"* can produce no refusal and therefore no halt. The generosity is in admitting new *surface* (a `config get`, an edge, a search verb if the Settle takes it); the boundary is new *domain*.

**Tier 1 — reliability, and the now-or-never subset.** The split matters because some of these are cheap now and **breaking after 1.0**:
- **F3 · HIGH, data loss.** `milestone provision` over a **non-registered leftover** destroys staged, unstaged **and** untracked work at **exit 0** under the ordinary success line; the untracked file is unrecoverable. Decisive framing: `milestone discard` and `jigc uninstall` **refuse over a single untracked file** — the same destruction guarded at two doors, unguarded at a third. Meets M46 entry 11's rider trigger with the loss *shown*.
- **F2 · HIGH, silent write loss (now-or-never).** `doc author` ignores a corrected `title:` on re-author, applies the rest of the payload, and acks the **old** slug — indistinguishable from success. Cost in-trial: a `task discard` plus re-minting a whole migration. Making it reject is a behaviour change on a shipped write path: cheap now, breaking later.
- **F6 · MEDIUM.** An **idempotent** `rename` (new title slugs to the current id **and** the H1 already matches) dresses git's empty-commit refusal in the survivable-hook-rejection frame — asserting a rejection that never happened and routing to a re-run that can only fail identically. The un-swept point of an axis whose sibling is fenced at `flow37_rename::placement_same_slug_retitle_succeeds`.
- **F7 · MEDIUM (now-or-never).** An optional section omitted *as its own template instructs* still renders its heading; a hollow `## Options` shipped into a committed ADR. Changes rendered bytes of committed docs.
- **F4 · F5 · MEDIUM.** `setup` lists the pre-commit hook as installed while its install commit omits it (in-repo `core.hooksPath`) — *"a clone gets no drift backstop, and nothing says so"*. `jigc config set` leaves config uncommitted and unmentioned, so it rides an unrelated feature commit — measured three times, including a fresh clone that inherited no config and logged nothing.

**Tier 2 — discoverability, and the wave's centre of gravity.**
- **F1 · HIGH, the sixth landing.** All three blind sessions went to the filesystem to read their own in-flight work while `jigc doc show --task <id>` has shipped since **M43** (recorded there as *"demanded three trials running"*) and is stated in its own help **and** in line 3 of the `AGENT.md` preload. **The gap is countable: of **66** pack step files — the count shipped by the pre-1.0.0 trial's binary, 1.0.0-rc.10 — exactly one names `doc show`, and that one is a committed-doc id lookup, not a read-back of in-flight work.** This row is the count's home: the live docs that restated it cross-reference here (CLAUDE.md → *Cross-reference, never restate*) and the dated records keep their own measurement, corrected in a dated bracket that points back. It is scoped to the binary it was measured on because a bare figure re-falsifies itself on the next step file the pack gains — the trial's own repro command reported **69**, which is `ls` counting its two directory headers and their blank separator on top of 66. The agent is told once at session start, then walked through six write verbs that never mention it again.
- **F8.** No config read verb (`config get`/`config list`), and — one cause, two doors — clap's similarity tip answers a **read** intent with a **write** verb (`get`→`set`, `read`→`create`).
- **F15.** *"Milestone"* names two objects with no edge: the `planning` workflow authors a roadmap entry and **mints no milestone**, and `add-from-spec` seeds from a spec's *criteria* while planning produces prose in a decomposition slot, so the halves cannot meet.
- **F12 · F9.** `doc schema` projects an enum repeatable's id-source as `category` while `doc author` accepts it only as `title`, and the rejection never says so. The generated slug statement omits that a **literal hyphen** splits words for the cap, while its glue clause implies hyphens bind.
- **The smaller verified set** (feedback-G1/2/3, each keyed to its row): `milestone create` positional vs `doc create --title` · silent slug truncation and easy collisions · `set-slot` raw prose vs `author`'s `<<…>>`, stated only in `--help` · no `doc author --dry-run` for a 9KB payload · no `--slug` on `doc create`/`author` though `rename` has one · no in-task doc-level title change · no path filter on `task diff` · `implement-from-spec` printing an empty criteria list before bind · `describe` as one undifferentiated wall with no `--workflows`/`describe <name>` · composed-output density (~200 lines embedding a whole unused schema; one warning verbatim 4×) · the generated hook conceding *"the exit code is wrong-way-round"*.
- **F10 · F13 · F14 · F16.** A stale conformance advisory attaching to unrelated tasks and named `-block` while advisory · the finalize header asserting the commit before the rejection · a routing gap manufacturing a guaranteed advisory (no workflow fits *"record a decision and point the code at it"*) · a walked Checkpoint leaving no record.

**Tier 3 — the conversion obligation.** Every confirmed repro block lands as its fix's red test; the refuted set is **already closed** (`fa343dc` — R1–R3 carry `pinned-by:` citations to existing contract suites, verified by content; R4 owes none). **The 1.0.0 call is not taken until every row of [the conversion ledger](../completions/artifacts/RC-pre-1.0/findings-verification.md) carries `pinned-by:` or a stated `UNPINNED: <why>`.**

**Settle forks — the four that need deciding before decomposition:**
1. **F3's fix shape.** Refuse + `--force` mirroring `milestone discard`, or refuse and route to `git worktree prune`? M46 entry 11 deferred *"a new blocking refusal plus a new `--force` flag"* as new surface — but that was about `finalize`, and this trigger is now met with measured loss. **Owed an independent robust-advocate**, because "add a flag" is precisely the cheap framing that entry warned against.
2. **F2's fix shape.** Reject the whole payload (atomic, consistent with `write.already-present`) versus ack `title ignored — create-only`. Rejection is safer and more consistent, and breaks any caller re-authoring with a stale title. Engages G3's underlying ask — an **in-task doc-level title change** — which the razor now admits.
3. **F1's scope and fence.** *Which* steps must state the read-back — every authoring step, or only those soliciting a slot write? And what does the pack-load fence key on? The `states-constraints:` stated-at mold (M43 fork 3 / M44 D5) is the precedent; this is the wave's one genuine design question.
4. **M46's ledger, adjudicated entry by entry — the agenda item that closes it.** Twelve entries, each ending with a stated disposition rather than surviving into another wave: **absorb** (entry 11's F3 half and entry 12's `hook_file` key are *fixes*, not capability, under this razor; likely entry 8) · **drain first, then re-count at wave close** (3, 4, 6 — their own recorded reasoning says the cheaper fix comes first: *"a ledger built over a floor a precision fix removes is capability for a state that no longer exists"*; *"a guard over a gap punishes the gap"* — and **F1's fix may drain entry 4's evidence base before its Settle runs**) · **stay deferred, re-counted** (2's re-encoding trigger and 7's pre-guard-corpus trigger are genuinely unfired). **The override to state explicitly if taken:** wanting a capability *for its own sake* rather than as an answer to a demand count is a legitimate product call, but it overrules the recorded "we counted N and still say no" reasoning and must be said in those words.

**Acceptance:** flow 48 in [worked-examples.md](../design/worked-examples.md), each arm **iterating its class's axis** from a code-side registry rather than pinning the reported repro (the M45 complete-fix contract; the M47 precedent). The wave ships **1.0.0-rc.11** at close (the rc-name rule). **Then the 1.0.0 call — the human's.**

**Provenance:** the M45 Settle's valve re-cut
 ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-23, Decisions 1 + 8). **T3 routes genuinely new capability here**; M45 keeps only what T1 (integrity) or T2 (axis) forces. Every entry below was **counted, re-Settled against its recorded rationale, and still deferred** — the demand-counter rule's own blessed outcome (*"we counted N demands and still say no, because…"*), written in that form so the count never restarts in a reader's head. **Sequencing:** unscheduled relative to the 1.0.0 call — nothing here is a v1-gating integrity item, which is exactly what T1 sorted.

1. **Gate-command / evidence rung — 3 demands (S2/S4 · lacon B13 · impl-rc5 M5), and still no.** Recorded rationale, quoted verbatim — [self-hosting.md](../design/self-hosting.md):79, the more on-point deferral the charter missed by quoting a *summary line* from this file instead: *"Surfacing a knob into composed step text would need a new compose data-value root (`cascade`/`knob`) that does not exist — disproportionate to its 'bounded leverage.' Deferred until a real recurring need prose can't carry."* It defers because it trips **T3 and neither T1 nor T2**: an unrecorded gate is a *trust* gap — no bytes produced unauthored, none discarded, no declared gate defeated. **And the fork was mis-posed:** its *"deterministic"* framing is false — an agent typing `cargo test → exit 0` into a slot is an **attestation, not evidence**, the inverse of the determinism boundary — so what M46 actually Settles is attested-prose (honest *if labelled as attestation*) vs CLI-observed (which crosses the recorded boundary). **RE-KEYED** (M46 Settle, 2026-08-18) — the corrected arms were posed and neither had anything to attach to: nothing at rc.11 records a cleared gate at all (the `Checkpoint:` string is read-path only, the task area holds no gate field, no verb could write one, and the invocation-log record shape has no field to carry it), so *attested-prose vs CLI-observed* is a choice about a surface that does not exist. *New trigger:* a trial produces a case where an **unrecorded** gate cost correctness, not discipline.

A checkpoint sailed past costs process discipline, never a byte (T1 clear). *Trigger:* superseded — the re-key is on the continuation line below. *(5th demand 2026-07-25, the sharpest instance: the project-alpha-4.0 unseeded P3 session walked planning's human-owned Settle on a history back-fill with genuinely nothing to settle — and nothing anywhere records that the gate was walked or vacuously passed; [RC-alpha4/recovered-analysis.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-alpha4/recovered-analysis.md), feedback P3 §3.)*
**Re-counted 2026-08-08 (post-M47, the owed re-count above): we counted 5 demands and still say no — untouched, the count unmoved.** M47 ships no new surface by its own boundary, and the only M47 commit to touch `planning.yaml` is increment 5's shared-`author-commit` split (`20686cf`) — a step-composition edit, not a re-encoding — so the recorded trigger stays unfired; no M47 commit touches the `checkpoint` step kind either. Nothing in the wave drained the motive: a walked gate still leaves no record, and the wave's own [planning gate-record](../completions/artifacts/M47/planning-gate-record.md) was filled by hand. **RE-KEYED, and taken off the schema window** (M46 Settle, 2026-08-18) — a new doctype is **not** a frozen-schema event (no hash moves, no snapshot is owed, `migrate-corpus` → `0 migrated, 0 blocked`, exit 0), so **it does not expire at 1.0**; two further blockers surfaced, that three locked sources give **three different gate sets** (13 / 12 / a third with `overload valve`) and that the spec's own growth path (*"a new lesson is a new gate-row"*) would make every addition a corpus-blocking required slot. *New trigger:* the planning workflow is re-encoded **and** the gate set is settled in one home — and it is built with a **repeatable `gates` section**, not one required slot per gate. **✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-30 (M49 Increment 9, T5–T7) — the re-key's trigger fired and its *shape* clause is overruled with its reason.** Both conjuncts landed (`planning.yaml` re-encoded with an `allows-create` role and an author step; the fourteen-gate set given one fenced home), and the record ships as **one required slot per gate** — the repeatable shape has no minimum-item fence, so it would have deleted the forcing function the doctype exists to be. Disposed in full on the standing entry under *No firm trigger yet* below.
3. **Acknowledged-findings ledger — 4th trial, and still no.** Engages the M41 (D) trigger verbatim: *"an adopter corpus surfaces a **measured, non-drainable** steady-state advisory floor"* — arguably fired (the `title-names-symbol` honest-title floor: renaming to satisfy it makes the docs worse). It defers because **the cheaper drain has not been tried**: the F5 option-(a) precision fix (near-match-of-anchored-symbol, declined at M40) may empty the floor the ledger would merely acknowledge, and a ledger built over a floor a precision fix removes is capability for a state that no longer exists. It stays purely additive when it lands (no one-way door; the stable finding key already ships), so waiting costs nothing. *Trigger:* superseded — the disposition is on the continuation line below. *(New datum 2026-07-25: project-alpha-4.0 — 21 `title-names-symbol` firings across an unseeded run (the trial's #3 finding code), the drift hook crying the same wolf line on four consecutive commits off cosmetic advisories, and the truncation twin: P5 piped output through `head`/`tail` and missed a fired `gate-granted-unused` advisory entirely — habituation and truncation are the same failure through different doors; [RC-alpha4/recovered-analysis.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-alpha4/recovered-analysis.md); [findings-verification.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-alpha4/findings-verification.md) → D3/D7.)*

Increment 10 (`33b698d`, D3) makes the message honest — it names the title-vs-anchor compound-identifier scan it actually ran instead of asserting a rename it never read. **The measured floor is untouched:** `crates/cli/probes/doc-code/` carries no M47 commit, so the 21 firings still fire — better worded and one door quieter, not fewer — and the F5 option-(a) precision fix is still untried; the truncation twin also stands (increment 7's `blocking_probes` key lets a shell bind the blocking set without scanning prose, but nothing shortens what a piped read misses). **RETIRED as posed** (M46 Settle, 2026-08-18) — driven, the floor is **9 firings / 16 components on one surface** (`jigc validate` only; the unrelated task gate, the unrelated finalize and the pre-commit hook print zero, so M47 Inc 7's re-key works), which means the habituation mechanism this entry tracked for four trials is **already gone**; the message does not lie — it states the comparison it made — and [validation.md](../design/validation.md):198 names the false-positive rate a *"conscious cost, recorded"*. *If it returns:* keyed to **a floor reaching a surface a worker did not ask for**, never to a count.

It defers because a read-side guard is **new capability** (T3) engaging the recorded ergonomics bet ([VISION.md](../VISION.md) principle #3, *adapter-enforced, not sandboxed*) and the cost-of-enforcement evidence, and because the failure mode is **staleness, not corruption** — the agent reads a true file by the wrong route (T1 clear). Shape candidates: [ideas/managed-doc-enforcement-hook.md](../ideas/managed-doc-enforcement-hook.md). *Trigger:* superseded — the disposition is on the continuation line below. *(4th read-side datum 2026-07-25, with the cause named: project-alpha-4.0's P4 worker — otherwise fully write-compliant — ran `grep -rn` over `docs/` because no search verb exists ("reading nineteen docs one at a time wasn't viable"), i.e. the missing [ideas/doc-search.md](../ideas/doc-search.md) verb is now *producing* the read-side bypasses this entry would guard against; the Settle should take the two together — a guard over a gap punishes the gap; [findings-verification.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-alpha4/findings-verification.md) → C2.)*

What the wave landed on the read surfaces improves what a *found* read says, never the finding of it (increment 10: `store.no-such-section` enumerating the real sections and teaching the `#section/<item>` form, `33b698d`; the read surfaces naming their own shapes, `241e822`). **RE-KEYED, post-1.0** (M46 Settle, 2026-08-18) — refused on better grounds than "capability": AGENT.md states an `unregistered` row is *"readable directly until adopted"* and a conformant managed doc reads clean, so **there is no unreadable-managed-doc contradiction to hang a guard on**. The search half is re-priced at **1–2 increments, not a wave** (`run_list` already enumerates every committed instance, and `rename.rs`'s `line_has_token` is a shipped, tested, gitignore-respecting repo-wide matcher), and [ideas/doc-search.md](../ideas/doc-search.md) stays parked — a *missing* capability, not an unnamed one, which is what this wave's lens has nothing to say about. *New trigger:* the first read-side bypass that costs **correctness rather than staleness**.

What defers is the tier itself — a per-language is-a-test detector (HD2/HD3, [ideas/multi-language-doc-code.md](../ideas/multi-language-doc-code.md)) — **new capability** (T3), and with the rider landed the surface stops lying, so nothing false remains standing (T4 satisfied without it). **Corrected 2026-08-19 (M46 Increment 7):** that closure clause was **false when written** — the M45 rider reached one of the *two* steps soliciting the anchor, and of the findings it touched (the `unsupported-language` no-grammar/is-a-test split) neither was the blocking one, so on a closure corpus `doc-code.criterion-maps-to-test` still asserted a name was *absent from* a file whose indexed blob carried it ([scope-audit.md](../completions/artifacts/M46/scope-audit.md) → N-5, reproduced on three frameworks by three parties). M46 Increment 7 lands the remainder — the diagnosis splits on whether the name is in the bytes the probe parsed, the route leads with the file-only fallback and states what it buys, and the second soliciting step gains the caveat under a **derived** owe-set ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-08-19 M46 Increment 7; [validation.md](../design/validation.md) → The anchor grammar + resolution). **The tier itself is untouched and still defers** on its own trigger (the `criterion-maps-to-test` port entry under *No firm trigger yet* below): what shipped is the integrity half, and no per-language is-a-test predicate exists. **Disposition of record: SPLIT — the message ships, the tier RE-KEYED** (M46 Settle, 2026-08-18) — the retirement was refused (reproduced on three frameworks by three parties; the blocking message asserted a symbol *"is absent from"* a file whose **indexed blob** contains it, and all three offered routes were inapplicable), while the *verdict* stays per-design at [validation.md](../design/validation.md):204. *Trigger:* the tier's own, and it is not this ledger's agenda — the `criterion-maps-to-test` port entry under *No firm trigger yet* below carries it.

It defers as **T3**: the sweep is a whole new checking surface, and the fork is unresolved between three shapes with very different costs (de-park the sweep · the cheaper "docs anchoring your staged diff" surface · print-the-bound-only) — exactly the decision an integrity wave has no room to make well. **RE-KEYED** (M46 Settle, 2026-08-18) — the *"80% already shipped"* hypothesis is refuted: the middle shape is **0% shipped as a surface, ~100% shipped as data**. *New trigger:* a second adopter report of prose-drift-after-refactor, with the middle shape costed against the full sweep.

This is not a hollow closure claim (T4): the class is closed **over the write path**, not retroactively over existing bytes, and that bound is stated. The retirement's basis and what discharged it are recorded once, in the disposition row above: a shipped verb chain **does** repair a pre-guard corpus, and Increment 5 landed the route the retirement left open. *Trigger:* none — the entry is closed; its standing control (`jigc doc add-item` still answering `write.wrong-shape` at the offending address) reddens if a verb ever becomes what was missing, and the row is re-taken then rather than inherited.
