New capability serving diagnosis convenience (T3), no integrity consequence. *Trigger:* a diagnosis needs the recorded set the finding text does not name.
**Re-counted 2026-08-08 (post-M47, the owed re-count above): we counted the one fired demand, M47 drained it, and we still say no — 0 live diagnoses.** The demand was N1's recovery reading the gitignored `file-state.json` by hand; increment 2 (`fcad40e`) makes the four milestone record-only doors restore the record's pre-image on a rejected commit, so the bricked state that forced it no longer occurs — its suite asserts `jigc validate` at exit 0 free of the wreckage findings — and `d281375` replaces the inapplicable `jigc task discard <task-id>` route on the conflict block with a caller-supplied applicable one. No verb reads the recorded set: the capability is exactly as absent as it was, now with nothing demanding it. **RETIRED** (M46 Settle, 2026-08-18) — five real file-state states each named their path *and* a route that closed it, while the recorded JSON holds only `{path: sha256}`, so there is no diagnosis waiting on a read of it. *Bound, carried rather than dropped:* if entry 9's residue ever produces a baseline nobody wrote, the motive returns. *Trigger:* that bound firing — not a count.
9. **~~Store locking / lost-update semantics — the corruption-removing subset ships at M45, the semantics defer.~~ DISCHARGED — built at M46 Increment 1 (2026-08-18).** The M48 adjudication admitted this **at the falsified predicate**, not at the exclusion: the disposition rested on *"contention, not corruption"*, and driven, a lost baseline **silently disables `reconciliation.conflict-block`** — an out-of-band human edit and a task's staged writes are merged into one commit at exit 0. What shipped: `FileStateRecord` stashes the loaded map as a merge base and `save` merges **base-relative** over `base ∪ ours ∪ theirs`; `engine::state::with_save_lock` runs the save under a stable `<filename>.lock` sibling with a bounded spin that **degrades to unlocked rather than blocking**; `EdgeIndex` takes the lock only (stamp-rebuildable by construction); and `tasks.json`'s four writers gain atomic temp+rename while staying **excluded from the merge on stated evidence** (orchestrator-only callers, a byte-idempotent re-seed, and a registry that a set-union would repopulate with retired ids). Design of record: [storage.md](../design/storage.md) → Concurrent writers. Standing pin: `crates/cli/tests/file_state_merge_hand_off.rs`, `file_state_concurrency.rs`, `milestone_task_list_concurrency.rs`, and the arm-A/arm-B pair in `reconciliation_baseline_contrast.rs`. **Residue, carried rather than closed:** the lock is best-effort by design (its degrade is stated, not hidden), and a baseline lost by any other route — a fresh clone, `jigc unmanage`, a deleted cache — still switches the reconciliation guarantee off; that is the cache's cost, now pinned rather than described. *Trigger:* none — built and closed at Increment 1; the residue above is a pinned bound, not a deferral.

10. **The durable "was provisioned" fact — settled at M47, withdrawn as unbuildable in charter, routed here (2026-07-26).** M47's Increment 3 halt settled a refusal: `milestone finalize` must **refuse when a milestone recorded as provisioned has no live worktrees**, rather than degrade to a docs-only commit at exit 0. The independent pass refuted it as worded: **no such record exists** (`provision_worktrees` persists nothing beyond git's own registration), and a `.jigc`-local marker **fails open by construction** on the strongest instance — a **fresh clone**, where the gitignored workbench is absent by definition (confirmed live: fresh clone → `finalize` → exit 0, zero work landed, the record flipped to terminal `joined`, so the milestone can never be re-finalized). The only fresh-clone-durable home is the committed `milestone-record`, a **frozen, manifest-governed methodology doctype** — `schema-version` 2→3 + a corpus migration + a manifest re-pin: a **one-way door**, and squarely outside M47's *"changes what surfaces say, never what surfaces exist"* boundary. M47 lands the two no-new-state substitutes instead (a refusal on the zero-contribution shape; the landing manifest naming sub-tasks with no provisioned worktree), which close the fresh-clone case but **not** the local post-teardown case where staged docs survive and a partial "2 docs" lands — the residue this entry owns. **RETIRED — residue empty, driven both arms** (M46 Settle, 2026-08-18) — the `cp -R`/`mv` arms fall to the path-subject fix at **zero stored state** (shipped at Increment 2) and the fresh-clone arm **already refuses** at exit 3 naming the cause, so nothing is left for a stored provisioning fact to serve. It keeps a frozen `milestone-record` 2→3 out of the wave **on evidence, not on a count** — and the bump is *buildable* (required + `default:` migrates byte-stable and stays machine-maintained), so this is a refusal on purpose, not on impossibility. *Trigger:* none — re-open only on a **measured** silent-degrade instance that M47's substitutes and Increment 2's path subject both miss.

11. **~~The `discard`-style dirty-worktree guard on `finalize`'s landed teardown — M47 takes the law-1 half, the refusal defers (2026-07-26).~~ DISPOSED at M46 Increment 2 (2026-08-18) — the narration half is built over the whole door axis, the refusal half is *refused on measured evidence*, and the rider is closed.** The finding stands as first recorded: the chain/combine commit only the **staged** set, then `remove_worktrees`' `git worktree remove --force` deletes the whole checkout, so a sub-agent's unstaged and untracked work dies at **exit 0 on the *success* path**; M47 shipped the in-charter law-1 minimum (warn before removing, name the discarded set in the landing manifest) with the bound written down rather than glossed — **visible, not prevented**. **M46 admitted it at the falsified predicate, not at the exclusion** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-08-18, T2 + T3; the driven repros in [razor-ledger.md](../completions/artifacts/M46/razor-ledger.md) → §2f): the exclusion rested on *"for the remainder it narrates"*, and the narration was **false** — a gitignored `secrets.env` was destroyed unnamed at exit 0, and `milestone discard`, `uninstall` and `milestone provision` carried **no narration surface at all** while destroying the same bytes. So `discarded_work` learned the `--ignored=matching` axis, `DESTROYING_DOORS` widened to four with destruction (not refusal) as its membership rule, and all four doors now name what they take. **The refusal half is refused, not deferred a second time.** A provisioned worktree arrives tracked-only while the sub-task walk tells the agent to build the code and run the tests, so `target/`/`node_modules/` sit in the ordinary fan-out **success** path (measured 1 → 4 → 43 entries as the probe widens): a refusal on that axis fires on success and trains `--force` into reflex — strictly worse than no guard — and no mechanical discriminator separates a disposable `target/` from an irreplaceable `secrets.env`. **Declared cost, restated because it is now a decision rather than a gap: the ignored-path loss is made visible, not prevented.** *Re-opening condition:* an adopter reports real work lost to a **narrated** ignored-path teardown — the narration is exactly what makes that reportable rather than invisible, and it is the one datum that would price a refusal against the habituation it costs. *The rider is closed on both halves:* `provision_worktrees`' `remove_dir_all` on a non-registered leftover was fixed at M48 (the fail-closed leftover classifier at the three refusing doors), and its surviving half — the **boundary** still reading the *registered* set, so a `cp -R` repo landed a docs-only commit over live worktrees — at M46 Increment 2, where the subject became the on-disk path. **The `jigc uninstall` half was already closed (2026-08-09, the M47 completion audit)** — its `remove_dir_all(.jigc)` refuses on a dirty `.jigc/worktrees/` with `uninstall.dirty-worktree`, because this entry's own justification (*"on the landed path the staged set … is safely in git; only unstaged/untracked scratch is lost"*) is **false for `uninstall`**, which is not on the landed path at all: nothing landed, so the staged code was reduced to a dangling blob and the sub-tasks' authored prose was destroyed outright ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-08-09). What remains uncounted there is the dangling `.git/worktrees/*` admin record a successful teardown leaves — a separate, non-destructive issue, deliberately left out of that fix. Standing pins: `crates/cli/tests/milestone_teardown_loss.rs` (the narration over all four cells), `crates/cli/tests/flow48_acceptance.rs` arm 1 (the four-door narration cell + the refusing subset), `crates/cli/tests/milestone_path_subject.rs` (the boundary's path subject).

12. **~~`setup --format json` still cannot name the hooks dir (2026-08-08, M47 Increment 10/T2).~~ DISCHARGED at M46 Increment 9 (2026-08-19) — the withheld key shipped at M48, and the declared bound is struck, not carried.** The agent-text summary names the hook path git resolved (`SetupSummary.hook_file`, plumbed from the already-shipping `resolve_hooks_dir`), so a `core.hooksPath` or linked-worktree install stops printing a file that does not exist. The **JSON arm was deliberately left at its four stable keys** (`installed` / `line_file` / `allowlist_file` / `install_commit`) for as long as M47's boundary held: a `hook_file` key is *additive machine surface*, which that wave forbade (*"the wave changes what existing surfaces say, never what surfaces exist"*). **M48's Increment 7 closed the pre-1.0 additive-key window and shipped the key** — with `hook_committed` beside it, so the envelope answers both halves a driver was told to reconstruct: *where the hook landed* and *whether it rides the install commit*. Driven, not relayed: a scratch repo at `core.hooksPath=.githooks` → `jigc setup --format json` prints `"hook_file": ".githooks/pre-commit"` with `"hook_committed": true`, exit 0 (`render.rs`'s install envelope). Fenced, so the key cannot be dropped back out silently: `crates/cli/tests/text_json_parity_axis.rs` — the text/JSON parity axis over `setup`'s envelope, which reddens with *"`jigc setup`'s envelope withholds `hook_file`"* if the key is deleted — and `crates/cli/tests/setup.rs`, which reads it back through the binary. **The bound is struck rather than annotated around:** it was false at HEAD, and a stale bound left standing is what the next planner sizes work against ([razor-ledger.md](../completions/artifacts/M46/razor-ledger.md) → §3). *Trigger:* none — the entry is closed, not re-keyed.

**Planning inputs from the confidence-audit wave (2026-07-24), carried so the Settle sees them:**
 two judgment-tier golden observations ([completions/artifacts/confidence-audit/VERDICT.md](../completions/artifacts/confidence-audit/VERDICT.md) → §5) — bare `jigc start` orientation is byte-invariant across all six fixture states and never names the live task (a discoverability candidate on the entry-2/entry-8 axis, not a law violation), and the degenerate zero-`Spawn:` `milestone-execution` walk now renders pinned-visible in the goldens (literal `<MILESTONE_ID>` placeholders under "one per `Spawn:` line below" — its substance belongs to whichever entry the Settle routes milestone-execution surface work to).

### The rc.8 wave (M44) — the pull tier — ✅ PLANNED 2026-07-21

*(Chartered 2026-07-20, **Settle complete 2026-07-21 — every fork resolved robust**, each argued by an independent robust-advocate with spike evidence, over a four-auditor + four-detector baseline that exercised the real binary at `026efb6` and corrected six charter premises before the human chose (the suffix arm verified-broken and "engages SLUG_RULE_VERSION" false · fork 4's M43-fork-2 mis-citation + the missing-workflow-not-doctype re-frame · the route split can't see the failing address and even hand-substituted points at the wrong target · the wire-argv is an already-tracked deferral · the states-constraints fence is existence-per-code not per-step · "every verb speaks --format json" is Law-1-false at the clap-error surface). **Settled:** fork 1 = the **path-hash derivation** (generalize the empty-slug fallback; drop suffix; task_id log field → 1.1) · fork 4 = a **new dev-pack from-knowledge adr workflow + the byte-floor rider** (both; = migrate-adr minus {{source}}, no schema change) · the route split = **CLI-layer real-address enrichment** to the containing section (only `not-present`) · fork 3 = **build the workflow preview** (compose_core-minus-mint; the capability half of change 2) · D5 = a **`{{schema:<singleton>}}`-derived per-step stated-at fence** (the `authors-into:` marker dropped at Review — circular; single-source-of-truth) · fidelity = boundary-guard (a) + report-split (b) both · item-count rider = in (`doc show` contract-version 4) · fork 2 = **preload-only** · change 1 = a 5th AGENT.md paragraph scoped to successful/validation outcomes · sequencing = **build M44, then the verification trial** · fork 5 = record the direction (structural absence + unseeded probes), mechanism at trial setup only if the M44 fixes don't remove the motive. Full record + rationale + the docs owed: [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-21 M44 planning: the Settle. Post-Settle scope + acceptance: [roadmap.md](roadmap.md) → Milestone 44. Graduated — remove on next pass; the one live deferral it mints is below.)*

- **(D) The `task_id` field on the invocation-log `Record` — DEFERRED to 1.1 at M44 planning (2026-07-21).** Fork 1's path-hash derivation already restores per-doc log attribution *through the argv* (collision-free ids make the id in `argv` a unique per-doc key again), so a first-class `task_id` field is not freeze-forced — and the deferral's basis is now **stated rather than assumed** (M51 / D9): the invocation log is **declared unversioned and additive-only** (`design/measurement.md` → The in-repo invocation log; `invocation_log.rs`'s `Record` doc-comment), which is safe on two legs — it is **rebuildable** (gitignored, opt-in, written by running the binary, so no committed artifact reads it back and no corpus migration is owed) and a new key is **purely additive** (every record carries `binary_version`, so an analysis meets the field exactly on the records the stamping binary wrote, and earlier records stay readable unchanged). No one-way door — but the key set is fenced, so the field joins `Record`'s exhaustive destructure or nothing emits it. *Trigger:* a driver/analysis consumer needs task attribution as a structured key rather than an argv scrape (pairs with the **`route` tagged-union wire-promotion** deferral under *No firm trigger yet* below — both are the invocation/finding surfaces graduating to typed fields).

### The rc.7 wave (M43) — the surface contract — chartered 2026-07-16, **Settle complete 2026-07-16**

*(**All seven (D) forks + five decisions the charter didn't number are settled 2026-07-16 at M43 planning — every fork resolved robust**, each argued by an independent robust-advocate. Full record + rationale: [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-16 M43 planning: the Settle. **Charter corrections from the gap pass:** the L1 rider half-shipped at M42 (exit-3 codes already log; the residual is exit-4 holds + errored verbs) · "extends `command-ref-resolves`" understated the route fence (routes are runtime-formatted strings; the settle is an internal `Route` value, golden-held) · fork 7 over-scoped M30/B1 (only a refuse engages it — the M42 correction re-learned) · forks 3/6 mis-imported A-3 (its scope is agent runtime judgment, not pack metadata) · and three unknown defects joined the scope: the migrate-resume `{{source}}` drop, the `task discard-write` ghost-verb route, and route-less blocking findings. **Settled:** route fence = internal `Route` value + floor widened to blocking gate findings (two exemptions re-affirmed) · A3 = the `{{schema:<doctype>}}` placeholder, unfed-blocks · fork 1 = the full `doc show --task` staged read (R7 revised on its rationale) + the transient staged-read address rider · fork 7 = rung 3, the start-time index snapshot + carryover refuse + `--carry-staged` (finalize.md:121 revised on its changed basis; M42 fork 2 distinguished, not reversed) · fork 5 = drop the `singleton &&` conjunct (create side spike-proven unchanged; M23's review-before-destroy restored) · fork 3 = tiered (seam-gen for slug/heading · structural `states-constraints` front-matter for prose contracts · guide-only for stdin) · fork 6 = shape asserts scoped to shipped packs, when-NOT assert declined · fork 2 = flip `decided-task` + decision-axis catalog · A14 = repo-real paths + file-state stops firing on transients (no seventh target form) · B6 = `doc schema` contract-version 3 · fork 4 = **declined** (M42 fork 6 stands) · riders all pulled (transient staged read · reconcile/back-out docs chapter · spec-router catalog surfacing). Post-Settle scope + acceptance live in [roadmap.md](roadmap.md) → Milestone 43; the laws + style guide live in [design/surface-contract.md](../design/surface-contract.md). Graduated — remove on next pass.)*

**Provenance:** the **lacon trial** — the third adoption corpus and the first combined migration+implementation arc on one repo, run 2026-07-15 on the installed **1.0.0-rc.6** (24 adoptions + four blind handover-driven sessions; 294 logged invocations; record [trial-record](../completions/artifacts/RC-lacon/trial-record.md), 31 claims adversarially verified — 2 refuted — in [findings-verification](../completions/artifacts/RC-lacon/findings-verification.md); [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-15 lacon trial + 2026-07-16 charter). **Zero correctness bugs** — for the first time a trial's findings don't hit the known-hole lens; they hit the **discoverability lens**: the verified through-line is *the capability exists, but no composed surface names it* (the `--task` default, `start --task` resume, `task validate`, the `#type` enum, `doc schema`, `doc list`, `decided-task` — all shipped, all missed), and the classes recur across trials (routing complaint ×3, staged-read ×3, advisory habituation ×3, template-understates-schema ×2). So the wave is **not** a papercut list: it names the class rule first and lands the fixes as instances, per the M42 close-the-class discipline.

**The concept — the surface contract.** Everything jigc prints (composed output, findings, routes, help, author templates, acks) becomes a contracted surface under three laws, each fenced where the surface is *generated* (the M42 lesson binds: a census cannot enforce a predicate — no grep-checklists over prose):

1. **Nothing lies.** Every claim a surface makes is generated from the thing it describes or asserted against it. Templates never hand-enumerate what the schema can project (the "fixed four-part schema" lie → section lists generated from the resolved schema, the M40 fillable-skeleton precedent); every printed path is repo-real or rendered as an identity (`commit:<task> — transient`, never the fictional `docs/commit:*.md` staging key); behavior claims match knobs (the "routes among the workflows" footer for a step that routes nothing). Fence: assert at the render/serialization seam (the M42 closure-claim precedent); strongest form is making the lie unrepresentable (strings built from the schema/`Command` value).
2. **Nothing hides.** Every affordance that is the designated recovery for a state is named by the surfaces that produce that state: finalize/compose footers name `start --task <id>` (resume) and `task validate <id>` (what's-left); `doc show` on a doc with staged edits hints at `task diff`; the composed prose says `--task` is an override with a single-task default; the fresh-authoring steps model `doc author` (today only the migrate templates do). Fences: routes and `Run:` lines must parse against the real CLI (extends the existing `command-ref-resolves` check to routes + help examples); any suppression flag (`selectable: false` and kin) carries a reason + expiry trigger, asserted at pack-load (the slug-rule-fence mold) — the `decided-task` lesson made mechanical.
3. **Nothing ambushes.** Every constraint is stated where it binds, before it can fail: the slot heading-depth ceiling, the `--from-file -` stdin convention, the `--approve` gate + clobber/retire contract, the slug word-cap rule — each named in the template/step/help that solicits the write that would trip it. Fence depth is Settle fork 3 (presence asserted via a declared stated-at mapping vs audit-only; the A-3 bound holds — presence, never content).

**The judgment tier — the surface style guide (the human's addition, 2026-07-16).** For the prose no fence can hold, a written authoring standard per surface kind, so "how to write it" is defined before writing instead of judged after: **workflow descriptions are routing surfaces** — state when-to-use, when-NOT-to-use, and the discriminating trigger axes (records a decision? test-first? touches managed docs? — the exact axes the trial's tester guessed across), the skill-description discipline applied to the catalog; step prose leads with the action, constraints before the solicit; routes name the exact next command for the state at hand; help verbs lead with the one-line common case, contract detail below the fold; advisory wording says what happened + whether to care in the first clause. Ships as a **design part-doc** (candidate `design/surface-contract.md`, owning both the laws and the guide; reading-order slot near `command-output-contract`) + the review checklist the milestone audits check pack-prose changes against. Light fence even here: the `when:`/`usage:` fields' *presence and shape* assert at pack-load; content stays judgment.

**Scope (each fix lands as "law N applied to surface X"; evidence per item in [findings-verification](../completions/artifacts/RC-lacon/findings-verification.md) → the candidate rc.7 scope):**

- **Law-1 batch:** the migrate author-templates state the full resolved schema, generated not hand-written (A3) · `baseline-adopt` + siblings print real paths or typed identities, never staging keys (A14) · the orientation footer stops claiming `start` routes (B1) · `doc create` ack distinguishes created vs already-existed (log surprise 2) · **the finalize manifest labels index-swept files** — a committed file the task never wrote (not a promotion, not in the task working area) says so; deterministic, finalize knows its own set (A6's fix-shaped half — the tester's #1 v1-gate item; the confirm rung is fork 7) · the ingest/`doc list` `unmanaged`/`unregistered` verdicts **bless the plain-file outcome as deliberate** — staying plain is a correct end-state, the route stops implying everything must migrate (A8).
- **Law-2 batch:** un-hide `decided-task` + the catalog decision-axis wording (B12, fork 2) · resume/what's-left affordances named in composed output + finalize text (B4) · the staged-read hint on `doc show` + the misleading clap `--task` tip replaced (B7, fork 1) · `doc list` empty-set line (log surprise 3) · wrong-task-id routes converge on `task list` + a guessed `task status`-class unknown subcommand routes to its real sibling (A1 papercut, log rec 254) · a `doc schema --addresses`-shaped settable-address projection (B6) · `--task`-is-an-override stated in composed prose (B3) · `doc author` modeled in the fresh-authoring steps (B15) · the migrate re-print path — re-invoking a live migration (or `start --task`) re-shows the rewrite instructions instead of erroring (log surprise 1).
- **Law-3 batch:** the constraint-surfacing pass — heading-depth, `--from-file -`, the `--approve`/clobber/retire contract, the slug rule — stated in the surfaces that solicit them (A11/A12/A1) · the same-path clobber route names `--approve` + detects source==destination (A4 — the fired `:194` trigger; the auto-retire half is fork 5) · **the command-ref enum hints actually render** — the methodology command-ref naming the `#type` vocabulary never reaches the composed `Run:` line, so an agent that guesses a valid value never learns it was an enum (B11 residual).
- **Riders (not law instances, same wave):** **`--slug` on `jigc migrate`** (A2 — deletes the onboarding link-repair phase; threaded to the doc mint) · **AGENT.md read-rule crispness** — one sentence defining managed = the `doc list` set → read via `doc show`, everything else read-freely, + the per-tier scoping of the "doc slices" promise (B17/B2) · **`doc --help` verbs lead with the one-line common case** (B8 — the style guide applied to help) · **blocked-finalize/errored-verb finding codes reach the invocation log** (the rc.5 L1 gap, reproduced on rc.6) · the **fence code** itself (seam asserts, route-parse check, suppression-flag reason field, `when:`/`usage:` shape assert).

**Explicitly out:** the full state-aware done/pending render + step cursor (stays [ideas/state-aware-compose.md](../ideas/state-aware-compose.md) — the cheap naming fixes above are the law-2 slice) · intent-matching in the CLI (the agent is the router — no-LLM invariant; [ideas/spec-router-matching.md](../ideas/spec-router-matching.md) surfacing is a candidate rider *only if* Settle pulls it) · the content-coverage fidelity scan ([ideas/migration-content-coverage.md](../ideas/migration-content-coverage.md)) · bulk onboarding ([ideas/bulk-onboarding-flow.md](../ideas/bulk-onboarding-flow.md)) · the milestone-fit cluster ([ideas/sequential-milestone.md](../ideas/sequential-milestone.md)) · issue-tracker/monorepo (parked 2026-07-15) · the reconcile/back-out **user-facing docs** pass (A7/A10 — a MIGRATING.md/QUICKSTART chapter; candidate rider, Settle decides in-wave vs with the 1.0 docs pass).

**The (D) Settle forks (human gate at M43 planning):**

1. **(D) The staged read:** the hint-only floor (a one-line "N staged edits in task X — see `jigc task diff`" on `doc show` + the tip repair) — vs. the full `doc show --task <id>` staged view (and/or a single-address `task diff` filter). Anything beyond the hint **engages the R7 committed-only bound** (rc.5 verification adjudicated it by-design; three consecutive trials have now demanded a staged read — revise the record, don't sidestep it).
2. **(D) The decided-task un-hide shape:** flip `selectable: true` + re-write the catalog one-liners on the decision axis — vs. also folding an optional decision branch into `dev-task` (the tester's alternative), vs. the dev-pack `single-task`'s existing adr-gate being the answer and the methodology catalog just naming it. The recorded `selectable: false` rationale expired — whichever shape, the record gets re-evaluated, not silently flipped.
3. **(D) Law-3 fence depth:** seam-generated constraint text (strongest, most build) — vs. a declared stated-at mapping per blocking write-gate code with a presence test — vs. style-guide + audit only. Calibrate against [cost-of-enforcement](../ideas/cost-of-enforcement.md): the fence must not make pack prose miserable to author.
4. **(D) The changelog `#type == docs` carve-out (B10):** suppressing the granted-and-unused advisory on docs-type commits reads a *field value*, not the diff — deterministic, but it hard-codes "docs commits are never user-facing" as CLI policy. **Engages M42 fork 6's record** (settled 2026-07-13); revise with rationale or decline.
5. **(D) Same-path `--approve` auto-retire (A4's second half):** should `finalize --approve` on a migration whose source *is* the destination retire the original itself, instead of teaching the `git rm`? **Engages the M23 retire-gated-by-approval design and the singleton-only `plan_clobber_guard` bound** (`finalize.rs:357-366`) — a deliberate-bound revision, not a fix; the route-text repair ships regardless.
6. **(D) Style-guide fence depth:** pack-load shape asserts on `when:`/`usage:` (presence, length ceiling, a when-NOT clause?) — vs. pure guidance + the audit checklist. Presence-never-content is the line; decide how much shape is presence.
7. **(D) The fold-in confirm rung (A6's second half):** with the finalize manifest labeled (law-1 ships that regardless), does finalize additionally act when the index carries files the task never touched? Rungs: labeled print only (in scope) · a pre-commit warning advisory · a confirm/`--strict` refuse knob. Anything beyond the label **engages the M30/B1 "surfaced, not prevented" record and M42 fork 2's print-over-refuse settle** — the migration tester ranked this his #1 v1 gate ("why is my ADR commit carrying an unrelated code change?" is the week-one bug report), so the record is engaged with real field weight this time; revise or re-affirm with rationale.

**Sequencing:** ~~M43 is now the last chartered wave before the **1.0.0 call**~~ *(superseded 2026-07-20: the rc.8 wave — M44, the pull tier, above — now precedes the call, and the verification trial gains a clean-room protocol: M44 fork 5)* (the call stays the human's, deliberately open; the repo publishing floor is **decided post-v1**, 2026-07-16 — see the M42-planning (D) entry below). The trial found zero correctness bugs, so this wave is *optimization of the contract surface*, not defect repair — if planning finds the scope ballooning, the fences + law-1/law-2 batches are the load-bearing core and the style guide must ship with them (the guide without fences is advice; the fences without the guide leave the judgment tier undefined — they're one concept). **The rc.7 verification trial carries one designed probe beyond the wave's own findings (human call, 2026-07-16): drive the milestone fan-out unit on genuinely parallelizable work with a live agent spawn** — the unit has never been field-driven on work that fits it (rc.5's probe hit it with sequential work; rc.6's pack-composed path never reaches it — verified: `decompose`/`execute` are serial by design and **no pack surface names `jigc milestone`**, so the unit has zero pack-level on-ramp), and the probe simultaneously discharges the **M39 honest bound** (*"real concurrent fan-out proven by an N-process binary sim, not a live Task-tool spawn — confirm at adoption"*). Unit sound → [ideas/sequential-milestone.md](../ideas/sequential-milestone.md) stays a fit-extension question; unit broken → learned before 1.0. The pack-level on-ramp question itself stays design-sized, out of this wave.

### The rc.6 wave (M42) — ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-14

*(The **M42** rc.6 wave graduated + **shipped 2026-07-14** — chartered 2026-07-12, all twelve Settle forks (six chartered + six the charter didn't have) resolved robust 2026-07-13, 12 increments built + audited + 5 findings fixed, re-verified green ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-14 M42 completion; [completions/artifacts/M42/VERDICT.md](../completions/artifacts/M42/VERDICT.md)). The charter + settled-forks record removed from pending; the still-live deferrals it minted remain below.)*

- **(D) A milestone base re-pin (`jigc milestone rebase`) — NOT BUILT at M42 (2026-07-13).** M42's Review established that **a live milestone is invalidated by any code commit landing after its base** — another milestone's finalize, an ordinary `jigc task finalize`, a `jigc rename`, a human's own `git commit` — and that this is the **M31 worktree-combine guard doing its job**, not a bug ([team-ready-state.md](../design/team-ready-state.md) → the real constraint). M42 fixes the *record-bookkeeping* half (the guard accepts any milestone-record path) and **routes the real constraint honestly** instead of offering `discard` — i.e. "throw the work away" — as the only exit. A **re-pin** (move the shared base to HEAD + re-provision the worktrees) would make the constraint recoverable rather than merely honest, but it is **new capability, not a fix**: a task's footprint is visible to the overlap test, a milestone's is code inside worktrees cut from the base, which `plan_milestone_finalize` **cannot see** (`engine::finalize::decide_base_repin`'s own doc comment records the milestone sibling as *"consciously unchanged"*). Building it in a fix wave, days before the 1.0 call, would be exactly the premature generality the principles warn against. *Trigger:* **a trial in which the base-mismatch block fires on milestone work the operator still wants, and every routed option is unacceptable.** (Until then the routed options — land the milestone first, or re-cut onto the new base — are honest, and both are out-of-band git, which the route now says out loud.)

- **(D) The `spec` lifecycle (criterion status / satisfied-by / open-decisions) — DECOUPLED from M42 at planning (2026-07-13); lands with the post-1.0 doctype-completeness milestone.** Fork 5's *"last cheap window before adopter corpora multiply the migration cost"* premise **self-refutes**: if the window is real, the migration machine has failed its declared purpose (`corpus-migration.md:3` sells `migrate-corpus` as the artifact that **bounds** the freeze's lock-in cost). The answer to a closing window is to **fix the machine that keeps it open** — which M42 now does — not to shove a frozen schema bump through the classifier's holes while it shuts. And the cheap shape available today (`open-decisions` as an optional **slot**) is a **data one-way door**: a slot has no items, so it cannot carry the per-item `resolved-by` ref that is the entire point, and the escape hatch `FixedSlotToRepeatable` **collapses the whole slot into one item named after the section** — per-decision identity and the `resolved-by` edges are never deterministically recoverable. A frozen doctype's permanent content model is **design-workflow work, not fix-wave work**. *Honest cost of the deferral:* three implemented specs assert fixed bugs in present tense under a clean validate; M42's P5 pack fixes address the *process* (the `maps-to-test` demand + open-decision recording), and the lying spec is prose a human can read, not a corrupted store. *Enabled by M42:* with the classifier fixed (`AddedRepeatableSection` + `AddedItemField`), **both** idea files' real shapes become buildable and the bump is a routine `migrate-corpus` run. Promote [ideas/spec-criterion-status.md](../ideas/spec-criterion-status.md) + [ideas/spec-open-decisions.md](../ideas/spec-open-decisions.md) into `design/` when de-parked. **Correction to the record:** `decisions-pending.md`'s deferral of the `spec —decided-by→ adr` edge said *"spec has no field-bearing section"* — **false since M33**; `spec.yaml:24-27` has a working `meta` header with a `ref` field, so `decided-by` is now a one-line delta. *(Bound established at M42 planning: **a NEW doctype costs nothing at 1.0** — it has no corpus to migrate — so the closing-window argument does **not** reach `reference`/`finding`/`postmortem`/`runbook`. They stay deferred, correctly. The window covers only bumps to doctypes that already have adopter instances, and the sweep found **no third** such hole.)*

- **(D) The repo publishing floor — OWED BEFORE THE 1.0.0 CALL, no owning milestone (surfaced at M42 planning, 2026-07-13).** LICENSE, a root README, `publish = false` (or a considered `true`), repo metadata. Recorded at `:179` below with trigger *"first external adopter, **public release**, or opening the repo."* ~~M42 is the **last chartered wave before the 1.0.0 call**~~ *(superseded 2026-07-16: M43 — the rc.7 surface-contract wave above — now precedes the call; the floor decision rides the same point)* — if 1.0 is public, this fires and currently nothing owns it. ~~**Decide at the 1.0.0 call, or charter it.**~~ **DECIDED 2026-07-16 (human): post-v1.** 1.0 ships internal; the floor's original trigger stands unchanged (*first external adopter, public release, or opening the repo*) and fires **after** the 1.0.0 call, not before it. *(Deliberately not folded into M42: it is not a fix-wave item and does not belong in a wave whose claim is about routes telling the truth. Surfaced so the drop is a choice, not an oversight.)*

*(The **(D) managed-docs enforcement hook** (`:181`) is **graduated at M42 planning, 2026-07-13** — its trigger fired ("the RC trials' invocation-log adherence data shows agents bypassing the adapter at a rate that costs correctness"; the impl-rc5 trial record, verbatim: *"the only way to land it was raw `git add` / `git commit` — the exact thing the contract forbids"*). Settled **not** as a hook but as the **cause removed**: fork 1 gives `migrate-corpus` a commit boundary, so the drive-around the trial performed no longer exists. Re-open only if a *new* bypass class appears with the cause intact.)* *(Re-open condition **met** 2026-07-22: the project-alpha-3.0 fan-out session read the managed roadmap with `sed` — a **read-side** bypass class, the 3rd read-side datum and the first *after* the M44 AGENT.md read rule shipped, so the naming fix alone did not hold; **re-Settled 2026-07-23 and deferred to M46** — a read-side guard is new capability, and a bypass that reads a *true* file by the wrong route costs staleness, not corruption; the capability wave above, entry 4.)*

### The rc.5 wave (M41) — ✅ SHIPPED 2026-07-11

*(The **M41** rc.5 wave graduated + **shipped 2026-07-11** — chartered + all five Settle forks resolved robust the same day, built + audited clean + 3 defects fixed (cross-model contract review · slug-idempotency halt · 2 audit LOWs), re-verified **1740/0** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-11 M41 completion; [completions/artifacts/M41/VERDICT.md](../completions/artifacts/M41/VERDICT.md)). The charter + settled-forks record removed from pending; its one live deferral below.)*

- **(D) The per-instance acknowledged-findings ledger — DEFERRED to 1.1 at M41 planning (2026-07-11).** Fork 2's robust arm: a keyed (finding-code + stable target) acknowledge surface, invalidated on target-hash change. Deferred because the undrainable-floor class it closes is **unmeasured today** (V4's Vue fix drains the entire current steady-state floor of 9; every other trial advisory drained by being fixed) and a 1.1 ledger is **purely additive** — no one-way door on the ledger file, so the pin does not force it. The stable finding-key it would build on **does** ship this wave (Fork 1 pins findings-as-data, so the key can't wait). *Design bound when it lands:* home the acknowledge state as a **rebuildable cache** re-derivable from committed content-hashes, never a committed source-of-truth ledger (the `.jigc`-is-workbench / repo-is-record split). *Trigger:* an adopter corpus surfaces a **measured, non-drainable** steady-state advisory floor (an `unsupported-language` stack not on the grammar roadmap, or a human-accepted inverse-cardinality the "fix the cause" path can't drain). *(Trigger-adjacent evidence 2026-07-15, third trial raising the habituation cost: two lacon-trial sessions independently reported "no-op advisories train me to ignore advisories" — `baseline-adopt` fires per first-encountered doc at every finalize, so in trial-shaped usage most advisories ARE no-ops; five codes ship "no action needed" routes. The fictional-staging-path half is fix-shaped separately — [RC-lacon/findings-verification.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-lacon/findings-verification.md) → A14/B9.)* *(Trigger status 2026-07-22, **4th trial — arguably fired as written**: the project-alpha-3.0 migration hit the `title-names-symbol` honest-title floor — renaming to satisfy it "makes the docs worse", i.e. a non-drainable floor, though not either parenthesized example; **re-Settled 2026-07-23 and deferred to M46** — the cheaper drain has not been tried: the F5 option-(a) precision fix may empty the floor the ledger would only acknowledge, so the two Settle together at M46; the capability wave above, entry 3.)*

*(The **M40** rc.4 wave graduated + **shipped 2026-07-11** — chartered 2026-07-10, both charter Settle forks resolved robust at planning, built + audited clean + 4 post-audit findings fixed, re-verified **1676/0** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-11 M40 completion; [completions/artifacts/M40/VERDICT.md](../completions/artifacts/M40/VERDICT.md)). The charter removed from pending; its one live deferral below.)*

Reorder has no commanding route, and the identity invariant only promises id-stability *under* reorder, not a reorder verb; an OOB hand-reorder is already legal (ordering is not identity). *Trigger:* a workflow or trial finding that needs item reordering through the CLI.

The same fork governs the ~33 milestones whose completion facts live only inside DECISIONS entries (blocker 3).

### The post-spine arc (M10–M14) — now scheduled (2026-06-06)

The foundational spine ended at M9; the post-spine cluster that previously had *no home milestone* is now **created and ordered** as the post-spine arc, shaped suggestion-by-suggestion ([roadmap.md](roadmap.md) → The post-spine arc; [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) 2026-06-06). Each is milestone-level only — decomposed when picked up. Mapping:

- **`doc↔code` pack-probe family → M10** — *planned + settled 2026-06-06* ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M10 planning; design elaborated in [validation.md](../design/validation.md) → The `doc-code` probe, [document-type-schema.md](../design/document-type-schema.md) → Pack-declared field types, [worked-examples.md](../design/worked-examples.md) → flow 13). Subprocess-from-the-start + a genuine pack-field-type extension axis; contract *satisfied, not waived* (timeout/crash/malformed-output enforced); OS-level sandboxing + `sandbox-violation` stay deferred (trusted-pack); staleness a stretch; **`add-item` re-deferred** (acceptance rides the editable channel — confirms the line below).
- **`describe` / introspection → M11** — *planned + settled 2026-06-07* ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M11 planning; design promoted to [design/introspection.md](../design/introspection.md), `ideas/describe.md` removed). Open thread resolved: two authored fields (`description:`/`usage:`) on workflows + doctypes (command-refs project their existing `hint`; data-value roots cut), optional/skip-on-absent; cascade reflection by **whole-file definition shadow** (no field-merge — honors [overrides.md](../design/overrides.md)); non-contractual enforced by an operational format predicate (not JSON/YAML, no machine-bindable surface); `jigc describe` whole-menu only. Field-granular override + left-behind pointers deferred (below).
- **Self-hosting → M12** *(exploratory)* — *planned + settled 2026-06-07* ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M12 planning; design promoted to [design/self-hosting.md](../design/self-hosting.md), `ideas/self-hosting.md` removed). Forks settled: **reduced-linear** encode of the **dev-workflow** only (zero engine/dialect code — pure pack authoring); methodology pack **subsumes** the dev surfaces (vendors `commit` + full knob surface) and composes **alone**; success bar = **composition-fidelity + qualitative judgment check** (quantitative build-health comparison deferred — no runnable home); dogfood on a `/tmp` copy of **`gherrink-galey`** (a TypeScript monorepo — unlike jigc). The test-first-ordering + gate + human-gate + fix-loop + owner-artifact degradations are **named as the dialect-extension milestone's triggers**, not built. Still mints the second pack M14 needs.
- **Doctype & surface completion → M13** — `arch-doc` earns its driver + the methodology's own docs (roadmap / deferral-ledger / decisions-log) become managed doctypes; the self-hosting fold-back.
- **Multi-pack composition → M14** — *planned + settled 2026-06-08* ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M14 planning; design promoted to [design/multi-pack.md](../design/multi-pack.md), de-parked from VISION → Open questions). Forks settled: **KEEP-SUBSUME** (thinning is forced out — breaks 7/8 regression tests + compose-alone + the `COMMIT_TYPE` hardcode), so M14 is *compose + collision-adjudicate*; **precedence-override whole-definition** collision resolution per id-space (no new merge primitive); **pack-local include resolution** (a separate correctness rule); **`.jigc/config/packs:` ordered list** with the base pack lowest (pack-precedence is a sub-order *within* pack-default — the team layer stays inert); scope bound = compose + collisions + provenance-of-winner + the single-pack byte-identity floor. The arc's last milestone. Bounded to our-two-packs, **not** a public pack-authoring API.

### The adoption arc (M15–M17) — now scheduled (2026-06-08)

Created after the post-spine arc closed, on the human's direction (multi-domain composition proven → the single-domain framing dropped from [VISION.md](../VISION.md); build the two self-hosting-completion prerequisites, then dogfood-and-measure). Milestone-level only — decomposed at pickup. The **M15/M16 boundary** is now settled (2026-06-09): M15 shipped the increment-workflow encode (the `checkpoint` step kind); the planning/completion encodes moved into **M16**, which **fuses** them with the working-doc → doctype graduation (earn-from-driver). Mapping:

- **Workflow-dialect extension → M15** — *planned + settled 2026-06-09* ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M15 planning). Slice = **encode the increment-workflow only** (planning + completion deferred). Net engine delta = **one** primitive: the **`checkpoint` step kind** (triggers #2 + structural-#4 unified — always-composes, emits a `Checkpoint:` halt directive the agent honors; halting, not branching; convention-honored, no runtime). #1's A==A lint, #3's gate-command knob, and #5's owner-artifact gate are **deferred** (no target / prose-is-better / completion-driven). The fix-loop's bound + re-run stay orchestration-level on the stateless restart-from-scratch resumption. **Built ahead of the dogfood — prereq #1.**
- **Close the self-hosting loop → M16** — *planned + settled 2026-06-09* ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M16 planning; design promoted to [design/methodology-docs.md](../design/methodology-docs.md), wired into the reading order before `multi-pack`). Forks settled: **authoring spine = `creates-task: true, selectable: false`** (the `sub-task` precedent — mint a task, author via the proven create-gate, off-router; spiked against the binary), so the gap-detectors' no-task-authoring crux dissolves; **Full-4 doctypes** (`roadmap` + `deferral-ledger` + `decisions-log` + `completion-record`) in the **methodology pack**, proven on **fresh instances** (jigc's own docs migrate at v1); `decisions-log` and `completion-record` are two distinct doctypes; the roadmap is **one-level** (increments-as-prose — two-level repetition stays deferred below); **build repeatable-section conformance + ref-edges** (`validate.rs`/`index.rs` lift); **idempotent create** (copy-in on existing → create-or-update + clobber fix); **#5 owner-artifact gate = a finalize-time presence check** (pure `exists(path)`, intrinsic, +1 across the severity lists). The single-agent-spine bound recorded (multi-agent recon/review/audit stays orchestration-level). Prereq #2, ahead of M17.
- **Dogfood + measurement → M17** — *planned + settled 2026-06-12* ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M17 planning; design home created at [design/measurement.md](../design/measurement.md)). Forks settled: **pilot-first** (galey pilot + A/B control behind a four-condition pilot gate, then lacon existing-with-docs, then a human-driven greenfield); **capture = hooks + three soundness increments** (finalize-success findings emission · absorb baseline-advance at landed finalize · validation-blocked exit code); **build-health = written counting protocol** (protocol-counted, not engine-emitted — the M12-deferred quantitative comparison homes here as protocol, its hand-run-trend *comparator* framing **superseded by the A/B control**); **net-new `dogfood-record` doctype** + `record-dogfood` workflow (spiked green through the binary at planning); **pre-fix run-breakers only** (planning finalize tail · base-mismatch re-pin · `--task` in composed commands · #5 omission-bypass · the spike-found artifact false-rename block · the stale QUICKSTART, case-3's verbatim fixture); the **seeded-failure obligation** per measured run. The settled design survived the independent pre-decompose review (14 findings, all baked — [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) 2026-06-12). Remaining gaps fill **need-driven** from what the dogfoods surface as blocking.

**Still genuinely unscheduled (deferred beyond both arcs):**
- **Multi-assistant adapter profiles — split out of M20 into its own future milestone (2026-06-14).** The adapter ships only the Claude Code profile (deferred since M9, [VISION.md](../VISION.md) → Open questions). M20 planning scoped it in, then the gap probe found it is **reach, not hardening**, the largest of the three basket items, and carries real design risk: the profile *model* is already data-driven (a second assistant is "a YAML + wiring"), but four points are hardcoded to Claude Code — **G1** no assistant selector (`const SETUP_ASSISTANT`, no `jigc adapter install --assistant` verb); **G4** `reference.to`/`syntax` are parsed-but-**ignored** (`inject_reference` hardcodes `@.jigc/AGENT.md`); **G3** the hook target reuses `allowlist.file` (needs a `HookTarget.file` schema field for an assistant whose hook config ≠ allowlist file); **G2** the spawn wrapper is dead-code (`render_spawn`), the emit path hardcodes the bare payload. *Two forks owed at that milestone's Settle:* **which** second assistant (a human pick — its profile content is unknowable until named, and it must be drivable in-env), and the **success bar** (install-fidelity vs a real-agent behavioral loop — the M19 lesson: "a second profile installs" is a *capability* win, not a *comparative* one). Sharpest risk: the chosen assistant's spawn primitive may **reopen the locked M8 launch-template lexical rule** (`validate_spawn_template` is Claude-one-line-shaped). Design home: [assistant-adapter.md](../design/assistant-adapter.md) (aspirational today — `--assistant`/`regenerate` verbs + a concrete second profile both unspecified). *Trigger:* a milestone is created for it (no firm number; post-M20).
- **`arch-doc` create-or-update-via-bind (the "living" maintenance path) — partially resolved at M16 planning (2026-06-09).** M16 builds **idempotent create** (a `create` on an already-committed slug copies-in the committed doc instead of clobbering it — killing the silent-overwrite landmine the M16 baseline found, and giving **create-or-update** for free: a workflow always "creates" then `add-item`/`set-slot`-edits, re-promoting the committed doc through finalize byte-stable — [methodology-docs.md](../design/methodology-docs.md) → engine work). So re-authoring a committed persisted doc *through a workflow* is now exercised (M16's running-doc doctypes). **What stays deferred:** the heavier `bind`→re-slot front-matter combination (`allows-create` + a *required self-`reads:` role*) — M16's maintained-doc edit is a **direct-address** `add-item`/`set-slot` (copy-in is automatic), needing no bound read-role, so the required-read-role gate is not built. *Trigger (narrowed):* a workflow that must re-author a committed doc via a *bound* read-role (not direct address) — pairs with the **required read-role finalize gate** below. **Re-confirmed OUT at M26 planning (2026-06-18): does NOT fire** — arch-doc *migration* creates a fresh doc from a foreign file (never re-opens a committed arch-doc via a bound read-role), so the trigger is untouched; named here to guard against scope-creeping the bind+re-slot combination into the arch-doc milestone.
- **slug-as-H1 (the title→H1 round-trip) — RESOLVED, fixed 2026-06-18** (keyed at M26 planning, fixed the same day in the post-M26 shakedown pass; commit `f06ec5e`). `create`/migration now render the **original title** in the H1 (`# Use MySQL`) while the **id stays the slug** (`adr:use-mysql`, filename `decisions/use-mysql.md`) — derived by `slugify(H1)`, so addressing + the stable-id invariant are unchanged. The "wide golden blast radius" that deferred it three times was **overstated**: only 2 engine assertions flipped (the e2e flows assert prose + byte-stability, not literal H1s). Round-trip `render(parse(x)) == x` holds incl. punctuation; collision-suffix still keys on the slug. Full record: [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-18 post-M26 shakedown.
- **`adr.cites-code` migration (the "separate cross-code pass") — recorded UNOWNED at M26 planning (2026-06-18).** M25's `migrate-adr` guidance explicitly defers the `cites-code` code-anchor ("a separate cross-code pass", `author-migration-adr.yaml`), so no migration authors a *resolving* `adr→code` anchor. M26 builds the code-anchor-on-migrated mechanism for **arch-doc** (`implemented-by`) but deliberately keeps `adr.cites-code` migration out (M26 = arch-doc only, the roadmap's stated scope). Its natural sibling, left with no owning milestone. *Trigger:* a milestone that migrates ADRs *with* their code anchors, or a flow that re-authors a committed adr's `cites-code` — whichever comes first.
- **Doctype-expansion milestone (G2) — ✅ SHIPPED as M22 (2026-06-14).** Built + audited clean (2 audit findings auto-fixed); the `changelog` doctype + `record-change` driver + `single-task` fold-in ship, and all four latent engine capabilities below are built. The rest of the project-doctype set stays **mapped, not built** ([doctype-map.md](doctype-map.md) → candidate set). *Honest bound holds:* Flow-A only — an existing `CHANGELOG.md` still routes `needs-reconcile` until auto-migration (G1, the next split-off milestone). See [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-14 M22 completion. *(Graduated — remove on next pass.)*
- **Auto-migration milestone (G1) — ✅ PLANNED as M23 (2026-06-14); graduates at completion.** The **transform** arm of existing-project ingestion: *rewriting* a non-conformant foreign doc into conformant shape + adopting it (vs the bounded **detect-and-route**, which classifies + routes but never rewrites — [project-setup.md](../design/project-setup.md) → Flow 2), so an existing project's foreign docs become **trackable** (the G2 doctypes are its migration *targets* — hence it came after). **Changelog only** at M23 (live Flow-B demand); M24 hardened that changelog reference; the location-bearing doctypes `adr`/`spec`/`prd` generalize at **M25** (planned 2026-06-17), and `arch-doc` migration + code-inferred docs are **M26+**. Design home: [design/auto-migration.md](../design/auto-migration.md). **The charter's original "relax the determinism boundary / needs fuzzy heading-mapping machinery" framing is SUPERSEDED** — settled as **Framing A** (boundary INTACT): the **LLM rewrites** the foreign prose through the existing write verbs, the **CLI strict-parses + conformance-gates + adopts iff conformant** (the strict parser stays the sole structural authority; **no CLI fuzzy-mapping is built**; [VISION.md](../VISION.md#the-determinism-boundary) table unchanged). *Forks settled at M23 Settle ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-14 M23 planning):* mechanism surface = a `jigc migrate <path> --as <doctype>` verb + a CLI-owned source seam (the only surface that reaches a root `Unmanaged` foreign file); foreign-source retirement = a CLI-owned retire step in finalize, gated by the review approval (jigc's first byte-destructive op); enforce the `enum` on `id-from` fields; migration-quality measure = a small real corpus + three metrics (round-trip conformance · fidelity-acceptance · content-preservation). *(Was paired with G2 at M21 planning; split 2026-06-14. See [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-14 split + M23 planning.)*

### Document shapes — discuss when a workflow drives each

The doctype/relation/grammar shapes deliberately left un-schema'd until a workflow creates or reads them ([doctype-map.md](doctype-map.md) → Unsettled; [document-type-schema.md](../design/document-type-schema.md) / [structural-grammar.md](../design/structural-grammar.md) opens). Each earns its schema *with its driving workflow* — defining one earlier is generality for a single-use thing ([PRINCIPLES](../PRINCIPLES.md)). Most are gated on a workflow that doesn't exist on the M1–M9 spine, so they cluster as "discuss when that workflow is designed." *(Recovered 2026-06-04 by mining DECISIONS + the design opens — the "document shapes we meant to discuss." The discussions themselves are a focused [design-workflow](design-workflow.md) session each, one at a time; this log only ensures they resurface.)*

- **`prd` doctype** — *settled M9* (2026-06-06): driven by the new-project idea-development setup workflow, schema earned there with **fixed prose slots** ([project-setup.md](../design/project-setup.md) → Flow 1; [doctype-map.md](doctype-map.md)). **Repeatable-requirements — GRADUATED, built at M25** (2026-06-17): the prd-migration driver earns the per-requirement structure, so `requirements` becomes a **repeatable section** (per-requirement `title` field + `statement` slot; pack-only — `add-item` M13 + multi-word-heading M22 cleared the blockers). The `prd —decomposes-into→ spec` edge **stays deferred** (needs a new header section + re-incurs the ref-shape landmine, no migration driver) — see [auto-migration.md](../design/auto-migration.md) → prd. *(Graduated — remove on next pass.)*
- **`arch-doc` doctype** (living architecture documentation) — **settled at M13 planning** (2026-06-07): schema earned from the **architecture-documentation workflow** ([architecture-documentation.md](../design/architecture-documentation.md)) — a doc-level `overview` slot + n→n `cites → adr` header ref + a **repeatable `components` section** (`description` slot + `implemented-by` `symbol-exists` code-anchor). Built in M13; design locked, not yet built.
- **`spec —decided-by→ adr` edge** (n→n) — a spec pointing at the decisions that shaped it. Deliberately **cut from M3** as single-use generality ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md):1250); **re-triaged OUT at M13 planning** (no driver in M13's arch-doc scope); **re-confirmed OUT at M25 planning** (2026-06-17) — a spec migration carries decision links as prose, not parseable ids, and the edge needs a *new header section* (spec has no field-bearing section) + re-incurs the ref-shape landmine, so it stays a multi-line schema delta when a spec-reading flow earns it. *No firm trigger.*
- **(Inline references ("mentions") in slot prose — SETTLED + built at M33 Inc-3, 2026-06-24.** The `schema-conformance.mention-resolves` check: a managed mention `#<type>:<slug>` (known doctype) in slot prose that names no committed doc is reported — advisory, store-scope, never a finalize gate; a bare external `#issue-42` is never flagged. [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-24 In-prose mentions; [document-type-schema.md](../design/document-type-schema.md) → In-prose mentions. No longer pending.)*
- **Multi-level repetition** — a second repeatable level (repeatable-inside-repeatable), today **not expressible** (`schema.rs` `Leaf = Slot | Field`, no `Leaf::Repeatable` — an *absence*, not a policy cap; confirmed at M16 planning) ([structural-grammar.md](../design/structural-grammar.md) opens). *Condition:* fires behind whichever doctype first needs nesting. **M16's `roadmap` is the first real consumer and routed around it** (milestone→increment is two-level, so the roadmap doctype carries the increment cut as **prose in a `decomposition` slot**, one level — [methodology-docs.md](../design/methodology-docs.md)), exactly as `prd`/`arch-doc` routed around it; still deferred until a doctype genuinely needs structured nested items. **GRADUATED — built at M22 (2026-06-14)** — `changelog`'s `release → change-group` nesting is the consuming target; built as `Leaf::Repeatable` general recursion (heading-depth = 2+nesting), graduates on build; the cap is **2 nesting levels**, derived from the **address hop budget** since M49 — M22 declared the number against the heading ceiling and the loader drifted from what the write path would accept ([structural-grammar.md](../design/structural-grammar.md) → Repetition) ([design/changelog.md](../design/changelog.md) → engine work #1).
- **Multi-slot sections + their rendering** — multiple prose slots per section and how they render under sub-labels ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md):374). **Partially graduated at M16 (2026-06-09):** the **repeatable-item** case is **built** (a roadmap milestone entry carries `proves` + `decomposition` slots) — each slot renders under a `#### <Leaf-Title>` sub-heading when an item template has >1 slot, bare-prose when exactly one (backward-compat); single-word leaf ids route around the multi-word reparse defect ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M16 build halt). The **Simple-section** multi-slot case **stays deferred** — no driver. *Trigger (narrowed):* a Simple (non-repeatable) section needs multiple prose slots.

### No firm trigger yet — condition-keyed (revisit when the condition arrives, not on a date)

Tracked so they're not lost, but honestly *not* milestone-scheduled — assigning each a milestone now would be false precision:

- **~~The planning gate-record doctype (pack-shaped D)~~ — ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-30 (M49 Increment 9, T5–T7).** The trigger fired at the M49 Settle (D9) and the doctype landed at the shape this entry always specified: `packs/methodology/schemas/planning-record.yaml` is **one required slot per gate** and nothing else, homed at its own `planning-records/`; `planning.yaml` gained the `allows-create` role and an author step that renders `{{schema:planning-record}}`, so a fifteenth gate would compose itself rather than owing fourteen hand-listed `set-slot` lines; and the gate set got **one fenced home** — [methodology-docs.md](../design/methodology-docs.md)'s table, with `crates/cli/tests/planning_gate_home.rs` deriving the ids from the shipped schema and reddening on a second checklist. The **A-3 bound stands unchanged**: jigc checks a slot is filled and never reads, scores or lints the judgment inside it. **The capability wave's re-key is overruled with its reason, not quietly dropped** — a repeatable `gates` section has no minimum-item fence (`min-items` was rejected as a frozen trap; `repeatable-populated` fires only at **zero** items, is advisory and is store-scope only), so a record carrying 3 of 14 would finalize clean, deleting `required-slot-present`, which is the doctype's entire forcing function; the bump-per-new-gate cost is accepted instead. **The entry as it stood, kept for its reasoning:** the consolidated plan-time forcing-functions, specified pack-shaped ([methodology-docs.md](../design/methodology-docs.md) → The planning gate-record): a per-milestone `planning-record` doctype, one required presence-slot per gate (judgment-content agent-authored, never scored — the A-3 bound). Run by hand from that table today; **encode owed at the self-hosting planning-workflow re-encode / v1 migration** (the M16 planning encode predates it). *Trigger:* the planning workflow is (re-)encoded into the methodology pack, or the v1 in-place migration ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-21 Direction). Pairs with the in-place-migration deferral below. *(First **field** demand 2026-07-15: the lacon trial's milestone session asked unprompted for a `task gate-cleared` stamp — "nothing recorded that the Settle gate was cleared … auditable rather than trust-based" — [RC-lacon/findings-verification.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-lacon/findings-verification.md) → B14.)* *(2nd–4th field demands 2026-07-22: three project-alpha-3.0 sessions independently — the planning session's checkpoint sail-through risk + the cross-session resume hole, the completion session's machine-readable `checkpoints:[]` ask, the adversarial session's "finalize proceeded straight past it" — [RC-alpha3/findings-verification.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-alpha3/findings-verification.md) → §3-C; **re-Settled 2026-07-23 and deferred to M46** — this entry's trigger is untouched (no M45 increment re-encodes the planning workflow) and a sailed-past checkpoint costs process discipline, never a byte; the capability wave above, entry 2.)*
- **Delta discipline for adapter-owned artifacts — a declared deviation from principle #5, recorded 2026-08-14 (M48 Increment 10).** The adapter's owned guide artifact ([assistant-adapter.md](../design/assistant-adapter.md) → The adapter's owned artifacts) **detects** a user-modified copy and refuses to clobber it, raising the advisory `adapter-guide.user-modified` with a route at both `jigc setup` and `jigc upgrade`. That is a **detector with no delta** — it does not record the user's change as a delta against a known base, re-apply it over a new version, or classify it clean/conflict/orphaned — so it is a deviation from *"all customization is a recorded delta against a known base version, never an untracked fork"*, not a small instance of it. Deliberate at **one** artifact, where the whole file is the unit and *"keep yours, or delete it and re-run `setup`"* is a complete answer; the machinery a delta needs (a per-artifact base record, a three-way re-apply, a conflict surface) would be built for a set of one. *Trigger:* **a second adapter-owned artifact ships** — at two the "whole file is the unit" answer stops scaling (a user who edits one wants the other refreshed, and a partial refresh has no vocabulary), and the deviation must either earn the cascade's delta discipline or be re-argued in writing. The Settle's own framing was corrected on exactly this point: refuse-to-clobber was proposed as *"the minimal honest form"* of principle #5 and is not ([settle-record.md](../completions/artifacts/M48/settle-record.md) → Accepted as corrections).
- **The item-field locator scans lines, and the guard blocks rather than corrects — recorded 2026-08-28 (M49 Increment 1 T5).** `write::set_item_field` finds its bullet with a **line scan** over the item's own (now schema-keyed, widened) leaf region, so a slot-prose line shaped like a field bullet (`- status: …`) is matched ahead of the real field group. T5 gives the item-field paths a validate-after confinement target — the item's own field-group list — so the write is now **rejected** `write.target-escape` with nothing persisted, instead of acking at exit 0 over bytes spliced into prose. **The locator itself is unchanged**: bounding the scan to the field-group list would let the write land correctly, but that is T1's region/locator concern rather than the guard's, and the acceptance this task was given pins the rejection. Cost while it stands: an agent whose slot prose shadows a declared key cannot set that field until it edits the prose. *Trigger:* the reject is observed on a legitimate corpus — a trial session, an adopter, or this repo's own port — at which point the locator is scoped to the field group and this arm's assertion flips from *rejected* to *landed correctly*.
- **A slotless item models no prose, and the cold-fill re-render drops what sits there — recorded 2026-08-28 (M49 Increment 1 T6).** Driven on a manufactured `{slotless, ¬nested}` item block: an out-of-band `#### Stray heading` added inside the item **parses clean** (the template reserves nothing at that depth, so no conformance code fires and `jigc validate` is silent), and the next `jigc doc set-field` on that item — the cold-fill arm, which re-renders the item whole — **removes it at exit 0**. This is *inside* the sanctioned item-bytes exception's declared bound ([parsing.md](parsing.md) → *The item-bytes path is the one sanctioned exception*: the regenerated region is exactly one item, and children and sibling leaves survive `render_item`'s recursion — bytes no leaf models are not promised), so it is a **hole in the parse-side contract, not a broken write guarantee**: the open question is whether content no leaf models is conformant at all. Deliberately **not pinned** in `item_region_shape_space` — a standing test over today's behaviour would pin the loss as expected output, the same reason M48's latent-surface sweep left the leftover re-`provision` out. *Trigger:* a doctype ships a **slotless** item block whose instances carry authored prose (an adopter's schema, or this repo's own port), **or** a second instance of unmodeled-bytes loss is observed on any shape — at which point the parser owes a located finding for content outside every declared leaf, and the write path inherits the guarantee for free.
- **The added optional slot at the *section* locus classifies nothing, and `diff_section`'s own comment blesses it — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 4 planning).** Driven at that increment's basis: `adr` bumped 2→3 with the `options` slot absent from the v2 snapshot yields `migrate-corpus.unclassified-change` at exit 1 — an added **optional** slot on an *existing simple section*, riding alone, classifies as nothing and bricks the migration — while `diff_section`'s comment asserts the opposite (*“An added optional slot leaves existing docs conformant, so it needs no transform”*), which is true only while the slot rides beside a classified change. Increment 4 closed the **item** locus (`item_field`'s filter) and named this sibling as a different class at a different seam, with no in-wave bump needing it. *Trigger:* it fires itself at the author's own gate — the next frozen-doctype bump that adds an optional slot to a simple section with nothing else in the diff is refused at the `migrate-corpus` door with `migrate-corpus.unclassified-change`, exit 1, which is *build the kind first* enforced rather than written down ([doctype-authoring.md](doctype-authoring.md) → The transform-kind coverage matrix, its ⛔ rows). A standing test over today's behaviour is deliberately **not** written: it would pin the brick as expected output, the same reason the slotless-item row above stays prose.
- **Whether a `ProseNeeding` rollback leaves a route anyone can follow — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 4 planning).** Driven at the section locus: the doc is restored **byte-identical** and left stamped **below** the current version, so the *author the prose, then re-run* route may not be runnable from the state the rollback leaves. A **pre-existing property of the shipped kind**, inherited by Increment 4 rather than introduced, and stated so it is not silently absorbed by the increment that met it. *Trigger:* the first shipped `ProseNeeding` bump against a real corpus — whichever comes first, that or a field report that the stated route does not run.
- **An empty `--task ""` is admitted once a live task exists, and the ack's route is unrunnable — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 5 planning), re-driven at HEAD with the precondition the first record omitted.** In a repo with **no** task, `jigc doc create adr --title X --task ""` answers ``no task `` — list live tasks with `jigc task list` `` at exit 1, which is correct; with **one live task** the empty id is admitted and reaches a route built from it — on the debug build that aborts at `finding.rs`' `Route::mechanical` argv fence (``argv ["jigc", "task", "discard", ""]``), and on release (recorded at Increment 5, not re-driven here) it prints `jigc task discard ` at exit 0. It is a different class at a different seam — task-id **admission**, not addressing — which is why the capability batch that found it did not fold it in. *Trigger:* the task-door admission axis is next opened (a wave that touches `--task` acceptance at the `doc` verbs). **FIRED and DISCHARGED at the M50 Settle, 2026-09-04 (D2).** M50 Tier 0 *is* that wave, and the entry was cited nowhere in the charter, handover or baseline — found by the gap pass. The *deliberately prose, not a test* clause **retires**: M50 converts it, so the standing test pins the **refusal**, not the unrunnable route. Recorded four days before the trial that reported the same class as new.
- **The `doc author` payload grammar has no `slug:` key — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 5 T4).** `jigc doc add-item --slug` ships, so an item's id can be decoupled from its title, and the batch's own collision refusal names it as the escape — but the payload itself cannot carry one, so a batch that would mint a colliding item has to drop to the single-item verb. Deliberately not shipped: the roadmap deliverable was the flag on `add-item`, and a payload key is a **grammar** addition to a pinned write surface. *Trigger:* a composed author step needs to mint an item whose id must diverge from its title **inside** one batch — the escape reported as unreachable from a payload, not merely as unavailable.
- **Each authored leaf still re-reads the whole document — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 5 T6).** The batch stopped re-parsing per **parse site**; what remains is one whole-buffer read per **leaf**, so n leaves over an O(n) document stay O(n²). Stated with its numbers where an adopter meets it ([MIGRATING.md](../MIGRATING.md) → *What migration does not give you*), because every one of the twelve `migrate-*` workflows authors its whole document in one payload and a several-thousand-item source is still minutes. *Trigger:* a real corpus makes one `doc author` batch the migration's wall — the measured kind, not the projected kind; the increment's growth-ratio assertion (never a wall-clock ceiling) is what would make an improvement checkable.
- **A hand-authored `packs.yaml` loses its comments and layout when the marker is written — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 6 T2).** `jigc setup` now writes `compose-embedded-methodology` through the same parse-mutate-serialize the function already used, so the `packs:` entries survive in order and content (asserted on the emitted bytes, not on a re-parse agreeing with itself) while comments and layout are re-emitted canonically. Recorded on the function; preserving them needs a comment-preserving YAML round-trip that is not in the dependency set. *Trigger:* an adopter's `packs.yaml` carries comments that matter, or a comment-preserving round-trip enters the dependency set for another reason and makes it free.
- **A project pack is a fork, not a subclass: every reused base step, command-ref and field-type must be vendored — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 6 T4, bound 1).** Body-references resolve against the **defining workflow's own pack** with no fallback — driven at that increment, a house workflow reusing `step:locate` composes only after `config/commands.yaml` *and* the step files are copied into the house tree — and a vendored copy does not track the base pack's upgrades. The fix is **not** a fallback, which is the M3-class hazard the pack-local rule exists to prevent, but an *extend-a-step* primitive nothing has earned; the bound of record is [multi-pack.md](../design/multi-pack.md) → Declared bounds of a project pack. *Trigger:* a project pack in real use goes stale against a base-pack upgrade its vendored copies missed, or a second adopter asks to extend a step rather than copy it.
- **The placement/home-read rule's only enforcement is that a future author remembers to read it — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 7 T5).** Its fifth application found **eleven** production home reads listed nowhere: none a live hole, but seven arrived after the fourth application's own commit with no entry recorded, and an eighth (`cli::milestone::record_only_range`) is an *ancestor* of the commit that certified no production miss — so that clean diff was not clean. The increment answered with two structural corrections (both lists re-keyed on the **function**, since nearly every M42 `file:line` had rotted; residue class (b) widened from `Finding::location` to any `Location` read, which is the only way `engine::finalize`'s six `Some(unit.location())` constructors are reachable) — and **the deliberate non-lint stands**. *Trigger:* the next application finds a **live** hole rather than an unlisted read; the non-lint decision is then what gets re-argued, not the census.
- **A join-suffixed instance's gate-repair route is left exactly as it was — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 8 T3).** The milestone door's conformance block now scopes its route to the **contributing** sub-task (driven: the scoped route repairs the sub-area and re-running `jigc milestone finalize` then lands). A join-suffixed instance (`adr:foo-2`) is an address its own area does not stage, so the staged-body check leaves that one route unscoped rather than making it confidently wrong. *Trigger:* a milestone finalize blocks on a join-suffixed instance in real use and the unscoped route is reported as unfollowable.
- **`jigc ingest` still adopts a file whose identity no door accepts — recorded 2026-09-05 (M50 Increment 2 T1).** The address slug-head guard makes a hand-dropped `docs/research/OddName.md` unaddressable at all nine address doors, and T1 swept the **store** surfaces onto one answer for it (`jigc doc list` → `unregistered`, `jigc validate` → `schema-conformance.unadopted-instance` with the adoption route, the un-baselined promise suppressed) by giving the shipped managed-vs-foreign discriminator an identity leg. **`jigc ingest` has its own classifier** (`conformance_gate`, not `is_unadopted_foreign`), and driven at T1 it still verdicts that file `adoptable — adopted, indexed + baselined, no file moved`: it registers an identity nothing can address. Not folded in — the ingest funnel is a different door with a different subject, is named in neither the increment's grouped scope nor its file list, and the shipped `adoption_route` already names a repair that **does** work (`jigc migrate <path> --as <ty>`, driven: it lands the doc at a slugged path). *Trigger:* the ingest funnel is next opened, or a corpus is observed where `ingest` registered an unaddressable identity and a later door refused it — at which point the question to settle is whether `ingest`'s adoptable verdict owes the same identity leg or whether registration is deliberately looser than addressing.
- **`engine::validate::adoption_route` emits an unquoted path token, so a spacey foreign file aborts `jigc validate` on a debug build — recorded 2026-09-05 (M50 Increment 2 T1), driven at `23487ab` and therefore pre-existing.** With `docs/decisions/Some Old Doc.md` committed, `jigc validate` panics at `finding.rs`'s `Route::mechanical` argv fence — *"token `docs/decisions/Some Old Doc.md` is not shell-safe as emitted … must be rendered through `crate::task::shell_token`"* — exit 101; on release the fence is compiled out and the route prints as `jigc migrate docs/decisions/Some Old Doc.md --as adr`, which pasted into a shell runs against `docs/decisions/Some`. A **law-2** breach at a shipped route, independent of this task's change (the same path reproduces it at the parent commit), and the reason T1's disposition fixture uses space-free names rather than the space-carrying one its axis drives. Not fixed here: `adoption_route` lives in the engine and `shell_token` in the CLI, so the repair is a seam decision this task has no mandate for. *Trigger:* the M50 completion audit's triage, or the first adopter corpus carrying a foreign file whose path has a space — whichever comes first.
- **Three facts `design/bootstrap.md` draws in its orientation examples are still unbuilt — recorded 2026-09-05 (M50 Increment 5 T2).** The active-task view ships without the header's `Branch: main (HEAD a3f9c2)` segment, without the clean state's `Recent: N finalizations on this branch · last: …` line, and without the active block's `step N/M (<step>)` progress. Each needs a read this view does not make: two `git` reads (current branch + HEAD; the finalize history over the branch) and a per-task **step cursor**, which nothing persists — a task's working area records its workflow, not where in it the agent is.
