The doc now says so where it draws them, instead of the render's own doc-comment carrying the reason, which is how the last deferral here rotted: `render.rs`'s deferral rested on *"no task store yet"*, false since M1, and that expired premise is why the whole active-task state went unbuilt for 49 milestones while a machine contract called those repos `clean`. *Trigger:* an orientation reader is observed acting on stale branch/HEAD state (the two git reads), **or** a wave gives a task a persisted step cursor for any other reason (the progress fragment falls out of it and is not worth minting state for on its own).
- **A `--task` read of an address that resolves nowhere is byte-identical to the task-less one, says *committed*, and its route drops `--task` — N15, recorded 2026-09-07 (M50 Increment 13 T2), driven at HEAD.** Over a task in which `vision:vision` is genuinely staged, `jigc doc show 'vision:wrong-slug' --task <id>` and the task-less form emit the **same bytes** — a `store.not-found` block saying the address *"names no **committed** doc"*, routed *"read `vision:vision`"*. Following that route verbatim runs `jigc doc show vision:vision` **without** `--task`, which serves the committed prose while the reader is holding a staged copy — driven end to end (the staged thesis said one thing, the route's output another). The sibling code is already right: an address that resolves *committed but not staged* answers `store.not-staged` with a route that explains which copy it serves. So the defect is one arm of one resolver, on a **1.0-pinned** read surface: the word *committed* is wrong under `--task`, and a route that silently changes which copy it reads is law 2's *nothing hides* at the last inch. **[Discharged 2026-09-16 (M51 Increment 11, T4):** **fixed at M51 Increment 5 / T6** — the `--task` read miss now names the copy it looked in and keeps `--task` in its route, so the word *committed* is gone from that arm and the route no longer changes which copy it serves. The trigger below **fired on its first clause** (the `doc show` resolver's miss arms were opened) and is therefore **spent, not live**. This row is the rc.14 trial's **F-7**, whose conversion ledger entry was converted from `UNPINNED:` to `pinned-by: staged_read_miss_arm::the_task_read_miss_names_the_staged_copy_and_keeps_the_task_in_its_route` in the same wave ([findings-verification.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-rc14/findings-verification.md) → F-7).**]** Not fixed at Increment 13: the close increment carries no tier, and every M50 tier had landed — recording it under T2's own hard bound rather than widening the close. *Trigger:* the `doc show` resolver's miss arms are next opened (any wave touching `store.not-found`'s message or route), **or** a trial worker is observed following that route and reading the committed copy as if it were their staged one — whichever comes first. Its home when it lands is [doc-read-surface.md](../design/doc-read-surface.md) → the `--task` arm.
- *(absorbed into M51 — D2, 2026-09-11: the frame is keyed on the **door**, over the new "doors that commit or move on the user's behalf" registry, whose cause vocabulary the posture family widens N20 into; the trigger fired at the M51 Settle.)* **The milestone boundary's non-hook refusal loses the survivable frame that was built for it — N20, recorded 2026-09-07 (M50 Increment 13 T2), driven at HEAD.** A `squash: true` fan-out whose fast-forward collides with ordinary main-checkout WIP **[Corrected 2026-09-16 (M51 Increment 10, T6):** the scope clause is struck — the defect is **knob-independent**. Re-driven 2026-09-10 on **`1.0.0-rc.14`** (repo HEAD `bd348a83`; the M51 evidence check, recorded into the wave at the 2026-09-11 Settle): the same probe under `jigc config set finalize.fan-out.squash false` produces the **byte-identical** bare `` `git merge --ff-only <sha>` failed: … `` — exit 1, no finding code, no `route:`, no state-truth clause, `error_code: null` — so this is the boundary's fast-forward step on **both** knob settings, and `squash: false` is the setting a Fix round runs under (M50 D11), which is the case this entry's own last sentence gestures at without measuring ([untrialled-surfaces-driven.md](../completions/artifacts/evidence-check-1.0/untrialled-surfaces-driven.md) → F.2). **Deliberately not re-driven at HEAD:** the frame landed at **M51 Increment 2** (`a0eff005`, *a non-hook boundary refusal keeps the frame that was built for it*), so a HEAD re-drive would grade this entry against a binary that already carries its fix — which is why this bracket names the binary and the date it was measured on rather than a verdict.**]** prints `` `git merge --ff-only <sha>` failed: … `` and nothing else: no finding code, no `route:`, no survived-state clause, and `Outcome::failure()` rather than `Outcome::error(frame.code)`, so the door has no identity in the invocation log either. **The frame is not missing — it is discarded.** Both boundary arms reach `crate::task::surface_commit_rejection` with a fully-built `RejectionFrame` (`milestone.rs:3905`'s comment, *"this arm catches EVERY way the boundary refuses … a `git merge --ff-only` refusal over ordinary untracked main-checkout WIP"*, is true of the **match arm**); `task.rs:3534` then renders it only when the error downcasts to `CommitRejected`, and a `git_run` failure is a plain `anyhow`. Confirmed **state-safe** rather than assumed: `HEAD` is unmoved, and moving the WIP aside and re-running lands the boundary at exit 0 — so this is a surface defect, not a recovery one, which is why it is carried rather than escalated. It is also the *normal* state a fan-out fix round runs in (M50 D11), which is what the fan-out cut's declared bound was pointing at. *Trigger:* the first fix round whose boundary is refused by anything other than a hook, **or** the next wave that opens `surface_commit_rejection`'s downcast — at which point the question to settle is whether the frame is keyed on the **door** (always rendered, the cause carried verbatim inside it) rather than on the error's type.
- *(absorbed into M51 — D12, 2026-09-11: the deregistration detect+route arm beside `orphaned_docs`, a `Human` route, and `doc list` printing the row instead of dropping it; **the trigger is recorded as fired** — the committed store-surface enumerator is exactly what M51 opens.)* **Retiring a doctype silently unmanages its committed, tracked corpus — N23, recorded 2026-09-07 (M50 Increment 13 T2), driven at HEAD.** With `arch-doc` and its two workflows removed from a loaded pack over a committed, tracked `docs/architecture/padding-layer.md` (the workflows matter only to isolate the answer: leave them in and `validate` reports a `workflow-refs.schema-ref-resolves` finding about the *definition*, still at exit 0, and still not one word about the orphaned doc): `jigc validate` prints *"no findings — the committed store validates clean"* at **exit 0**, `jigc doc list` drops the row **entirely** (not `unregistered`, not anything), `jigc migrate-corpus` reports `0 blocked`, and `git ls-files` still carries the file. Only `jigc doc show arch-doc:padding-layer` tells the truth — `store.unknown-type`, exit 1, routed at `jigc describe` — and only for a reader who already knows the address the store surface stopped showing. The **staged** arm contemplates exactly this state by name ([doc-read-surface.md](../design/doc-read-surface.md):167, *"a type the resolved cascade no longer defines"*); the committed arm's enumerator simply skips unknown types. Not a data-loss cell — the bytes are tracked and `git checkout`-recoverable — and reachable by an ordinary adopter act now that PB-1 lets a project own its own doctypes. **[Amended 2026-09-16 (M51 Increment 10, T6):** re-driven and **confirmed** — this is **registration loss, not byte loss**, so the classification above stands and it is not a misfiled loss cell. Driven 2026-09-10 on **`1.0.0-rc.14`** (repo HEAD `bd348a83`; the M51 evidence check, recorded into the wave at the 2026-09-11 Settle) from the other side of the same condition — a listed project pack defining no doctypes: `jigc validate` printed *"no findings — the committed store validates clean"* at **exit 0** while `jigc doc list` dropped **3 of 4** managed rows entirely, and `git ls-files` still carried every file with every sha unchanged ([data-loss-hunt.md](../completions/artifacts/evidence-check-1.0/data-loss-hunt.md) → §5, §6 axis 6). **The consequence this entry left unstated, and the reason it outranks its *not a data-loss cell* framing:** the false green lands on the verb [MIGRATING.md](../MIGRATING.md) tells adopters to gate on — *"Ask `jigc validate` whether you need to migrate — it blocks if you do"* — so an adopter's CI passes over a committed corpus jigc no longer claims. **Deliberately not re-driven at HEAD:** the detect+route landed at **M51 Increment 8** (`d947a1ba`, `schema-conformance.orphaned-instance` — blocking, and a `STORE_EXIT_FLIPS` member; `9044d4ec`, `jigc doc list` printing the orphaned row), so a HEAD re-drive would grade this entry against a binary that already carries its fix.**]** Not fixed at Increment 13 (no tier open; the close carries none). *Trigger:* a project pack in real use drops or renames a doctype over a committed corpus, **or** the committed store-surface enumerator is next opened — the `doc list` / `validate` / `migrate-corpus` triple that answers for the committed store, whose disagreement here is the defect.
- *(absorbed into M51 — D10, 2026-09-11: `AMBUSH_CLASS_CODES` becomes **derived**, and this entry's own two questions are answered — the stated-at fence's subject is **packs that ship steps**, and an unstamped managed corpus **gets a store-surface answer**, its exact shape owed at decompose. **The trigger fired verbatim at the M51 Settle.**)* **A manifest-less project pack stamps nothing, so the conformance intrinsic can never fire for its doctypes — and the escape hatch does not open — N26, recorded 2026-09-07 (M50 Increment 13 T2), driven at HEAD as a real listed project pack.** A `packs:` entry shipping `note.yaml` and no `config/schema-manifest.yaml` loads at exit 0, `jigc doc schema note` declares no `schema-version`, and the ingested instance's front-matter is empty — so `schema-conformance.schema-version-current`, whose entire job since M42 is to flip `jigc validate`'s exit code over an unmigrated managed corpus, **cannot fire for that pack's doctypes**. After a shape change: `migrate-corpus` reports `0 blocked` with no route, and `doc show` blocks `store.unparseable` routed at *"fix the committed file"* — hand-repair, not a migration. **The relayed row is corrected twice by driving:** `jigc validate` **does** report the break (`conformance.section-missing`) — what is absent is the exit flip and the migration route, not the report; and the fence half is a **stated** design (`schema-manifest.yaml`'s header: *"an absent manifest = unchecked"*), so the defect is the consequence, not the absence. **The new fact, and the load-bearing one:** adding a correct manifest to that same pack (slug-rule block, right hash) makes **every door** exit 1 at the pack-load *stated-at* fence — *"no step of this pack declares `states-constraints:` for the ambush-class code(s) `finalize.promote-clobber`, `finalize.left-out`, `finalize.nothing-staged`, `finalize.carried-staged`"* — A/B'd in one run against one tree. A schema-only project pack therefore cannot buy the stamp without shipping workflow steps it has no reason to own. Distinct from the un-markered listed-pack entry further down (*which precedence rule the un-markered listed-pack path should obey*): that one is about a manifest-less pack **shadowing a frozen doctype**; this is about a pack's **own** doctypes never being stamped, and it fires with the marker present. *Trigger:* a project pack evolves a doctype it owns over a committed corpus, **or** the stated-at fence's owe-set is next opened — at which point the question to settle is whether the fence's subject is *packs that ship steps* rather than *packs that ship a manifest*, and whether an unstamped managed corpus deserves a store-surface answer of its own.
- **`jigc task diff`'s cold-start form answers *what is here* with almost nothing — N27, recorded 2026-09-07 (M50 Increment 13 T2), driven at HEAD.** Over a task with no code changes yet, `jigc task diff <id>` prints `# staged docs` and the commit doc's empty skeleton: no `# code changes` header, and no task id, workflow, intent, bound read-roles or provenance; `--format json` carries `task` and `base` and none of the rest. This is the second half of fork 2's claim. D3 landed the **orientation** half at Increment 5 (the `ActiveTask` view naming the open task and its intent), and the baseline's claim 4 nominated `task diff` as the verb that already answers *what is here* — driven, it answers that only once there is something to diff. Not fixed at Increment 13; no tier open. *Trigger:* [the duress cell re-measure](#the-trial-that-follows-m50--protocol-inputs) — the next trial's plant-E arm, whose 1/3 reading is what routed F-5 and fork 2 into M50 in the first place. If a worker again goes to the filesystem to read its own abandoned working area, this row is the named candidate and is re-argued against its cost, not re-discovered.
- **Two forward edges may declare the same `inverse:` name at exit 0 — N28, recorded 2026-09-07 (M50 Increment 13 T2), driven at HEAD.** A second `ref` declaring an inverse name another edge already declares (`arch-doc.also-derived-from → prd, inverse: has-specs`, beside `spec.derived-from`) is accepted by pack-load, `jigc describe`, `jigc doc schema prd` and `jigc validate`, all at exit 0. **It is inert today for a reason worth writing down**, which is also why it is carried rather than built: the inverse **name** reaches exactly one surface — the `schema-completeness.inverse-cardinality` advisory's prose phrase *"(its `has-specs` inverse)"* — while that finding's `(code, target)` key is built from the **forward** relation (`index.rs`'s `{target}#{relation}`), so two colliding edges still discriminate and no machine output changes. The razor's **necessity** leg therefore fails at HEAD, and a uniqueness fence over a namespace nothing reads would be a fence with no consequence. *Trigger:* the derived back-edge injection the format's own doc describes is built — [document-type-schema.md](../design/document-type-schema.md):81, *"name of the derived back-edge in the target type's read view"* — since that is the surface on which two edges sharing one name become one ambiguous read. A `to:`-and-`inverse:` uniqueness assert then lands beside the M50 Increment 8 ref-target fence, which is already the pack-load home for questions about a `ref`'s ends.
- **~~Three read/gate doors print an absolute host path in a finding — N31~~ DISCHARGED 2026-09-09 — its trigger fired at the M50 completion audit and finding F3 closed it, over a *larger* class than this entry named: `untrackable_reason`'s shared predicate plus **4** read-path sites rather than 3, with `repo_relative` moved into `crates/engine/src/path.rs` as a pure render-against-a-root function so the engine stays filesystem-free — the seam decision this entry said the close increment held no mandate for, taken by the audit that did. Verified at HEAD: `engine::store.rs:155`, `:288`, `engine::milestone.rs:604`, `:621` all render through it. **Struck 2026-09-09 by the session executing the next trial, which found it still reading as open while briefing §0** — the file's own preamble failure, *it fired once already and nobody noticed*, so the live carried count is **six, not seven**, and `CLAUDE.md`'s *"seven defects … carried"* is corrected in the same motion. The remaining bound is the one F3 declared: **[Corrected 2026-09-20 (M52 Increment 10, T10): **79 producers across 11 files** in `UNSWEPT_PRODUCERS`, each mechanically counted — kept as the measurement F3 made at `b34a8c72` rather than re-pinned to today, on `crates/cli/tests/count_fences.rs`' rule for a historical count. The live figure is stated once, at the axis-6 trigger above, and fenced there.]** *(Original entry text kept below for the reasoning it records; its trigger is spent.)* **Three read/gate doors print an absolute host path in a finding — N31, recorded 2026-09-07 (M50 Increment 13 T2), found at Increment 13 planning and widened by driving.** `jigc doc show vision` over a repo with no committed `VISION.md` prints a `store.not-found` block reading *could not read `vision:vision` at `/private/var/folders/…/repo/VISION.md`*; driven at T2 the class is **three** producers, not one — `store.not-found` (`crates/engine/src/store.rs:151`), `store.unparseable` (`:275`), and `finalize.render-io`'s missing-commit-doc locus at the milestone boundary — each interpolating `path.display()` where law 1 wants a repo-real path. **It is not a regression of M50 Increment 12 / T1**, which declared its domain as *what the destroying and provisioning doors print* and whose standing source fence reads `.display()` over `crates/cli/src/milestone.rs` alone. The repair is a **seam decision, not a relativization**: two of the three producers live in `crates/engine`, which ships filesystem-free and holds no repository root — the same structural reason T1 declared the orientation header's cascade homes absolute — so the choice is *give the engine a root* or *relativize on the CLI's way out*, and neither is a task the close increment carries a mandate for. *Trigger:* the M50 completion audit's triage (the precedent is the `engine::validate::adoption_route` entry above, deferred the same way from an M50 task for the same reason), **or** the first reader observed pasting one of these loci and reaching nothing.

- **No `migrate-planning-record` workflow ships — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 9 T5).** The registration checklist owes one only where foreign instances are plausible in the wild, and the only foreign instances that exist are this repo's nine hand-built gate-records; adopting them is a later act under *we migrate the project to fit the product*. If it lands it is a **workflow addition with no schema event** — no hash moves, no snapshot is owed. *Trigger:* the self-hosting port adopts this repo's hand-built planning gate-records, or a foreign `planning-record`-shaped corpus turns up outside it.
- **An operational-funnel refusal reaches `--format json` as a flattened `{"error": …}` string, not as the structured finding projection — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 11 T1, with T4's `rename` half and Increment 8 T1's `remove-item` sibling).** Driven at HEAD: `jigc rename adr:nope --to other --format json` emits one `error` string with the code, the target and the route fused into prose — no `code`, no `key`, no `findings` array — at exit 1. Those four flatten through the shared `render::finding_error`, which is the shape `config` / `describe` / `milestone`'s three earlier private copies already ship, and the underlying `finding_to_err` reduction reaches further still — it is the posture [command-output-contract.md](../design/command-output-contract.md) already pins — *a finding consumed by `finding_to_err` projects no key at all* — inherited by M49 rather than introduced by it. The `remove-item` cell one level down is the same shape at the **address-resolution** seam and was characterized, not converted, at Increment 8 T1. *Trigger:* a driver needs to key on `(code, target)` at one of those doors — the error-envelope question opening for a reason rather than on principle. A wire change here is contract evolution, so it prices with the `route` tagged-union promotion above, not below it.
- **`locate`'s unset-`$HOME` arm answers with a code and a route that are both wrong — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 11 T2).** Every *directory* fault at that door converged on one text and one route; the one remaining failure mode — an unset `$HOME` — is not on that axis (no directory you can stand in produces it) and keeps its shipped bytes: a `<verb>.repo-root` code and a *run it from inside the repository* route for a fault that is neither. A law-1 wobble left standing on purpose, because repairing it means **minting a code**, which is a contract move that task did not own; named in `locate_finding`'s doc-comment so it is carried rather than buried. *Trigger:* a code mint is being spent at that door anyway, or the state is met in the field.
- **`jigc task validate` reports a milestone sub-task clean while its per-task finalize is refused outright — recorded 2026-08-31 (M49 Increment 12 T1).** Driven at HEAD: `jigc task validate <sub>` answers *no findings — the task validates clean* at exit 0, while `jigc task finalize <sub>` blocks `finalize.milestone-sub-task` at exit 3 — so the surface whose contract is *previews what finalize gates on* previews a boundary this task can never reach, and says nothing about that. It is the stated residue of `MINT_DOORS`' two `Exempt` members, **not** a data-safety hole: no committing door consumes a sub-task's snapshot, and the milestone area's is what the aggregate boundary reads. Widening the preview to the milestone boundary is new behaviour, outside the increment that recorded it. *Trigger:* it fires itself in the author's gate — `crates/cli/tests/mint_doors.rs`'s `assert_sub_task_exemption_holds` drives `finalize.milestone-sub-task` through the binary for every exempt member, so the day the per-task finalize stops refusing a sub-task the exemption reddens and this entry is back on the table.
- **Per-developer `local` layer · team-layer distribution** ([overrides.md](../design/overrides.md)) — when multi-developer / team adoption is real.
- **`import` three-way-merge authoring** ([write-commands.md](../design/write-commands.md)) — detection ships; *authoring* deferred past M5/M6, fires when a real merge-conflict authoring need arises.
- **Reconciliation strictness · concurrent-OOB-during-a-task · external-edit notification** ([reconciliation.md](../design/reconciliation.md)) — post-MVP robustness.
- **Misc hardening opens** — commit-msg hook capture ([finalize.md](../design/finalize.md)) *(the `--dry-run` half dropped from this list 2026-08-09 at M47 Increment 11: it shipped 2026-06-19 as the finalize pre-commit manifest preview and was re-scoped by M47 Increment 4, so it is resolved, not pending)*; command-ref scoping / stdin binding / multi-target ([command-catalog.md](../design/command-catalog.md)); parser span-precision / mentions-in-prose ([parsing.md](parsing.md)); engine→crate splits ([module-layout.md](module-layout.md)); multi-slot sub-label syntax.
- **`route` tagged-union promotion — SPLIT at M43 planning (2026-07-16): the internal slice lands, the wire promotion keeps its trigger.** M43 builds the internal `Route` value (`Mechanical{argv}`/`Human`/`Informational`, constructed at producers, serializing **byte-identical** to today's `Option<String>` — spike-proven, so the pinned `Finding` golden does not move and the deferral's recorded rationale is honored, not overridden). What stays deferred: promoting the **wire** shape to the tagged union (a `command-output-contract.md` versioned extension + adopter-driver migration — after the internal slice it is a serializer flip + contract bump). *Trigger (unchanged in spirit):* a driver/consumer needs to discriminate route kinds structurally from the JSON, or the acknowledged-findings ledger lands and wants typed routes. ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-16 M43 Settle #1; originally re-deferred at M5 + M6, [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md):1742.)
- **`add-item` (+ item-leaf `set-slot`/`set-field`) — built M13 Increment 3** (2026-06-07, [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M13 Increment 3 T1–T3): the `add-item` verb, the item-leaf engine setters (`set_item_slot`/`set_item_field` over the per-item spans `ParsedItem` records), and CLI `#section/item/leaf` addressing all ship, proven end-to-end byte-stable on the shipped `spec` doctype — **retiring the `spec.criteria` authoring deferral** (M3 left criteria un-authorable through the binary; `add-from-spec` only read them). **`remove-item` — built M24** (2026-06-16, [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-16 M24 completion): `jigc doc remove-item <addr>` ships as a general recovery verb over both the top-level `remove_item` and the new nested `remove_nested_item` (the parent-scoped id-chain dual of `add_nested_item`); the top-level `remove_item` was also hardened for the last-block EOF edge (`6fce01e`) it silently mis-handled. **`reorder` stays deferred** (no flow needs it yet).
- **Optional-field / optional-slot capability — built M22 Increment 3** (2026-06-14, [design/changelog.md](../design/changelog.md) → engine work #3): an `optional: bool` flag (serde default false, skip-serialize-on-false, mirroring `singleton`) on **both** `Slot` and `Field`; the requiredness check is skipped when set at **all four** enforcement arms — simple slot (`schema_conformance` gate on `check_slot_present`), simple field + repeatable field (`is_author_required` early return), and the repeatable slot leaf (`check_item_leaves`). An absent optional slot/field finalizes clean; an absent required one still blocks (masking-guard proven both arms); the absent optional field renders with no stray `<!-- fields -->` line (the value-driven writer was already byte-stable, red-proven through `render`). *Consuming target is the changelog release `link` **field*** (inc-5); the inc-3 build proves the capability + both arms on a fixture doctype. *Note:* the earlier `summary`-**slot** wording is superseded by [design/changelog.md](../design/changelog.md) review-finding B1 (a leading bare-prose slot would swallow nested `####` groups — dropped for an optional scalar `link` field); the optional-**slot** arm is still built and proven via a fixture. *(Was: `commit.scope`/`body` author-required though conventional-commits treats them optional; a logged latent defect, [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md):1320.)*
- **Doc-level `set: on-create` (+ doc-level `default:`) materialization — built M22 Increment 4** (2026-06-14, [design/changelog.md](../design/changelog.md) → engine work #4): a CLI-side `on_create_doc_fields(schema)` (`crates/cli/src/doc.rs`) walks the schema's simple-section (header) `fields` at `create`, stamping `type: date, set: on-create` leaves with `today_iso` and any `default:`-carrying field with its literal, seeding the instance before render via the additive engine `on_create` seam (T1) — **the engine stays clock-free**, mirroring the proven item-level deriver. The consuming target is **`adr`**: a freshly-created `adr` now carries `status: proposed` (default) + `date: <today>` (set-on-create) in schema field order, round-trips byte-stable, and is **inert** for a header with neither (e.g. `commit`). The predicted front-matter-test churn (the supersedes/cites-code injection helpers pinning the empty `---\n---\n` fence) was absorbed in this isolated increment. *(Was: surfaced at M16 completion as the inc-4 LOW-2 fix — item-level dating shipped in M16 but doc-level was never materialized; the `adr` date was a hardcoded test fixture.)*
- **Multi-word section-id round-trip defect — built M22 Increment 2** (2026-06-14, [design/changelog.md](../design/changelog.md) → engine work #2): `parse::heading_matches` now re-slugs the rendered heading before comparing (`slugify(text) == section_id`), the true inverse of the title-casing writer — so a hyphenated section id (`unreleased-changes` → heading "Unreleased Changes") round-trips byte-stable instead of aborting the whole-doc parse with `conformance.section-renamed`. No writer change; single-word ids reduce to the prior lowercased compare (a strict superset), and OOB upper-case headings (`## CONTEXT`) stay tolerated. Proven on a multi-word-section doctype (round-trip) with an additive `adr` guard. *(Was: a logged latent defect found at M9 Scope; `prd`/`arch-doc` routed around it with single-word ids.)* This **narrows the deferred `prd` repeatable-requirements blocker** (above) — the multi-word-heading half is no longer owed; the `add-item` half shipped at M13.
- **Parser tolerance for a genuinely-absent optional section (M36-surfaced, 2026-07-03)** — the body-section parser matches **strictly by index** (`parse.rs` `body_sections.enumerate` over `headings.get(i)`) and fires `conformance.section-missing` **unconditionally** for any schema section whose heading is absent — including an `optional: true` slot section. M36 sidesteps this: the canonical writer always renders an optional section's heading, so the frozen-v1 corpus is never non-canonical, and the adr `options` v1→v2 migration *inserts* the heading (`AddedOptionalSection`) rather than teaching the parser to skip it (the migration path keeps a single canonical byte form; parser-tolerance would fork it + touch the byte-stable core — rejected at M36 planning). So the defect is **latent, not exercised** by any v1 flow. *Trigger:* a flow needs a genuinely heading-absent optional section to conform — foreign-doc *adoption* (a migrate source lacking the optional heading), or a future "omit-the-heading-when-empty" canonical-form policy. Then the parser needs a two-pointer optional-tolerant match + a writer that omits the absent heading, re-established byte-stable. ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-03 M36 planning F1; [corpus-migration.md](../design/corpus-migration.md) → `added-optional-section`.)
**Counted at the M48 Settle (2026-08-13), and still deferred.** The pre-1.0.0 trial's **F7** *is* this entry's second trigger arm — *"a future 'omit-the-heading-when-empty' canonical-form policy"* — and the M48 charter drafted F7 without citing this entry, precisely because it is filed under *No firm trigger yet* rather than keyed to a milestone. **The trigger is acknowledged as fired and the answer is still no**, on a cost the charter had not established: the change is **invisible to the freeze** (`adr.yaml` is untouched, so no `schema_hash` moves and no version signal exists), so an rc.11-authored ADR omitting the heading would be a **hard parse failure on rc.10 and every 1.0 predecessor**, reported as a bogus `section-renamed` — and **a version bump cannot repair it**, since `schema_diff` over an unchanged schema is empty and `migrate-corpus` degrades to a pure restamp leaving every hollow heading in place. Normalizing existing corpora would need a **section-removal transform kind that does not exist** ([doctype-authoring.md](doctype-authoring.md) → *build the kind first*). Two byte forms, permanently. M48 fixes the **template** instead: F7 is a law-1 defect in pack prose (the guidance instructs an omission the format does not have), not a format defect. *Trigger:* unchanged — and note that the M36 rationale it rests on was independently re-derived by all four M48 gap-detectors and **still holds**.

- **Required read-role finalize gate** — "a required `reads:` role blocks finalize when unbound"; fires when a workflow ships a required read-role ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md):1259).
- **Empty-slot + field-group item render byte-instability — RESOLVED, built M26 Increment 1** (2026-06-18; found M22, re-keyed M22→M24→M25→M26). `render_item_at` now canonicalizes an empty present-slot (`Some("")`) to the one-blank slotless form, so a minted-but-unfilled slot+field item round-trips `render(parse(x)) == x` (direction B). Full record: [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-18 M26 completion; design home [parsing.md](parsing.md) → Round-trip guarantees → Empty-slot canonical form.
- **Cross-pack `allows-create` (methodology `dev-task` → dev-pack `changelog`)** — the doctype-expansion milestone folds changelog-authoring into the **dev-pack** `single-task` (pack-local, no cross-pack create); folding it into the **methodology** pack's `dev-task` under `[dev ▸ methodology]` composition needs cross-pack `allows-create` resolution, an **unexercised M14 surface**. *Trigger:* a real composed-project flow needs methodology's `dev-task` to author a dev-pack doctype ([design/changelog.md](../design/changelog.md) → driver).
- **Repeatable nesting beyond 2 levels / individually-addressable entries / multi-slot-leaves + nested-repeatable in one block** — the doctype-expansion milestone builds `Leaf::Repeatable` general recursion, capped at 2 nesting levels (the **address hop budget** — this entry previously stated a higher ceiling the write path never accepted, reconciled at M49; [structural-grammar.md](../design/structural-grammar.md) → Repetition), which `changelog` exercises exactly, with entries as **prose** (a single slot per release, so no `####` multi-slot-vs-nested-item contention). *Trigger:* a doctype genuinely needs a 3rd+ nesting level, addressable per-entry records, a multi-slot item that also nests a repeatable, or **a leading bare-prose slot before a nested repeatable** (the depth-aware bare-prose boundary — `changelog` avoids it by carrying only scalar fields, not a leading slot, on a release item, per review finding B1) ([design/changelog.md](../design/changelog.md) → What stays deferred).
- **Ship the `doc-code` probe through `cargo install`** — M10's HIGH-finding fix (`build.rs`, commit `55d96c7`) makes a normal **build tree** place `doc-code` next to `jigc` (the QUICKSTART/local/release path the e2e exercises), but `cargo install jigc` relocates only declared `[[bin]]` targets, so it won't copy the build-script-produced sibling. Closing it cleanly needs a **`jigc setup` embed-and-extract step** (the probe bytes ride in the binary, extracted next to it at setup). *Trigger:* `cargo install` (or any non-build-tree distribution) becomes a supported install channel for jigc. *(M17 planning 2026-06-12: trigger consciously **kept unfired** — the dogfoods install by copying binary + probe sibling together to a PATH dir. The M17 audit also verified the failure shape on an installed binary: a misleading floored-blocking `pack-probe-integrity.probe-failure`, plus the 418MB binary-bloat latent defect — `include_dir!` embeds the probe's ~401MB `target/` dir as dead weight. Both ride this trigger.)* **GRADUATED — trigger fired at M20 (2026-06-14):** M20 Inc 1 makes `cargo install` a supported channel — embed only the ~2MB probe via `include_bytes!`, extract-beside-`jigc` at `jigc setup`, fix the task-scope absence→one-operational-error; the 418MB bloat + probe-failure shape ride the same fix ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M20 planning; design [module-layout.md](module-layout.md) → Probe distribution).
- **Field-granular per-definition-metadata override** — M11 resolves describe's cascade reflection by **whole-file definition shadow** (override a definition's `description:`/`usage:` by restating the whole `workflows/<id>.yaml` / `schemas/<id>.yaml` at the project layer), honoring [overrides.md](../design/overrides.md)'s *no field-level merge across layers* invariant. A latent pressure logged at M11 planning (2026-06-07): if overriding *one* metadata field without restating the file becomes a real, **recurring** need, that is what would legitimately reopen the no-field-level-merge invariant — deliberately *not* broken speculatively (one near-miss is absorbed by whole-file shadow). append-merge of authored prose is explicitly not pursued. *Trigger:* the contortion recurs across a second milestone, with a real authoring need (not aesthetics).
- **`override-default` content-target check** — checks step-*file* presence, not workflow include-list membership; "revisits with a future multi-workflow pack" ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md):1710). **Trigger fired at M14** (dev + methodology *is* a multi-workflow pack-set); **resolved = defer** alongside reconciliation-over-a-composed-base (below) — M14's flow 17 layers no project override over the composed packs, so the M5/M6 probe stays single-pack-keyed for now ([multi-pack.md](../design/multi-pack.md) → Scope and deferrals).
- **Reconciliation over a *composed* base (M14-surfaced, deferred)** — the M5/M6 `override-default` + `jigc upgrade` path re-reads the base pack-direct and keys `base-hash`/`base-version` to a **single** pack; a project delta recorded against a multi-pack base has no pack-id in its recorded basis. *Trigger:* a project records a delta against a multi-pack-composed base, or a real upgrade of a composed pack. Subsumes the content-target check above. *(Engaged 2026-08-31 — **M49 Increment 6 T4, bound 3**: the composite is now three packs deep, `[listed… ▸ dev ▸ methodology]`, while `config fork` still records `base_version` from the highest-precedence pack with no pack-id — the recorded basis is one pack-id short of a set that grew. Trigger unchanged.)*
- **Prior-schema snapshot integrity + `jigc upgrade` relationship (M34 Inc-4-deferred, 2026-06-25 → trigger fired + re-argued at M36 planning 2026-07-03; stays DEFERRED on a new basis).** M34 stores prior schema shapes as `schema-snapshots/<type>.v<N>.yaml` (the verb sources `from` keyed on a committed doc's stamp). **M36 Inc 3 fires the trigger** — the adr `options` v1→v2 bump is the first real v1→v2 shape migration, shipping `schema-snapshots/adr.v1.yaml` as the embedded pack's first real snapshot. Re-evaluated (the original defer rested on "the snapshot set is empty" — now stale): **both pieces stay deferred on the line-103 basis instead.** **(a) snapshot hashing** — an **embedded** pack can't drift in a normal install (the snapshot travels in the binary, hash-identical), and a corrupt snapshot still fails the migration transform/round-trip loudly; the **embedded-snapshot migrate acceptance** M36 ships (`load_prior_schema` over the `EmbeddedPack`, exercised for the first time) is the standing backstop, so a second pack-load hash gate over snapshots is premature. **(b) `jigc upgrade` ↔ the snapshot store** — how a pack *upgrade* publishes a new current schema + matching prior snapshot stays unspecified; jigc is the sole pack author, no external upgrade path exists. *Re-trigger:* the snapshot set grows **beyond one** (a second frozen doctype advances past v1, or adr reaches v3), **or** filesystem packs ship in production (external pack distribution — pairs with line 103). ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-03 M36 planning + 2026-06-25 M34 audit Findings 1+2; [corpus-migration.md](../design/corpus-migration.md) → Snapshot hashing + `jigc upgrade`.) *(The sibling deriver footgun — the on-create stamp ignoring per-doctype manifest version — was **fixed** at M34 audit, `f71a255`, not deferred.)* **M41 Fork 4 fires the re-trigger** (deferral-ledger advances to v2 — a frozen doctype past v1 in a *second* pack, and the **first methodology** snapshot `packs/methodology/schema-snapshots/deferral-ledger.v1.yaml` is populated). **Re-affirmed DEFERRED (2026-07-11):** the line-103 basis still holds — the methodology snapshot is embedded too (can't drift in a normal install), and Fork 4's value-remap migration exercises `load_prior_schema` over the **methodology-origin** pack for the first time (a standing backstop, per M36's embedded-snapshot precedent, now for a second origin). A second pack-load hash gate over snapshots stays premature; jigc is still the sole pack author. *(Re-trigger narrows: filesystem packs ship in production — external distribution — pairs with line 103.)* **M49 Increment 9 produces the configuration the narrowing never contemplated — the store's first TWO-snapshot doctype — so the entry is re-argued rather than skipped on a technicality.** `milestone-record` reaches schema-version 3, so `packs/methodology/schema-snapshots/milestone-record.v1.yaml` now sits **beside** `.v2.yaml`: for the first time a committed doc's stamp `k` has more than one legal value, an **intermediate** version exists that a future bump could forget, and picking the *wrong* snapshot is possible at all. (The M41 narrowing had left only the filesystem-pack arm, still unfired; this is new regardless.) **Re-affirmed DEFERRED (2026-08-30), on a basis that is now DRIVEN rather than reasoned** — `crates/cli/tests/snapshot_store_two_snapshots.rs`, four arms through the real embedded packs and the shipped binary: **(i)** a v1-stamped and a v2-stamped record, same body, in **one** corpus migrate in **one** `jigc migrate-corpus` pass, each byte-identical but for its own stamp — neither blocked, neither already-current, the added `.v2.yaml` neither intercepting the v1 record nor stranding it; **(ii)** the two snapshots are **distinct** and each carries the shape its own version declared (v1 pre-`discarded` in both of the homes that schema declares it, v2 pre-`workflow`), so the store is keyed on `k` and not on being a singleton; **(iii)** the store is **complete and surplus-free** across BOTH shipped manifests — exactly `<ty>.v1 .. v(current-1)`, every one resolvable out of the embedded binary, iterated *from the manifests* so the next doctype to bump is covered by construction; **(iv)** an unstamped v0-era record still folds against the **earliest** snapshot in one pass. **What that buys and what it does not.** The failure the set-growth actually opened — a *missing intermediate* snapshot silently stranding every corpus at that stamp — is now a **build-time redness** (iii), which is cheaper than a hash gate and fires on the pack author rather than the adopter; hashing therefore stays premature while packs are embedded and cannot drift. **And the recorded bound is sharpened from hypothesis to demonstration:** (i) also shows the two records migrating to **byte-identical** results, because every link of this chain is a byte no-op — so a *plausible-but-wrong* snapshot is **provably invisible** to the migration's outcome, not merely suspected to be. That is the argument to re-open with when filesystem packs ship; it is no longer speculative. **(b) `jigc upgrade` ↔ the snapshot store** is unchanged — jigc is still the sole pack author, no external upgrade path exists. *Re-trigger:* filesystem packs ship in production (unchanged), **or** arm (i)'s byte-equality assertion reddens — i.e. a link on a two-snapshot chain stops being byte-neutral and the fold's output starts discriminating its source, at which point the outcome itself becomes the drift detector this entry has been deferring.
- **Which precedence rule the *un-markered* listed-pack path should obey (M49-surfaced, deferred).** The M49 freeze demotion — *a project pack may not shadow a doctype the freeze governs* — lives in the **marker** composition only. On the plain M14 path (a `packs:` list with no `compose-embedded-methodology` marker, or `JIGC_PACK_DIR` selecting an explicit base) the set stays listed-highest with no demotion, so a **manifest-less** listed pack still shadows a frozen doctype with nothing checking it — driven live at M49 Increment 6 (a listed pack shipping an `adr.yaml` with the frozen `options` section deleted and no `config/schema-manifest.yaml` resolves as the composed `adr` at **exit 0**; adding the marker restores the frozen shape). Deliberately **not** closed there: extending the demotion would invert [multi-pack.md](../design/multi-pack.md)'s own base-lowest statement *and* break the flow-17 acceptance, whose discriminating case is methodology's listed `commit` legitimately winning over dev's — a **governed** doctype. So the two paths want opposite rules and choosing between them is a design question, not a repair. Bounding fact: `jigc setup` writes the marker into every project, so the shipped topology is the protected one and the residue is reachable only by hand-authoring a marker-less `packs.yaml` or setting `JIGC_PACK_DIR`. *Trigger:* a real project composes a listed pack **without** the marker (a distribution channel that does not run `jigc setup`, or an operator who removes it), **or** the flow-17 methodology-primary ordering stops being an acceptance requirement — either one makes the two rules choosable. Recorded at [multi-pack.md](../design/multi-pack.md) → Declared bounds of a project pack, bound 2.
- **Cross-pack probe / field-type collision (M14-surfaced, deferred)** — the `doc-code` probe-program resolves single-sourced (`invoke.rs`) and `pack_field_types` reads one pack's `field-types.yaml`; in M14's slice methodology ships neither, so no collision arises (a dev-only doctype's `code-anchor` must still resolve from dev's pack — in M14 scope). *Trigger:* a second pack ships its own `field-types.yaml` or probe.
- **Closed-enum union + ~~finer pack-precedence~~ + ~~the N>2 collision-winner~~ (M14-surfaced) — TWO OF THREE DISCHARGED, ✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-30 (M49 Increment 6).** The trigger — *a composition of 3+ packs* — fired the moment PB-1 composed a listed project pack with the embedded `[dev ▸ methodology]` pair. **(a) Finer pack-precedence: shipped as per-id-space precedence.** The base was fixed *lowest* for every id-space; the freeze needs the opposite for one id-space and only one, so a `Schemas` id declared in either embedded manifest's `doctypes:` list demotes the listed packs below the pair — *a project pack may not shadow a doctype the freeze governs* — while workflows, steps, catalogs and `knobs.yaml` keep the listed-highest convention. What stays unbuilt is a *general operator-authored* precedence surface, which nothing has asked for and which leans on the excluded public pack-authoring API. **(b) The `--explain` collision-winner: corrected.** The winner is read from the id's **origin pack** (never `provenance_segments()[0]`) and the adjudicated set is derived from the composed pack-set, so a freeze-demoted doctype is named as the loss it is instead of being absent. **(c) Union of colliding closed enums stays DEFERRED** — M14 resolves a `default-workflow`-style enum collision by **precedence-override** (winner's whole enum+default win), accepting that the loser's values can't be *defaulted*; **union** of colliding `of:` sets would be the first field-level merge-across-packs primitive, and PB-1 did not need it (the demotion is whole-definition, no merge). *Trigger, unchanged for (c):* a real, **recurring** need for a composed project to default to either pack's workflow ([multi-pack.md](../design/multi-pack.md) → Scope and deferrals; the three declared bounds of a project pack are recorded there, not here).
- **Store/milestone-scope inverse-/min-cardinality enforcement** — designed as "hard-enforced at store/milestone scope," never built (task scope is advisory-only); needs store-scope completeness validation (post-roadmap).
- **Minor / perf** — the unbound-spec-role "did you mean single-task?" advisory ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md):1262); findings recomputed-vs-cached ([validation.md](../design/validation.md) opens).
- **M17-audit-surfaced frictions, deliberately let-surface (2026-06-12)** — verified by real exercise at M17 planning but **not** pre-fixed (the need-driven policy: they are the dogfood's data, fixed when a run surfaces them as actually blocking): the Rust-hardcoded gate in `packs/methodology/steps/re-verify.yaml` (composed `fmt · clippy · test · build` verbatim onto a non-Rust project — the #3 portable-gate-knob trigger's sharpest exemplar) — *RESOLVED 2026-06-19: trigger fired (a PHP + TS + Vite project will compose the methodology pack); de-Rusted by **prose alignment** with the pack's already-portable `gate.yaml` ("the project's own gate, whatever toolchain"), **not** a knob — the gate is agent-run, never CLI-owned, so a cascade knob would be premature generality ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-19 re-verify de-Rust)*; the **finalize dirty-tree sweep** (unrelated untracked/WIP files are committed into the task's commit) — *B1 SHIPPED 2026-06-19: a landed finalize now surfaces a pre-commit manifest (untracked sweeps flagged) and `--dry-run` prints it without committing, so the set is visible; **Option A** — *GRADUATED → split into M30 + M31 at M30 planning (2026-06-20). The naive "3-task cut, both per-task + squash callers" was a **data-loss regression in disguise** — the milestone squash path's sub-agent code is unstaged-by-design and reaches the commit only via `git add --all`, so narrowing it drops sub-agent code. **Split (human):** **M30 = the per-task narrowing only** (the production blocker — index-as-change-manifest, `StagePolicy` 3-way with squash explicitly `Sweep`, block-on-empty, code-anchor tracked-status gate, agent `git add` + adapter allowlist, the left-out manifest surface; see roadmap M30 + [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-20 M30 planning); **M31 = the worktree fan-out redesign** (the correct fix for the squash/fan-out path — see the new M31 entry below). The 6 M30 forks + the WF1–WF4 M31 forks are settled in DECISIONS.md → 2026-06-20 M30 planning.* the **owner-artifact raw-`git add`** friction (the one structural write the composed prose routes around the CLI) — *RESOLVED by M30's G5: the agent now stages all its edits with plain `git add` (adapter-allowlisted), so the owner-artifact's raw `git add` is ordinary staging, no longer an anomaly routed around the CLI*; **ingest scan breadth** — *RESOLVED (stale premise): `engine::ingest::discover_candidates` already walks the **whole repo recursively**, pruning only `.jigc/`+`.git/` (commit `929b8e7`), so a docs-elsewhere corpus (`rfcs/`, `wiki/sub/`, deep dirs) is reachable — verified empirically 2026-06-19. No scan-root knob needed. **Superseded at M40:** the recorded resolution's whole-repo walk is replaced as the production candidate set by `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard -- '*.md'` — the walk listed gitignored files (`node_modules/**/*.md`) as candidates, the F8 funnel-poisoning finding; internals prune kept, no no-git fallback, the engine walk stays as engine-test substrate ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-10 M40 Settle)*; the **doc-code non-Rust silent symbol pass** (`#symbol` on a non-`.rs` file returns no finding — citations look validated and aren't; an "unverifiable" advisory is the likely shape) — *GRADUATED 2026-06-19: trigger fired (a real PHP + TS project composes the dev pack). M27 generalizes the probe to 6 languages (Rust + TS/JS/Python/PHP/bash) via a per-language node-kind allowlist; a `#symbol` on an un-grammared file now emits a non-blocking `doc-code.unsupported-language` advisory, never a silent pass ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-19 M27 planning; design [validation.md](../design/validation.md) → Multi-language resolution)*; **relative `packs.yaml` paths resolve against process CWD** (silent pack drop from a nested dir); `describe` under multi-pack lists only the winning pack's `commands.yaml` (the menu under-reports) — *both **NOT reproduced** in the 2026-06-20 project-alpha-2.0 dogfood: jigc walks up to the project root and resolves config to an absolute path (byte-identical root-vs-nested for `start`/`ingest`/`describe`), and `describe` lists the **union** of both composed packs' surfaces; the relative-path footgun has no surface on the embedded-pack install. Re-open only if an external relative-path pack is configured.* *Trigger, each:* a dogfood run surfaces it as blocking or as a measured-run contaminant.
- **~~M17-dogfood-surfaced: no sub-milestone decision-recording / managed-doc workflow (2026-06-13).~~ RESOLVED 2026-06-13 (built) — the `decided-task` workflow.** The methodology pack recorded a decision only at the **milestone grain** — `author-decisions` (the managed, validated `decisions-log` singleton) lived solely in the heavy `planning` and `completion` loops; `dev-task` and `increment` carried no `allows-create`, so they produced only a transient `commit`. A **smaller task that warranted recording a decision** therefore had *no lightweight path* — it had to invoke milestone-grained planning/completion (a real grain mismatch, surfaced when the M17 galey increment comparison needed one violation-policy decision recorded to exercise jigc's differentiators, and found the only route was the full milestone-planning ceremony). This is also *why* a lone `dev-task` ties a static CLAUDE.md in the M17 single-task comparison — it touches no managed doc by construction. **Built as the M17 self-hosting dogfood target** ([completions/artifacts/M17/self-hosting/](../completions/artifacts/M17/self-hosting/)): `decided-task` — a `creates-task:true` dev-task variant (scope → implement → gate → **author-decision** → finalize) with `allows-create:[{decisions-log}]`, `selectable:false` (off-router until the router-flip), reusing the idempotent create-log + an `author-decision` step. The lightweight sub-milestone decision-recording path now exists; jigc dogfooded its methodology *by building the workflow that would have helped*.
- **NGT-surfaced: no verb to update a field on a committed doc; `set-field` on a `0..*` ref is silently last-write-wins (2026-06-13).** The native-grain thesis test ([completions/artifacts/M17/native-grain-test/](../completions/artifacts/M17/native-grain-test/)) surfaced two real gaps, each a measured cost: **(a)** completing a supersession requires flipping the superseded ADR's `status → superseded`, but the MVP exposes no verb to set a field on an *already-committed* doc, and the composed `superseded-context` flow neither does it nor guides the agent to the idempotent-create + re-promote workaround — so jigc left the supersession structurally incomplete (the test's *only* committed defect, and it was jigc's). **(b)** `jigc doc set-field` on a list-cardinality (`0..*`) ref splices a single value span — repeated calls are last-write-wins, silently dropping earlier entries; the agent set `cites` "one per ADR" per the guidance and kept only the last, recovering only via the inline-list `--value "[a, b, c]"` form. *Sub-part (a) GRADUATED — closed (confirmed by exercise at M25 planning, 2026-06-17):* `set-field`/`set-slot` on an **already-committed** doc copies-on-first-touch (provenance `edited-from-base`) and **re-promotes** through finalize — a full supersession (B supersedes A + A's `status` flipped to `superseded`) completes with **no workaround**. *Sub-part (b) GRADUATED — closed (2026-06-20, dogfood trigger fired on a real repo):* a single-value `set-field` onto a populated `0..*` ref no longer silently last-write-wins — it is **blocked** (`write.list-overwrite`) naming the field + showing the inline-list `--value "[a, b]"` form (the only multi-value path, now also surfaced in `set-field --help`). The first set on an empty `0..*` ref, a scalar/`0..1` re-set, and the explicit bracket-list whole-list replace (the blessed re-point idiom) are unchanged. *(graduated — trigger fired)*
- **NGT-scoping-surfaced: doc↔code integrity is enforced at AUTHORING time, not maintained OVER TIME — no store-wide re-validation (2026-06-13, → M18).** Empirically confirmed by three probes on a jigc twin (a committed `arch-doc`'s `implemented-by` anchor, whose symbol was renamed in the code): the stale anchor is **not** caught at an unrelated task's finalize, **not** caught when the doc drifts and is absorbed, and caught **only** when the doc is re-authored through its own workflow — and **no store-wide `jigc validate`** exists to sweep all committed docs' anchors against current code. So a committed doc **silently rots** under moving code until someone deliberately re-authors it, and nothing prompts them to. This is the core of VISION's strongest claim ("living documentation that stays honest against the code") and it is **not built as a passive/over-time guarantee** — only an authoring-time check. *Owed (M18):* a store-wide doc↔code re-validation surface (an on-demand `jigc validate`/`check` that re-checks every committed doc's code-anchors against current code, reporting stale anchors), **then** the maintenance/drift-over-time thesis comparison (jigc-with-store-validate vs a static doc set over a code-evolution sequence). Honest bound to carry into that test: `symbol-exists` catches **structural** anchor drift (renamed/deleted symbols), never **behavioral** drift (the prose lies but the symbol still exists). **SETTLED at M18 planning (2026-06-13)** → built as the top-level read-only **`jigc validate`**, report-only, scoped to doc↔code over the committed store ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → M18 planning; design [validation.md](../design/validation.md) → Store-scope re-validation). Four forks resolved (command shape / severity / multi-valued-anchor guard / probe distribution); the test half is pre-registered post-build. *(graduated — trigger M18 fired)*
- **~~CSS + docker-compose code-anchor resolution (M27-parked, 2026-06-19)~~ RESOLVED (CSS M28 + YAML/docker-compose M29 — both halves shipped).** M27 generalizes the `doc-code` probe to six languages whose addressable symbols are AST *named items* (Rust/TS/JS/Python/PHP/bash). CSS (addressable units = *selectors*, custom properties, `@keyframes` names) and docker-compose (YAML; units = *service keys*) do **not** fit the `path#symbol` named-item model — they need a distinct resolution semantics (a selector / YAML-path query), not the node-kind allowlist. Out of M27 scope by decision; until built they take the `doc-code.unsupported-language` advisory. **Now shaped (2026-06-19):** subsumed by the multi-language north-star — CSS/YAML fit via the **HD1 "addressable unit"** generalization (selectors / service-keys as addressable units, `path#name`, zero schema change), designed in [ideas/multi-language-doc-code.md](multi-language-doc-code.md) → HD1 (CSS is tier-1, the first breadth target). **CSS half SHIPPED at M28** (2026-06-20): the live PHP/TS/Vite repo fired the trigger; M28 shipped CSS `symbol-exists` via the HD1 extractor generalization (class/id/`@keyframes`/custom-property addressable units, `--` kept, exact→blocking) — the addressable-unit keystone, proven e2e on the installed binary ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-20 M28 completion; design [validation.md](../design/validation.md) → CSS addressable units). **docker-compose/YAML half SHIPPED at M29** (2026-06-20): the trigger fired (a real arch-doc cites a compose service as a `path#name` anchor and the uncheckable-advisory was no longer good enough); M29 shipped YAML `symbol-exists` riding the HD1 seam CSS forced — a mapping-key extractor (block + flow pairs, surrounding quotes stripped, `.yaml`/`.yml` extension-keyed, compose-schema-blind), so `compose.yaml#web` blocks on a vanished service key, passes when present, with per-item disambiguation, proven e2e on the installed binary ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-06-20 M29 planning; design [validation.md](../design/validation.md) → YAML addressable units). **No deferred remainder** — both halves of this entry are shipped. *(graduated — trigger compose/YAML fired)*
- **Non-Rust is-a-test predicate (`criterion-maps-to-test`) port (M27-deferred, 2026-06-19)** — M27 ships `symbol-exists` for six languages but keeps the **is-a-test** half Rust-only (`#[test]` attribute); a non-Rust `maps-to-test` anchor blocks on a *symbol that resolves to no declared unit* (the `symbol-exists` floor) but only *advises* that the is-a-test assertion is unverified. Porting the predicate means a per-language is-a-test detector (pytest `test_*` naming, Jest/Vitest `test()`/`it()` calls, PHPUnit `#[Test]`/`test*`) — convention-dependent, no portable AST signature. **Now shaped (2026-06-19):** subsumed by the multi-language north-star — the is-a-test **confidence tiers (HD3)** + JS/TS **label-addressing (HD2)** in [ideas/multi-language-doc-code.md](multi-language-doc-code.md): attribute-based markers block, name-convention tests advise (symbol-exists stays the floor), JS/TS `path#test:"label"` is an exact-match block. *Trigger (**corrected 2026-07-23** — as written it was **unfireable**: `maps-to-test` is declared exactly once, in the **dev pack** (`crates/cli/pack/schemas/spec.yaml:38`), and both shipped packs are Rust, so no *pack* can ever ship the field into a non-Rust corpus; what fires is the **project** side):* a project authors `maps-to-test` against a corpus whose test framework the is-a-test predicate cannot verify, and the symbol-exists floor + advisory is no longer enough → schedule the is-a-test tier. (Distinct from the A==A-oracle lint above, which still has no target.) *(Trigger **FIRED** 2026-07-22: project-alpha-3.0 — the first real `maps-to-test` authoring campaign, on the Pest corpus (the corpus was always Pest; no prior trial wrote the field); per-criterion anchoring to `it(...)` closures impossible, and the M42-added `<path>#<test-fn>` hint is itself the misleader on closure frameworks — [RC-alpha3/findings-verification.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-alpha3/findings-verification.md) → §3-C; **re-Settled 2026-07-23**: the cheap rider — reword the hint · the file-only fallback · split the lying `unsupported-language` message — lands **in M45**, and the tier itself defers to **M46** as new capability; the capability wave above, entry 5.)* *(**M46 disposition, 2026-08-18: SPLIT** — the message half's remainder shipped at M46 Increment 7 (the M45 rider reached one of the two soliciting steps and neither of the findings it touched was the blocking one), and the **tier came back here** rather than to any Settle. The trigger above is the live key; entry 5 records the split and does not restate it.)*
- **Never-started fan-out backstop** — the runtime guard that surfaces a sub-task with no recorded CLI activity as a distinct join outcome (catching a launch template that *passes* install-time schema validation but still misfires at runtime). **Deferred at M8 Settle** (2026-06-04): M8 builds install-time template validation only; the runtime misfire of a schema-valid template is an accepted unsurfaced gap ([workflow-dialect.md](../design/workflow-dialect.md) → `fan-out`/`join`; [assistant-adapter.md](../design/assistant-adapter.md) → Bind the spawn mechanism). *Condition:* fires when a real fan-out launch-misfire is observed, or a workflow earns mid-fan-out resumption (pairs with the restart-from-scratch resumption policy). Likewise the **Resume / post-compaction hook** — capability-gated on the assistant exposing a resume event, not M8 ([assistant-adapter.md](../design/assistant-adapter.md) opens). *(M15 planning 2026-06-09: confirmed **not triggered** — M15's checkpoint/fix-loop rides the existing **stateless restart-from-scratch** resumption with no new state, and M15 encodes **no fan-out** methodology workflow, so neither condition has arrived.)*

- **Compose-time `checkpoint`-in-fan-out-target guard (M36-verified safe, guard deferred 2026-07-03).** Verified: a `checkpoint` step cannot reach a `fan-out` target in either shipped pack — the sole fan-out target (`sub-task`) + its include-closure are checkpoint-free, and all five methodology checkpoints live in never-fanned top-level workflows ([workflow-dialect.md](../design/workflow-dialect.md) → a fan-out target must be checkpoint-free). But this is **authoring discipline, not engine-enforced**: `compose.rs` treats a checkpoint as valid anywhere and `emit_fan_out_spawns` never inspects the target body, so a mis-authored pack would silently compose a `Checkpoint:` into a sub-agent (which has no human to resume it → deadlock). A compose-time guard (checkpoint unreachable from any `fan-out` `run:` target's include-closure) is the machine-enforced form. *Trigger:* a third-party pack-authoring surface ships (the non-goal's own release condition), or a pack is authored that could place a checkpoint in a fan-out target — until then the by-construction safety + the recorded authoring rule suffice.

- **~~Multi-process concurrency — no store locking (v1-audit-recorded, 2026-07-02; rationale corrected + the corruption subset closed 2026-07-23).~~ GRADUATED — trigger fired at the M48 Settle's exercised baseline, discharged at M46 Increment 1 (2026-08-18).** The lost-update half is built; the design of record is [storage.md](../design/storage.md) → Concurrent writers (the per-file rules) and the capability-wave entry 9 above (what shipped, and the residue that did not). The entry is kept in full below because the rationale it *corrects* is the load-bearing part. Two concurrent `jigc` processes on one repo have no locking primitive anywhere in `crates/`; engine state writes assume a single writer. **The recorded acceptance — *"fan-out sub-agents write isolated areas by design"* — is false and is corrected here:** a provisioned worktree binds to the **main checkout's `.jigc`** (`start.rs:143-146`), so what is isolated is **task doc areas and worktree code**, while N sub-agents **share one `.jigc/state/*` (incl. `file-state.json`), one `.jigc/index/*`, and one `tasks.json`** — and the project-alpha-3.0 trial ran 5 parallel worktrees against exactly that. **M45 lands the corruption-removing subset** ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-23, Decision 9): `state::write_atomic`'s fixed `.tmp` sibling becomes process-unique and `FileStateRecord::save` (the one state writer bypassing `state::persist`) routes through it — closing the shared-temp race that produced 11/44 and 16/42 unparseable reads under 5 writers. ~~**What stays deferred is the semantics, not the corruption:** lost updates (two writers' state edits interleave, one silently wins) are contention, and a locking primitive is a real design — the capability wave (M46) above, entry 9.~~ **The trigger fired at the M48 Settle's exercised baseline and the semantics shipped at M46 Increment 1:** the *"contention, not corruption"* premise was falsified — a lost `file-state` key silently disables `reconciliation.conflict-block`, so an out-of-band human edit and a task's staged writes land merged in one commit at exit 0, which `CLAUDE.md`'s *"never silently merged"* forbids. What remains is not a deferral but a **stated residue**: the save-scoped lock is best-effort by design (it degrades to unlocked rather than blocking), and a baseline lost by any non-concurrent route — a fresh clone, `jigc unmanage`, a deleted cache — still switches the guarantee off. Both are stated at [storage.md](../design/storage.md) → Concurrent writers and pinned by `crates/cli/tests/reconciliation_baseline_contrast.rs`.
- **~~Freeze hash assertion fires only at the compose front door (v1-audit-recorded, 2026-07-02).~~ RESOLVED — trigger fired, discharged at M42 Increment 6** (2026-07-13). The recorded acceptance ("the embedded pack can't drift in a normal install") was already false when it was written: the M14 multi-pack surface reads pack **directories** from `.jigc/config/packs.yaml`, so a production project *does* load filesystem packs. Against a listed pack whose frozen `adr` schema had drifted with no manifest bump, `validate` / `describe` / `doc schema` (which cheerfully projected the phantom shape) / `migrate-corpus` all ran clean at exit 0 — and `jigc milestone create` **wrote and committed a new milestone record**. Fixed by moving `assert_schema_freeze` out of its single compose call site (`start::compose_drained`) into the pack-source factory (`pack::make_pack`, now `-> anyhow::Result<Box<dyn PackSource>>`), where every door inherits it; the **one** deliberately non-blocking consumer is `invocation_log::enabled_logs_dir` (instrumentation is opt-in and best-effort — a drifted pack silently disables the log while the verb blocks loudly a moment later). Acceptance: `crates/cli/tests/freeze_enforcement.rs` — six doors through the real binary. Design home unchanged: [corpus-migration.md](../design/corpus-migration.md) → The enforcement gate fires at pack-load.
- **A mis-keyed leaf inside a `repeatable:` block silently erases the whole section (found while probing at M45 planning, 2026-07-23).** An unknown key on a repeatable's leaf makes the `repeatable:` body fail to deserialize, and because `Section` cannot carry `deny_unknown_fields` alongside the flattened `SectionBody` ([schema.rs](../crates/engine/src/schema.rs) — the note at the enum), it falls through to `SectionBody::Simple { slot: None, fields: [] }` — **an empty section, no error**. Verified: a `pattern:` typo on `commit.trailers.key` made `jigc doc schema commit` report `summary`, `body`, and **no `trailers` at all**; the same typo on a *simple*-section field errors loudly. It was caught only because the schema-hash moved — **a freeze-exempt or manifest-less pack would ship it silently**, which is the real exposure (a pack author's typo deletes a section from every surface). Recorded, not scoped: M45 is an integrity wave over the *write* path and this is a *schema-load* hole. *Trigger:* a third-party or project-layer pack is authored (the freeze no longer backstops the typo), or a doctype reshape lands a leaf-key change. ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-23, the two latent defects.) — *✅ SHIPPED 2026-08-29 (M49 Increment 3, T3): refused at load with a located message, over the whole axis of mappings the model cannot deny — including a sibling this entry does not name (a stray key beside a **slot** leaf's `slot:`, absorbed silently at exit 0). [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-08-29 T3.*
- **The `transform.rs` id-from siblings — a migration that silently skips items and reports success (found while probing at M45 planning, 2026-07-23).** Two holes on the *only* post-1.0 route to change a frozen schema. **(a)** `apply_value_remapped`'s repeatable arm looks the field up in `item.fields`, but the parser excludes the `id-from` leaf from item fields by construction — so a remap targeting an id-from field finds **nothing, edits nothing, and returns `Ok`**. Its in-code justification (*"the enum field is never the id-from — an enum id-from item is reslug-refused"*) is a claim about the **write** path (M40's `retitle-item` refusal), not about the migration transform, and it is **false against the shipped schemas**: `changelog.unreleased-changes` and `changelog.releases/changes` are both `id-from: category` with `category` an enum — the exact analogue of M41's shipped `deferral-ledger` remap. **(b)** `apply_added_item_field` has no id-from guard and would splice a bullet the parser then rejects. **Neither transform has a nested arm at all**, so `changelog.releases/changes` is never visited either way. **Why this is one-way-door-adjacent:** M42's empty-diff backstop guards *"no transform kind was emitted"*, not *"the transform kind was applied to nothing"* — so a no-op migration lands bytes, advances the stamp, and reports success, unrecoverably. No shipped migration targets an id-from field today, which is why it is deferrable — but the freeze makes `migrate-corpus` the only legal route, so the guard is owed before one does. *Trigger:* any authored migration whose transform targets an `id-from` field or a nested repeatable — i.e. the first `changelog` reshape. Design home when it lands: [corpus-migration.md](../design/corpus-migration.md). ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-23, the two latent defects.)
- **`engine::write::set_slot` stays `pub` and ungated — a latent bypass of the ceiling seam (M45-audit-recorded non-defect, trigger minted at the 2026-07-24 validation).** M45 unified every slot write behind `set_slot_validated` (the ceiling + validate-after door) and demoted the raw item splices to private — but the raw section-slot `set_slot` remains `pub` with no gate. Verified at completion: **no shipped caller bypasses the door** (the one raw caller outside `write.rs`'s own tests sits in `transform.rs`'s `#[cfg(test)]`), so it is a latent footgun, not a live hole — but it is exactly the shape the wave's own set-fence rule warns about: an assumption ("every caller goes through the door") that a later, locally-correct commit can falsify without touching the door. *Trigger:* any new non-test caller of `engine::write::set_slot` outside `set_slot_validated`, or the next engine-visibility pass — then either demote it (`pub(crate)`/private, the tier-1 fence) or gate it; the cheap check at pickup is one grep, the honest fix is the visibility change.
- **Sparse-checkout absence classifies as a blocking weak-deletion — a declared false-deletion conservative bound (confidence-audit, 2026-07-24).** A path excluded by sparse-checkout is absent from the worktree while tracked in HEAD, so the history oracle (`git log HEAD -1 -- <path>` non-empty) reads it as a deletion and **blocks** — over-blocking, never destroying (the safe direction). Pinned by its op-row test (`file_state_history_gate.rs`), declared at the oracle doc-comment and Decision 7's entry; no in-oracle detection built. *Trigger:* a sparse-checkout user exists (a jigc-managed repo is worked under sparse-checkout and the false block costs someone real time) — then weigh cheap in-oracle detection (`core.sparseCheckout` + the sparse patterns) against the conservative block. ([completions/artifacts/confidence-audit/VERDICT.md](../completions/artifacts/confidence-audit/VERDICT.md) → §5.)
- **The retitle id-from guard is first-field order-dependent — safe for every shipped schema, unfenced for third-party packs (confidence-audit mutation adjudication, 2026-07-24).** `retitle_id_from_refusal` locates the id-from leaf by first-match over the block's fields; verified true of all 15 shipped schemas that the id-from field is first, so the guard cannot misfire today — but a third-party pack whose id-from field is not the block's first field would have the guard adjudicate the wrong leaf. Same trigger family as the checkpoint-in-fan-out guard above. *Trigger:* a third-party pack-authoring surface ships — then either derive the lookup from the schema's declared `id-from:` (the tier-1 fix) or fence the first-field assumption at pack load. ([completions/artifacts/confidence-audit/mutation-results.md](../completions/artifacts/confidence-audit/mutation-results.md) → the (b) adjudications.)
- **Corrupted task-state (`provenance.json`) silently tolerated (v1-audit-recorded, 2026-07-02).** A garbage provenance file rides through the task lifecycle without a finding. *Trigger:* an RC-trial/dogfood diagnosis is hampered by it, or task-state gains more consumers.
- **Project-workflow-shadow compose/gate divergence (found at the round-2 surface review, 2026-07-17).** A project-layer `workflows/<id>.yaml` shadow changes the *compose's* `create-gates:` line while `doc create`'s enforcement still reads the pack's `allows-create:` list — the composed surface can advertise a gate the write path refuses (verified live at batch D6-f; the gate-blocked route was made honest about the pack-authoring reality in the same batch). One of the two sides must move: the enforcement reads the resolved (shadow-aware) workflow, or the compose line renders from the enforced list. *Trigger:* a project actually shadows a workflow's `allows-create:` (no shipped or trial project does today), or the rc.7 trial hits the divergence. ([completions/artifacts/M43/surface-comprehension-review.md](../completions/artifacts/M43/surface-comprehension-review.md) → Round 2, carried forward.)
- **Repo publishing floor — LICENSE, root README, `publish=false`, repo metadata (v1-audit-recorded, 2026-07-02).** Fine for internal go-live; owed the moment the repo is shown outward. *Trigger:* first external adopter, public release, or opening the repo.
- **~~Non-singleton migrate-in-place block routes the wrong way (v1-audit-recorded, 2026-07-02).~~ GRADUATED — trigger fired 2026-07-15, settled robust at M43 planning (2026-07-16).** The lacon trial's first same-path adoption cost 4 finalize attempts ([RC-lacon/findings-verification.md](../completions/artifacts/RC-lacon/findings-verification.md) → A4). Settled as **both halves**: the route repair *and* the bound revision — drop the `singleton &&` conjunct in `plan_clobber_guard` so the in-place carve-out covers every doctype when source==destination (spike-proven: the create side needs no change — non-singletons seed blank by construction; `plan_retirements` is already doctype-blind; today the fidelity review gate never renders on this path because the clobber block fires first). `--approve` stays the sole destructive gate — the M23 review-before-destroy rationale is *restored*, not revised away. Builds at M43 ([DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) → 2026-07-16 M43 Settle #5).
- **(D) Managed-docs enforcement hook (`PreToolUse` block on direct edits under docs-root) — DEFERRED 2026-07-02.** Would convert part of the adapter's ergonomics bet (VISION #3, "adapter-enforced, not sandboxed") into deterministic enforcement (`exit 2` route-to-jigc). Deliberately *not* taken at v1: it engages both the recorded ergonomics bet and the cost-of-enforcement evidence (the block→recover loop is the measured Opus cost driver). Shaped in [ideas/managed-doc-enforcement-hook.md](../ideas/managed-doc-enforcement-hook.md). *Trigger:* the RC trials' invocation-log adherence data shows agents bypassing the adapter (drive-arounds) at a rate that costs correctness — decide with that evidence, not before.
- **(D) Org-policy cascade floor (a non-overridable layer above `project`) — noted 2026-07-02.** The cascade's layers are all rebuttable deltas; enterprise governance wants one that isn't (the managed-policy pattern). One more resolution layer, but non-overridability is a new property — don't build speculatively. *Trigger:* a multi-team/enterprise adopter needs enforced org mandates (same driver class as the public-pack surface).
- **Small-batch advisory at finalize — noted 2026-07-02.** A deterministic diff-size/one-concern advisory at the commit boundary (SmartBear ~400-LOC review ceiling; DORA 2025: AI raises volume, stability needs the guardrail more). Advisory-only, jigc-shaped (rides the boundary the CLI owns). *Trigger:* milestone/PR-shaped workflows produce large diffs in practice, or a team asks for the guardrail.
- **Stable `jigc validate --format json` as a CI-gating contract — the *envelope shape* pins at M41 (2026-07-11); the external-CI *declaration* stays noted.** The trigger fired — the rc.4-rerun agent drove jigc from a Python driver (a non-agent orchestrator). M41's Fork 1 (the [command-output-contract.md](../design/command-output-contract.md) full pin) pins the **findings-as-data shape** once, with a stable `(code, target)` key + evolution posture; `validate --format json`'s findings envelope **inherits that shape** rather than inventing its own. What stays open past M41: formally *declaring* `validate --format json` a CI-gating contract for external pipelines (the SLA promise, gate-on-store-findings semantics) — a posture statement, not a new shape. *Trigger (narrowed):* first demand to gate a PR/pipeline on store-scope findings.
- **Pack-choice step (identify-then-apply) — noted 2026-07-02.** Vacuous with one domain pack (packs auto-wire, recorded in project-setup.md). The KB's `types/identify.md` (three-forces questions, evidence-over-names, exit ramp) is a ready-made design sketch for the orient-shape selector workflow when it's earned. *Trigger:* a second domain pack exists.
- **Future domain packs (collective pointer) — noted 2026-07-02.** The KB type modules (wordpress, library-cli, claude-code-tooling) are candidate tenants of the deferred pack platform; recorded collectively, no per-pack files. *Trigger:* an external domain concretely earns a pack **and** the pack-authoring API is stabilized (the non-goal's own release condition).
- **Fresh-clone machine-local half undetected — noted 2026-07-02.** A teammate's clone reads as "set up" (committed `.jigc/config/` present) while the pre-commit hook + doc-code probe are silently absent; nothing routes them to re-run `jigc setup`. Cheapest robust cut: orientation detects and emits `Run: jigc setup`. *Trigger:* multi-developer adoption of a jigc-managed repo (pairs with the per-developer `local` layer entry above).

*(This is the **triage view** of [VISION.md](../VISION.md) → Open questions, not a second copy — VISION stays the full categorized index; this adds each still-open item's trigger. Several VISION opens are already **resolved** and not repeated here: slug-normalization + collision-suffix form (M7), `milestone-execution` orchestration + milestone-worktree (M7), config layout + committed-config-dir (M4), emitted-format micro-syntax (2026-05-28).)*

## Build-time — increment-keyed (historical: M1)

*The original worked example: the decisions M1's increments forced, surfaced before each build. Kept as the pattern; M2–M7 recorded their per-increment decisions directly to [DECISIONS.md](../DECISIONS.md) via the build harness.*

- **(I) Hashing** — `blake3`, one algo for `file-state` + content-drift.
- **(I) Test tooling** — `insta` (snapshot/golden) + `proptest` (property/fuzz).
